# 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Lena Marsh, Documentation Specialist, Process and Governance
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review

**Who this is for:** Anyone who designs, illustrates, lays out, checks or prints a SIDE EYE issue: art directors, designers, image makers, rights checkers, production staff, outside vendors and AI agents.
**Use it when:** You start a new issue, pick up an issue mid-stream, need to know who approves what, or want to change a standard.
**Related:** 00 Start Here: How to Use the SIDE EYE Art Direction Standards; 02 Cover Standards; 03 Page and Spread Standards; 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards); 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits; 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP); 08 Quality Checklists; 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs; 11 Templates; 12 Decision Log; 13 Open Questions for Heather; SIDE EYE Brand Standards hub; SIDE EYE Design System v3: Neon Zine Print; SIDE EYE Design System v2 Core; Design System v2 Addendum A: KDP Production Spec 8.5 x 11; SIDE EYE Research Guidelines Portal: Proposed Plan.

---

## 1. The process on one page

Every issue moves through nine stages, in order. Each stage ends at a gate. A gate passes only with Heather's recorded yes, or, where this document says so, the art director's.

| # | Stage | What it produces | Who passes the gate | Checklist in 08 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Issue Brief | The approved Issue Brief | Heather | Stage 0 Issue Brief |
| 1 | Flatplan | The approved page-by-page flatplan | Heather | Stage 1 Flatplan |
| 2 | Art Direction | One art-direction doc per feature, plus cover and departments | Heather (cover and concepts); art director (revisions inside an approved concept) | Stage 2 Art Direction |
| 3 | Art Production | Final image files, a complete art log and a rights log with every placed image Cleared | Art director (picks each image); Heather (cover, openers, real people) | Stage 3 Art Production |
| 4 | Layout | A full review PDF of interior and cover | Art director (ready for review) | Stage 4 Layout |
| 5 | Review and Legal Read | A signed-off layout, the legal read checklist and a fixed review PDF | Heather | Stage 5 Review and Legal Read |
| 6 | Prepress | Print-ready interior and cover PDFs that pass KDP's previewer | Production (preflight); Heather (upload) | Stage 6 Prepress |
| 7 | Proof Copy | A proof report and a yes to publish | Heather | Stage 7 Proof Copy |
| 8 | Release and Archive | The live book, the release note and the archived issue package | Heather (publish) | Stage 8 Release and Archive |

Three rules hold across every stage:

1. **Heather has the final yes.** Nothing ships, prints, posts or costs money without her sign-off (Brand Standards hub, "The golden rules").
2. **Agents never mark work Approved or Published.** An agent moves work up to a gate and says it is ready. Only Heather marks Approved (Brand Standards hub, "What always needs Heather's yes").
3. **Nothing counts as delivered until it is in Drive,** in the issue folder that doc 09 names, under the name doc 09 sets.

The status of each issue (which stage, who is on it, dates, who approved each gate and when) lives in **the production tracker**. This set of standards says how the work is done. The tracker says where the work stands. Never track status inside a standards doc.

---

## 2. Roles

Use these role names in every brief, flatplan, log and handoff note.

| Role | Does | Can an AI agent fill it? | Can a human vendor fill it? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Publisher / Editor in Chief (Heather) | Sets the issue, approves every gate, spends money, uploads to KDP, publishes | No | No |
| Art Director (AD) | Owns the look of the issue; writes the brief draft, flatplan and art-direction docs; approves individual images; runs internal checks | Yes, with the side-eye-art-direction skill | Yes |
| Designer / Layout | Builds pages and the cover to docs 03, 04 and 07; places art, captions and credits; makes review PDFs | Yes | Yes |
| Illustrator / Image Generator | Runs Midjourney prompts, picks and upscales results, edits and composites images | Writes prompts, reviews results and edits files. A person runs Midjourney itself (see note) | Yes |
| Rights Checker | Opens every source page, reads the license line, fills and signs the rights log | Yes, if it can open the source page itself | Yes |
| Fact-check / Research | Hands over the research package; checks every new fact, number, caption and display line added in design | Yes, as an independent checker in a fresh session | Yes |
| Production | Prepress, KDP preflight, proof checks, final files, archive package | Yes, except anything inside the KDP account | Yes, except anything inside the KDP account |

**Note on Midjourney.** In Vol. 1 and Vol. 2 the prompts were written by agents and run by hand in Midjourney by a person on SIDE EYE's account. Keep it that way: Midjourney is run by hand, never by automation. Who runs it for a given issue is named in the Issue Brief.

**Note on co-editing.** Heather may bring Edwin into any review. His notes go to the AD through Heather's review notes, and Heather's yes is still the one that counts.

**Conflicts of interest.** Nobody checks their own work. The person who sourced an image does not sign its rights row when someone else is available. The fact-checker on design text did not write that text. The legal read is run by someone who did not write the piece (Research Guidelines Portal plan, DEC-2026-005).

---

## 3. RACI by stage

R = does the work. A = gives the final yes (one per row). C = asked before. I = told after.

| Stage or task | Heather | Art Director | Designer / Layout | Illustrator / Image Gen | Rights Checker | Fact-check / Research | Production |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 Issue Brief | A | R | C | I | I | C | C |
| 1 Flatplan | A | R | R | I | I | C | C |
| 2 Art-direction docs (features, departments) | A | R | C | C | C | C | I |
| 2 Cover concept | A | R | C | C | C | C | I |
| 2 Independent review of cover and living-person openers | I | A | I | I | C | R | I |
| 3 Generate and edit images | I | A | C | R | I | I | C |
| 3 Source historical images and fill the rights log | I | A | I | C | R | C | I |
| 3 Approve cover, openers and images of real people | A | R | C | R | R | C | C |
| 4 Layout build and review PDF | I | A | R | C | C | C | C |
| 4 Fact-check of display text added in layout | I | C | C | I | I | R, A | I |
| 5 Full layout read and sign-off | A | R | R | I | C | C | C |
| 5 Legal read checklist | A | C | I | I | C | R | I |
| 6 Prepress files and KDP previewer pass | I | C | C | I | I | I | R, A |
| 6 Upload to KDP (draft) | A, R | I | I | I | I | I | C |
| 7 Order a printed proof (spends money) | A, R | C | I | I | I | I | C |
| 7 Proof check and proof report | A | R | C | I | I | I | R |
| 8 Publish | A, R | I | I | I | I | I | C |
| 8 Archive package and release note | I | C | C | C | C | C | R, A |

Where Heather is "A" on a stage, the AD prepares the gate request (a Handoff Note, doc 11) and Heather answers it. Her answer is recorded in the production tracker with her name and the date.

---

## 4. How editorial hands off to design

Design does not invent or check facts. It works from what editorial hands over. The handoff happens per feature (so art direction can start early) and then for the whole issue (so layout can start).

### Per feature, before Stage 2 starts on it

- **Text locked.** The feature's text is approved by Heather. Status LOCKED in the file name or header.
- **Scorecard with the Must NOT claim list.** Every rated claim, plus the list of things the piece must never say or imply. The Must NOT list binds the art as much as the text: art never implies a crime, deal or motive that the Scorecard rates below Proven (side-eye-art-direction skill).
- **Research package accepted.** The package from the research process (Fact Ledger, sources doc, rulings, Living Person Register where living people appear), accepted by Heather at the research hand-off gate (R8 in the Research Guidelines Portal plan).
- **Register and tab.** LOUD or QUIET, and which of the issue's three tabs the piece files under.
- **Word count** for the text and each sidebar.

### For the whole issue, before Stage 4 starts

- The full manuscript is LOCKED, including the opening letter, departments, captions written by editorial, cover lines and the issue thesis line.
- The fact-check log is closed: zero open items, or each one signed by Heather.
- The back-of-issue sources section text exists and matches the fact-check log one to one (Brand Standards hub, "What's in every issue").
- The headline pass is done.

### What design does when a handoff item is missing

Do not start the stage for that piece. Log it in the production tracker as blocked, name the missing item, and work on another piece. Never fill a gap with your own facts, quotes, numbers or dates.

### New facts found during design

Any fact, number, date or quote that design adds (an infographic figure, a caption, a pull quote, a map pin, a cover line) goes back to Fact-check / Research as a change request. It gets a ledger row, a rating and a check before it prints (Research Guidelines Portal plan, section 7.1). Packaging is rated too: cover lines and headlines follow the same scale as the body.

---

## 5. The stages in detail

### Stage 0: Issue Brief

**Purpose.** Fix what the issue is, what it costs and when it ships, before anyone designs a page.

**Inputs (must exist before you start).**
- The issue's plan in the content calendar (theme, thesis, three tabs, lineup).
- A lineup with a status and rough word count for every piece.
- A proposed launch (on-sale) date from Heather.
- The issue's research tier list (which features run the full research process).

**Steps.**
1. Copy the Issue Brief template (doc 11) into the issue's plan folder.
2. Fill theme, thesis line, audience, volume number and launch date from the calendar and Heather's notes. Use the newest Heather decision where the calendar and the manuscript disagree, and say which you used.
3. Set the page count target. It must be even. Plan 116 to 132 pages (Design System v2 Addendum A). Over 150 pages changes the gutter and the grid, so flag it.
4. List the features with tab, register and word count. Confirm the issue rules: at least one QUIET feature; at least two stories centered on women and two from outside Europe (content calendar, "Rules every volume follows").
5. Copy in the issue's Must NOT claim list: the combined Must NOT lines from every feature's Scorecard, plus issue-level flags from the calendar.
6. Write the hero idea: one surreal cover idea, and the cover side. The hero alternates sides by volume (Vol. 1 right, Vol. 2 left; Brand Standards hub, "What's in every issue"). Doc 02 says which side each volume takes.
7. List every item KDP cannot print and how it will be redrawn: no gatefolds, inserts, die cuts, perforations or spot inks (Addendum A, sections 1 and 5).
8. Fill the budget lines: proof copies (number and who orders them), and confirm zero paid image licenses.
9. Name who fills each role, and who runs Midjourney.
10. Send the brief to Heather with a Handoff Note.

**Outputs.** The approved Issue Brief, filed in the issue's 01 Plan folder (doc 09 sets the name).

**Who.** AD drafts. Fact-check / Research supplies the Must NOT list. Heather approves.

**Gate.** Heather approves the thesis, page count target, launch date, cover side, proof budget and role list. The AD cannot pass this gate.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 0 Issue Brief.

### Stage 1: Flatplan

**Purpose.** Decide what goes on every page and spread before any art is made.

**Inputs.**
- The approved Issue Brief.
- The lineup with word counts.
- The standard running order (Up Front, The Files, Out Back) from the content calendar's standard issue template.
- The page recipes in Design System v3 and doc 03.

**Steps.**
1. Lay out the running order spread by spread. Keep the standard order so readers know where things live.
2. For every page, set: section, recipe, register (LOUD or QUIET), tab, print process (RELIEF, COLLAGE, WOOD TYPE, HYBRID or NEWSPRINT), owner and status. Use the Flatplan template (doc 11).
3. Copyfit. LOUD body runs 3 columns, QUIET body 2 columns (Addendum A, section 2). As a starting estimate, a LOUD feature with art carries about 450 words a page (the Napoleon feature needed 20 pages for about 9,050 words). Check the estimate against a test page before you lock.
4. Start features on a left-hand page when the opener is a spread, so each pair is a true spread.
5. Apply the binding rules: the first and last spreads do not open flat, so no art there depends on it. Any cut-out page goes on a right-hand page with a sacrificial back (Addendum A, sections 5 and 6).
6. Mark first cuts: the pieces you drop if the count runs over.
7. Check the total is even and matches the brief. If it doesn't, file a Change Request (section 7).

**Outputs.** The approved flatplan (a Google Sheet), filed in 01 Plan (doc 09 sets the name).

**Who.** AD with Designer / Layout. Heather approves.

**Gate.** Heather approves the page count and the running order. After that, the AD may move pieces between spreads, swap recipes or rebalance pages as long as the page count, the running order and the cover stay the same. Log each move in the flatplan's change notes.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 1 Flatplan.

### Stage 2: Art Direction

**Purpose.** Decide the visual idea for every page and write the instructions that make it, before any image is generated or bought.

**Inputs.**
- The approved flatplan.
- For each feature: its LOCKED text and its Scorecard with the Must NOT list (section 4).
- Design System v3: Neon Zine Print, the v2 grid rules, and the model deliverable: the Napoleon Art Direction doc.
- Docs 02, 03, 05 and 06 of this set.

**Steps.**
1. Write one art-direction doc per feature, and one each for the cover, Up Front and Out Back. Use the Art-Direction Doc template (doc 11), which follows the Napoleon model.
2. State the concept in a sentence tied to the piece's thesis. Name the joke and the one thing pink marks (one pink focus per page in print).
3. Pick one process per image. HYBRID only for real historical people. QUIET pages get no neon, no collage and no jokes.
4. Write two prompts per generated image: A the safer read, B the bolder joke (doc 05). No artist, studio, shop or magazine names. No generated lettering: describe paper and signs as "completely blank".
5. List each historical image the piece needs with its proposed source and rights basis. Paid licenses are not allowed (Brand Standards hub, "Image rights"). Mark each as a candidate for the Rights Checker.
6. List everything built by hand in layout: wood-type plates, cover lines, charts, maps, timelines, ornaments.
7. Check every image idea against the Must NOT list. Art never implies a crime, deal or motive the Scorecard can't prove. Living people follow the sourced-public-facts-only rule. Generated likenesses of historical figures are allowed.
8. For the cover and for any opener about a living person, have an independent reviewer (someone who did not write the doc) check the plan against doc 05's checklist and the Scorecard.
9. Send the set to Heather with a Handoff Note.

**Outputs.** One art-direction doc per feature, plus cover, Up Front and Out Back docs, filed in 04 Art > 01 Briefs. Each starts its rows in the issue's art log and rights log (doc 11; doc 05 section 12 and doc 06 section 5 set the columns).

**Who.** AD (agent with the side-eye-art-direction skill, or a human AD). Illustrator and Rights Checker consulted on what is feasible. Heather approves.

**Gate.** Heather approves the cover concept and each feature's concept. After that, the AD may revise prompts, swap an image for another with the same process and rights status, or change a crop without asking, as long as the concept, the joke and what pink marks stay the same.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 2 Art Direction.

### Stage 3: Art Production

**Purpose.** Make, source, clear and finish every image the flatplan needs.

**Inputs.**
- Approved art-direction docs.
- The art log and rights log started in Stage 2.
- The two style references named in Design System v3 (the powdered-wig skull print and the approved emu cover).

**Steps.**
1. Run the prompts in Midjourney by hand. Record each run in the art log: prompt as run, A or B, generator and version, job ID and grid position, date and file name (doc 05, section 12).
2. Generate the hardest or most important image first to lock the look (the Napoleon doc locked the opener first, then ran the cast set in one sitting).
3. Pick variants against the art-direction doc. Reject anything with stray lettering, stray faces on hands-only scenes, glow, chrome or gradients (doc 05 has the fixes).
4. Upscale and edit to final size: 300 dpi at placed size; a full-bleed page needs 2,588 × 3,375 px; nothing under 200 dpi is placed at any size (Addendum A, section 8).
5. Source historical images. The Rights Checker opens every source page, reads the license line, saves the rights statement as evidence, and fills the rights log row. Only a Cleared row can go to print; Pending can sit in a layout comp; Blocked is never placed (doc 06, section 5).
6. Composite HYBRID figures and cut-outs. Never recolor period art pink or neon. Never put an illustration inside a period document, and never make generated art look like a period print or record.
7. Convert finished images to CMYK for print. Keep total ink under 240% (Brand Standards hub, "Print spec").
8. Name and file each image under its image ID (doc 09).

**Outputs.** Final image files in 04 Art > 09 Finals, named per doc 09; the art log complete; the rights log with every placed image Cleared and every credit line written.

**Who.** Illustrator / Image Generator and Rights Checker do the work. The AD picks. Heather approves the cover, every opener and every image of a real person.

**Gate.** The AD picks each image against its art-direction doc and the Stage 3 checklist (art log status Picked). Heather approves the cover, every opener, every image of a real person and anything flagged in an art-direction doc's open items; she may approve the rest in one pass on the art-direction doc (doc 05, section 12.4). Only Heather, or the person she names, fills the art log's "Approved by and date" column. An image without a Cleared rights row may sit in layout as a marked placeholder, but it cannot reach Stage 6.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 3 Art Production.

### Stage 4: Layout

**Purpose.** Build every page and the cover to spec, with locked text and finished art.

**Inputs.**
- The LOCKED manuscript and closed fact-check log (section 4).
- The approved flatplan.
- Finished art, or marked placeholders.
- Fonts: the free stand-ins named in doc 04 until the paid faces have a recorded license.

**Steps.**
1. Build on the 12-column grid and 13 pt baseline, at the type sizes in doc 04 and the page recipes in doc 03.
2. Pick one of the three layout routes in doc 09, section 4: a packaged layout file (InDesign or Affinity Publisher), an HTML build (the Vol. 1 route: HTML and CSS printed to PDF), or a page spec for agents who can't run layout software. Any route is fine if the output meets doc 07 and the job can be rebuilt from the package.
3. Place text exactly as locked. Never cut or rewrite text to make it fit. If it doesn't fit, file a Change Request to editorial with the overage in lines.
4. Set captions and credit lines from the art-direction docs and the rights log. Build the colophon credits (one Midjourney credit, image credits, influence credits).
5. Send every display line added in layout (headlines if changed, pull quotes, chart numbers, map pins, cover lines) to Fact-check / Research for a check.
6. Export a review PDF of the interior and one of the cover. Name them as builds (doc 09), so old builds never get mistaken for the latest.
7. Run the Stage 4 checklist and fix everything it finds before Heather sees the PDF.

**Outputs.** Interior and cover review PDFs in 05 Layout > Exports, build-numbered per doc 09, plus the layout source files.

**Who.** Designer / Layout builds. AD reviews. Fact-check / Research checks display text.

**Gate.** The AD marks the build ready for review. This is an internal gate; the next gate is Heather's.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 4 Layout.

### Stage 5: Review and Legal Read

**Purpose.** Get Heather's yes on the whole issue as it will print, with the legal check run last. Understanding comes first; the legal check comes last (Brand Standards hub, "How a feature gets made").

**Inputs.**
- The ready-for-review interior and cover PDFs.
- The issue's Must NOT list, Living Person Register and rights log.
- The fact-check results on display text.

**Steps.**
1. Heather reads the full layout and returns notes. Plan two working days for this read.
2. The AD sorts notes into fixes (no approval needed beyond Heather's note) and Change Requests (anything that changes page count, the cover, locked text or the running order).
3. Run any sensitivity or expert reads the brief calls for.
4. Run the issue-level rights check (doc 06, section 14): every placed image Cleared, every credit line in place, the credit list matching the rights log one to one, and no Pending or Blocked row placed. This is the "rights check before print" that confirms each Cleared row.
5. Apply fixes. Export the near-final build.
6. Run the legal read last, on the near-final build. The legal read is a checklist run by someone who did not write the piece. Any line or image that frames a living person as doing wrong is softened to court and filing language, or cut (Research Guidelines Portal plan, DEC-2026-005). Check the Must NOT list line by line against text and art.
7. Apply any legal fixes. Export the next build. Heather confirms the fixes.

**Outputs.** Heather's review notes; the signed legal read checklist; the signed rights check; the fixed review PDFs. File them in 06 Reviews and Legal.

**Who.** Heather reads. AD and Designer fix. Fact-check / Research runs the legal checklist. The Rights Checker runs the rights check.

**Gate.** Heather signs off text and layout for prepress. After this, only fixes found in Stage 6 or 7 are allowed, each by Change Request.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 5 Review and Legal Read.

### Stage 6: Prepress

**Purpose.** Turn the approved layout into files KDP will print without surprises.

**Inputs.**
- The Stage 5 approved build.
- The final page count, and KDP's cover template downloaded for that count.

**Steps.** (Doc 07 holds the full spec. The short version, from Addendum A and the hub:)
1. Export the interior as one PDF at 8.625 × 11.25 in (trim plus bleed), single pages in reading order, fonts embedded, no crop or registration marks, transparency flattened, all images CMYK.
2. Check bleed (0.125 in on outside edges, none on the gutter), interior text safety (0.375 in inside trim, KDP's minimum; doc 07) and the 7 pt type minimum. Cover safety is set in doc 02.
3. Check ink: total 240% or less (doc 07). Use the rich black the hub names (see doc 07).
4. Build the cover on KDP's template for the final page count. Spine width is page count × 0.002347 in.
5. Upload both files to the KDP draft. Only Heather, or someone she has given access, does this step.
6. Run KDP's Print Previewer and fix every flagged page.
7. Fill the preflight report and attach the previewer result.

**Outputs.** Final interior PDF and cover PDF in 07 KDP and Print (doc 09 names them; only the files actually uploaded carry `-final`), and the preflight report.

**Who.** Production does the work and signs the preflight. Heather uploads.

**Gate.** Production signs that the files pass the Stage 6 checklist and KDP's previewer. Heather's upload to the KDP draft is her yes to move on.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 6 Prepress.

### Stage 7: Proof Copy

**Purpose.** See the issue as a reader will hold it, before anyone can buy it.

**Inputs.**
- Files that passed KDP's previewer in the KDP draft.
- The Issue Brief's proof line (how many proofs, or waived).

**Steps.**
1. Heather orders the printed proof from the KDP draft. Proofs carry a "Not for Resale" watermark (KDP Help, proof copies). KDP shows the delivery estimate at checkout, and says manufacturing time changes with order volume (KDP Help, "When will my proof or author copy arrive?"). Plan 10 business days per proof round.
2. Check the proof against the Stage 7 checklist: neon builds and pink, the pale cyan, show-through on dark spreads, gutter loss, bleed, spine, 7 pt type, image sharpness.
3. Write the proof report: page, problem, fix. Fixes go back through Stage 6. A fix that changes art or text needs a Change Request.
4. If a second proof is needed, Heather decides and orders it.

**If the proof is waived.** Heather may waive the printed proof for an issue in its brief (she did for Vol. 2). Then the KDP previewer and the Stage 5 full read are the last checks, and the proof report records "waived" with the date of her decision. The Brand Standards hub says to order a proof copy for any neon build. Until Heather sets the series default, follow the hub unless the Issue Brief records her waiver (open question in 13 Open Questions for Heather).

**Outputs.** `SE-V## Proof Report`, and Heather's yes to publish.

**Who.** Heather orders (it spends money). AD and Production check.

**Gate.** Heather approves the proof, or approves publishing without one.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 7 Proof Copy.

### Stage 8: Release and Archive

**Purpose.** Publish, confirm the live book is right, and file everything so the next issue (or a corrected edition) can be rebuilt.

**Inputs.**
- Heather's yes from Stage 7.
- All final files, logs and docs for the issue.

**Steps.**
1. Heather publishes in KDP. KDP says it takes about 3 business days for a book to go live, with the Amazon.com detail page and in-stock status taking up to 72 hours (KDP Help, publishing timeline). KDP paperbacks can't take pre-orders, so the book must be live on launch day.
2. Once the book is live, check the live listing page and the look-inside preview against the final files.
3. Build the archive package in the issue's 08 Package folder (doc 09): layout source files, every placed image, the font list with license status, all art-direction docs, the art log with Midjourney job IDs, the rights log and its saved rights evidence, the final interior and cover PDFs, and a text-searchable PDF or text export of the final pages.
4. Move every older build and draft to the archive folder, marked SUPERSEDED.
5. Write the Release Note (doc 11).
6. Hold a short look-back. File what went wrong as Standards Change Requests (section 9), and add open calls to 13 Open Questions for Heather.
7. After release, corrections follow the house rule: printed in the next issue, in the same type size as the original error (Brand Standards hub), plus the public corrections process in the research standards.

**Outputs.** Live book; `SE-V## Release Note`; the archived issue package.

**Who.** Heather publishes. Production builds the archive and writes the release note.

**Gate.** Heather's publish click is the gate. Production marks the archive complete in the production tracker.

**Checklist.** 08 Quality Checklists, Stage 8 Release and Archive.

---

## 6. Timeline template

Work back from the launch date (L). Count business days. Skip holidays. KDP paperbacks can't take pre-orders, so L is the day the book must be live.

### With one printed proof

| Business days before L | Milestone | Stage | Who |
|---|---|---|---|
| L | On sale | 8 | Heather |
| L − 4 | Publish in KDP (about 3 business days to go live, plus 1 day spare) | 8 | Heather |
| L − 6 | Proof fixes re-uploaded; previewer passes again | 7, 6 | Production, Heather |
| L − 8 | Proof arrives and is checked; proof report done | 7 | AD, Production |
| L − 18 | Proof ordered (plan 10 business days for print and delivery) | 7 | Heather |
| L − 21 | Files uploaded to the KDP draft; previewer passes (allow up to 3 days before KDP lets you order a proof; doc 07) | 6 | Production, Heather |
| L − 23 | Prepress starts | 6 | Production |
| L − 24 | Heather signs off layout after fixes | 5 | Heather |
| L − 28 | Review PDF to Heather (2 days to read; then rights check, fixes and the legal read last, 2 days) | 5 | Heather, Rights Checker, Fact-check |
| L − 34 | Layout starts (about 6 business days for a 116 to 132 page issue) | 4 | Designer |
| L − 36 | Manuscript LOCKED and fact-check log closed for the whole issue | Editorial handoff | Editorial |
| L − 42 | Art production starts (runs about 10 business days, overlapping layout) | 3 | Illustrator, Rights Checker |
| L − 47 | Art-direction docs to Heather (cover first) | 2 | AD |
| L − 50 | Flatplan approved | 1 | Heather |
| L − 52 | Issue Brief approved | 0 | Heather |

### Without a printed proof (proof waived in the brief)

Drop the proof rows. Upload at L − 5 and publish at L − 4; every earlier date moves 16 business days later. Heather chose no printed proof for Vol. 2.

### Second proof round

Add 13 business days (order, about 10 days to arrive, check and fix). Decide on a second proof at the first proof report, never later.

### How the numbers were set

- KDP go-live times come from KDP Help (publishing timeline). The proof delivery estimate comes at KDP checkout, so 10 business days is a planning number, based on the "1 to 2 weeks" the Vol. 2 plans assumed. Replace it with the checkout estimate as soon as the proof is ordered.
- Layout, review and art spans come from the Vol. 2 schedule (layout about 6 business days for about 124 pages; a 2-day full-layout read; art about 10 days alongside layout). They are planning numbers. Change them in the Issue Brief if the issue is bigger, smaller or has a new vendor.

---

## 7. How changes are requested and recorded

### When you need a Change Request

Use the Change Request template (doc 11) for any change after a gate has passed that:

- changes the page count, the running order or a piece's page budget;
- changes locked text (editorial owns the fix; design never edits locked text);
- changes the cover, a cover line or an approved concept;
- adds a fact, number, quote or date (it also goes to Fact-check / Research);
- swaps an image for one with a different process or rights status;
- moves the launch date or a gate date;
- spends money or adds a paid service;
- needs a color, font, logo use or format not in the Brand Standards hub.

Small fixes inside an approved item (a typo in a caption, a crop, a widow, a kerning fix, a reflow within the same pages) need no Change Request. Note them in the build's change notes.

### The steps

1. Anyone can raise a Change Request. Give it the next number for the issue (`CR-001`, `CR-002` and so on).
2. The AD triages it within one working day: what it touches, what it costs in days, which stages must run again.
3. Decide it at the right level:
   - **AD decides:** anything inside an approved concept that keeps page count, running order, cover and text the same.
   - **Editorial decides, Heather approves:** any change to locked text.
   - **Heather decides:** everything else on the list above.
4. Record the decision in the issue's Change Log (the CR list in the issue's plan folder) and in the production tracker.
5. Re-run the checklist for every stage the change touched.

### Rules

- Never apply a change before it is decided, even a "quick" one.
- A change that touches a fact always goes through Fact-check / Research.
- If Heather's answer is not in writing, it is not decided.

---

## 8. What always needs Heather's yes

From the Brand Standards hub ("What always needs Heather's yes"):

- Anything published: an upload, a post, a listing, a print order.
- Any gate in the production pipeline.
- Any new logo use, color, font or format not in the Brand Standards hub.
- Spending money or signing anything for SIDE EYE.
- Sharing unpublished work outside the team.

AI agents never mark work Approved or Published, and never send, post or buy anything on their own.

For issue design, that means Heather's yes is needed for:

| Item | Stage |
|---|---|
| The Issue Brief: thesis, page count, launch date, cover side, proof budget, roles | 0 |
| The flatplan's page count and running order | 1 |
| The cover concept and each feature's concept | 2 |
| The cover art, every opener and every image of a real person (the rest may be approved in one pass) | 3 |
| Any paid image, font or service (paid image licenses are not allowed at all) | Any |
| Any change to locked text | Any |
| The full layout sign-off | 5 |
| Upload to KDP | 6 |
| Ordering a proof, or waiving it | 7 |
| Publishing | 8 |
| Any change to these standards | Any |
| Sharing unpublished pages with anyone outside the team, vendors included, beyond the folders they were given | Any |

What the AD may decide without Heather: picking among generated variants that fit an approved concept; swapping images of the same process and rights status; crops, reflow and type fixes inside approved pages; moving pieces within the approved page count and running order; marking a build ready for review; picking images (art log status Picked).

---

## 9. How these standards change

### Who owns what

- **Heather** approves every change to this set. Nothing changes without her written yes.
- **The process owner** (the Documentation Specialist for Process and Governance) keeps the set, batches change requests, and keeps 12 Decision Log and 13 Open Questions for Heather up to date.
- **Each doc's owner** drafts changes to their doc.

### The steps

1. Anyone may file a Standards Change Request (doc 11, Change Request template, type "Standards"). Say which doc, which rule, what went wrong, and the proposed wording.
2. The process owner checks it against the Brand Standards hub and 12 Decision Log. If it would contradict the hub, it goes to Heather as a hub question, never as a change to this set alone.
3. Heather approves, edits or declines.
4. On a yes, the doc owner edits the Markdown master, bumps the version, and adds a line to 12 Decision Log: date, decision, what it replaces, who approved.
5. The Drive copies and the web portal are rebuilt from the same Markdown. Every doc and page shows the same version and date.
6. The old version is kept for history, never deleted.

### Version numbers

Follow the hub's pattern (Brand Standards hub, "Approvals and versions"):

- **Whole number (1.0 to 2.0):** a rule changed. Anyone working to the old rule must be told.
- **Decimal (1.0 to 1.1):** wording, a fix, an example, or a new template that changes no rule.
- A draft is 0.x or carries "draft" in its status line until Heather approves it.

### Naming in Drive

Use the names already in use in Drive:

- The live version carries `(CURRENT)` at the end of its title, for example `01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files (CURRENT)`.
- A retired version is renamed `SUPERSEDED – <old title> (use v<N>)`, the pattern in doc 09, and moves to the set's archive folder the same day (doc 09 names the folder). Never leave it beside the live file. Never trash it.
- Title and first line always match.

### Issues already in progress

An issue past Stage 2 when a standard changes may finish on the old version, unless Heather says otherwise in the change. Note which version the issue used in its Release Note.

### Which rule wins

1. Heather's newest recorded decision.
2. The Brand Standards hub, for anything about brand: logo, color, type, voice, rights and the rules in its Decision log. If this set disagrees with the hub, the hub wins, and the set gets fixed.
3. This set, for how an issue is designed and produced.
4. The source design docs this set is built on: Design System v3: Neon Zine Print, then Design System v2 Core and Addendum A where v3 does not replace them.
5. Older docs and skills. Where a skill disagrees with the hub, the hub wins (Brand Standards hub, "Rules inside skills that may be out of date").

When two sources disagree and neither the hub's Decision log nor 12 Decision Log settles it, do not decide. Follow the hub until Heather decides, and add the question to 13 Open Questions for Heather.

---

## 10. What Vol. 1 taught us (and the rule each lesson made)

These come from how Vol. 1, The Loser's Issue, actually went. Each one is now a rule in this process.

| What happened in Vol. 1 | The rule now |
|---|---|
| The fact-check errata (64 items) were logged a week after the print file was final | The fact-check log closes before layout starts (section 4), and display text added in layout is checked in Stage 4 |
| The page count grew from 96 to 102 to 111 pages, and the contents order and titles changed late | Heather approves the page count at Stage 1. Changes after that go through a Change Request |
| Pieces were pulled in from other working threads during layout | Every piece is in Drive and on the flatplan before Stage 4 starts |
| The final print PDF had no text layer, and it was too big to read back from Drive, so later work could not quote the printed text | The archive keeps a text-searchable PDF or a text export of the final pages (Stage 8) |
| There was no layout package a designer could reopen; the issue was built as HTML and CSS printed to PDF | Any tool is fine, but the archive package must let someone rebuild the issue: source, images, fonts, art direction notes and approved Midjourney links |
| The Napoleon art plan listed paid museum licenses | No paid licenses. Every image has a Cleared row in the rights log before Stage 6 |
| The content calendar planned gatefolds, perforations and inserts that KDP can't print | The Issue Brief lists every item KDP can't print and its printed replacement (Stage 0) |
| Several review builds sat side by side with similar names | Builds are numbered, older builds move to the archive folder, and only the newest build is reviewed |

---

## 11. How this set fits with everything else

- **Brand Standards hub.** The hub wins on brand. This set governs issue design and production. The hub's sections that issue designers use most are "Visual identity", "The magazine", "For vendors, editors and AI agents" and "Decision log".
- **Research standards.** The research process (in the Research Guidelines Portal plan) ends with a package handed to design. This set starts there (section 4). Research rules on facts and ratings always apply to text added in design.
- **The production tracker.** Holds status: stage, owner, dates, blockers, and who approved each gate and when. This set never holds status, and the tracker never holds standards.
- **Skills.** The side-eye-art-direction skill does Stage 2 for agents. Where a skill is out of date, the hub's list of out-of-date skill rules tells you which rule wins.

---

## Sources this is built on

- SIDE EYE Brand Standards hub (sections: Start here, The golden rules, How this guide changes, The magazine, How a feature gets made, Image rights, For vendors, editors and AI agents, What always needs Heather's yes, How the production app works, Approvals and versions, Decision log)
- SIDE EYE Design System v3: Neon Zine Print
- SIDE EYE Design System v2 Core
- SIDE EYE Design System v2 Addendum A: KDP Production Spec 8.5 x 11
- SIDE EYE Content Calendar, Vols. 2 to 12
- Napoleon Art Direction (model art-direction deliverable)
- side-eye-art-direction skill
- SIDE EYE Research Guidelines Portal: Proposed Plan (v0.2, Nov 24, 2026)
- Issue planning records for Vol. 2 and Vol. 3 (Nov 24, 2026)
- Vol. 1 production history (layout builds, print files and errata, Sept to Oct 2026)
- KDP Help: publishing timeline; proof copies; "When will my proof or author copy arrive?"


---

# 02 Cover Standards
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Marisol Quint, Art Director, Covers and Packaging
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review
**Who this is for:** Any designer, vendor or AI agent who makes a SIDE EYE front cover, spine or back cover, or the full-wrap cover PDF that goes to Amazon KDP.
**Use it when:** You start a cover concept, generate cover art, set cover type, build the KDP cover file, or check a cover before Heather sees it.
**Related:** 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files; 03 Page and Spread Standards; 04 Grid and Typography; 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards); 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits; 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP); 08 Quality Checklists; 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs; 12 Decision Log; 13 Open Questions for Heather. Sources: Design System v3: Neon Zine Print; the Brand Standards hub (sections The magazine, Logo, Color, Type); Design System v2 Core; v2 Addendum A, KDP Production Spec 8.5 x 11; Brand Identity: Name, Tagline and Pitch; Front and Back Matter, Vols 1-3 (The Money Pages).

---

## 1. The short version

A SIDE EYE cover is one surreal picture that argues the issue's thesis. It is built as a COLLAGE in six layers. A photoreal cut-out hero with oversized eyes leans in from one side and breaks the frame. The logo sits in a flat color field at the top with room around it. Four cover lines at most, set in typewriter mono on torn paper strips. The issue title runs down one edge, and the thesis line sits with it.

If you remember five rules:

1. One surreal idea per cover, and it must say the issue's thesis.
2. Never put any lettering in generated art. Every word is set in layout, and the logo is a placed file.
3. Four cover lines, maximum. Every line is true on the issue's fact-check log.
4. The hero comes in from the right on odd volumes and from the left on even volumes.
5. Build the wrap on KDP's own template for the final page count. Never guess the spine.

Heather has the final yes on every cover. No agent or vendor marks a cover approved.

---

## 2. Where covers sit in the issue process

The cover is made late on purpose. The thesis and the cover lines must be locked first, because the picture has to argue them.

| Step | What must be true before you start | Who signs off |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Thesis locked | The issue thesis is final in the Issue Hub | Heather |
| Cover lines picked | The headline pass is done and Heather picked four lines or fewer | Heather |
| Concept | One-sentence idea, hero, hero side and joke written down | Heather picks A or B |
| Art | A and B prompts generated, best result cut out and composited | Art director |
| Type and logo | Masthead file placed, type set, nothing generated | Art director |
| Wrap | Interior page count final, KDP template downloaded | Production (see 07) |
| Preflight | KDP previewer clean, cover checklist passed | Production, then Heather |

The full order of work is in 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files. The detailed approval steps for covers are in section 12 below.

---

## 3. The cover concept rule

**Rule 3.1. One surreal idea.** Write the idea in one sentence before you make anything. If you need "and" to describe it, it is two ideas. Cut one.

**Rule 3.2. The picture argues the thesis.** A reader who sees only the picture should get the issue's point. Write one line that ties the picture to the thesis. Example from Vol. 1 (The Losers Issue, thesis "History belongs to whoever keeps the files"): a second-place podium in an empty wheat field, an old army truck, and an emu peeking in from the right. The joke: the emu won, and nobody gave it the podium.

**Rule 3.3. The joke must be true.** The cover is held to the same proof standard as the articles. It must not imply a crime, deal or motive that the issue's Scorecard rates below Proven. The Scorecard's "Must NOT claim" list binds the cover art too.

**Rule 3.4. Everyday things out of place.** The surreal idea comes from ordinary objects in the wrong place (a podium in a wheat field, an envelope over a swimming pool). Avoid fantasy scenes, space, glowing magic or anything that looks rendered on a screen.

**Rule 3.5. Write two concepts.** Concept A is the safer read. Concept B is the bolder joke. Heather picks one, or asks for another round.

**Rule 3.6. Who the hero can be.** The hero is an animal or an object with oversized eyes (the emu, the peacock). A historical figure may be the hero as a clear caricature, because generated likenesses of historical figures are allowed. Do not make a living person the cover hero. Living people follow the sourced-public-facts-only rule, and the house rule is never to joke about anyone's looks. (See 13 Open Questions for Heather if a future issue needs a living person on the cover.)

---

## 4. The COLLAGE build: six layers

Every cover is a COLLAGE (Design System v3: Neon Zine Print). Build it in these six layers, bottom to top. Keep each layer on its own layer or group in the layout file, so any part can be changed without regenerating the rest.

| Layer | What goes in it | How it is made | Vol. 1 example |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| 1. Ground | Cream or kraft paper, scraps of old print at the edges | Midjourney background or scanned paper | Aged paper, newsprint scraps at the bottom edge |
| 2. Scene | One photoreal scene colored in flat silkscreen fields | Midjourney background | Outback wheat field, flat blue sky, vintage truck, second-place podium |
| 3. Color fields | Flat neon blocks with hard edges; the top block holds the masthead | Midjourney blocks out the shape; you lay a flat vector fill in the house CMYK build over it | Flat pink block across the top third |
| 4. Marks | A loose painted neon brush border, a thin wobbly black ink frame, scanned handwritten margin notes | Midjourney for the border and frame; real scanned handwriting for notes | Pink brush border and black ink frame round the cover; handwriting down the left edge |
| 5. Hero | A photoreal cut-out subject with exaggerated, oversized eyes, breaking the frame from one side | Midjourney hero on a white ground, cut out in Photoshop | The emu, peeking in from the right, eyes swiveled at the reader |
| 6. Cover lines | Short lines set in type on torn paper strips | Set in layout, printed or photographed on torn paper, pasted on | "Australia vs. 20,000 Emus" on a torn strip in typewriter mono (approved emu draft) |

Rules for the layers:

- **Rule 4.1.** Never mix RELIEF and COLLAGE in the cover frame. The HYBRID recipe is for feature openers, not covers.
- **Rule 4.2.** Covers may use more than black plus two inks. Use the house inks only (Toner Black, Side Eye Pink, Pale Cyan, Neon Orange, Acid Yellow, Tab Red, Tab Blue, Deep Navy, Peach for period skin, Warm Cream, Kraft). Builds are in the Brand Standards hub, section Color.
- **Rule 4.3.** Set every flat color field as a vector fill in its CMYK build (for example Side Eye Pink C0 M72 Y10 K0). Never rely on the pink that Midjourney painted. On Vol. 1 the masthead field came from the generated art and printed salmon, not Side Eye Pink.
- **Rule 4.4.** Keep total ink under 240% everywhere, including under the hero and in dark fields. Rich black for big fields is C50 M40 Y40 K100 (230%).
- **Rule 4.5.** Paper and texture go in the art around the logo, never on the logo.
- **Rule 4.6.** Beyond the pink masthead field and pink brush border that the v3 template uses, give pink to one thing only: the thing to see first. See the open question on pink in section 14.

---

## 5. Hero rules

**Rule 5.1. Breaks the frame.** The hero crosses the black ink frame and the brush border and runs off the trim on its side. On Vol. 1 the emu crosses the frame, overlaps the masthead field, and runs off the right edge and the bottom.

**Rule 5.2. Oversized eyes, looking at the reader.** The eyes are exaggerated and round, and they swivel toward the reader in a side-eye. The eyes are the first thing a shopper sees after the logo.

**Rule 5.3. Side alternates by volume.** Odd volumes: the hero leans in from the right. Even volumes: from the left. The covers then face each other on a shelf.

| Volume | Issue (as planned November 24, 2026) | Hero enters from |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Vol. 1 | The Losers Issue | Right |
| Vol. 2 | The Money Is Fake Issue | Left |
| Vol. 3 | The Mating Issue | Right |
| Vol. 4 | The Poison Issue | Left |

The issues were renumbered on November 24, 2026 (Money Is Fake moved ahead of Mating). Always go by the volume number in the Issue Hub, never by an older plan. Design System v2 shows a peacock entering from the left as "Vol. 2"; that peacock now belongs to Vol. 3, so it enters from the right.

**Rule 5.4. Keep the face safe.** The hero's eyes and face stay at least 0.25 in inside the trim and 0.375 in from the spine fold. Only the body, feathers, fur or the edge of an object may run off the page.

**Rule 5.5. Stay out of the logo's clear space.** The hero may overlap the masthead's color field. It never enters the logo's clear space (section 6).

**Rule 5.6. Cover lines never cross the eyes or face.** A strip may overlap a neck, a wing or a background.

**Rule 5.7. One hero.** No second animal, no crowd. Generate props for the hero separately and composite them if Midjourney drifts.

**Rule 5.8. Resolution.** The hero cut-out must be 300 dpi at placed size. Nothing below 200 dpi gets placed. Upscale before cutting out (Vol. 1 art used a 4x upscale), then sharpen after scaling.

---

## 6. Masthead and logo

The logo rules live in the Brand Standards hub, section Logo. Read it before you place the masthead. What a cover designer needs at hand:

**Rule 6.1. Place the file. Never rebuild it.** The masthead is the logo pack's horizontal lockup with tagline (file name pattern SIDE-EYE_horizontal-tagline_<color>), print CMYK PDF. Never retype SIDE EYE or HISTORY, ALLEGEDLY. in Anton or any font, and never ask Midjourney for the logo or the words. Vol. 1 was set before the logo pack existed and used typed Anton letters; do not copy that.

**Rule 6.2. Check the version.** Use the newest logo pack on the Brand Standards hub (version 1.1 as of November 24, 2026). Work already started may finish on the old version within 30 days.

**Rule 6.3. Position.** Put the masthead in the top color field, aligned to the outside and top live-area edges, with air around it. It is the highest element on the cover except an optional banner line (see the open question on banners in section 14).

**Rule 6.4. Clear space.** On a cover, leave the height of the eye (about 1.75X) on every side, where X is the width of the pink iris in the file you placed. The bare minimum is 1X. Measure from the eye's points, the letter edges and the tagline's comma. Nothing enters the clear space: no hero, no strip, no brush stroke, no busy picture detail.

**Rule 6.5. Size.** Make the masthead as wide as the top field allows after clear space. Never place the tagline lockup smaller than 60 mm wide (the print minimum). If you use the positive version (black and pink) on cream, it must be at least 132 mm wide, because the pink tagline is hard to read on light grounds.

**Rule 6.6. Which version on which field.** Pick the file by the field color. Never edit the file's colors.

| Top field color | Logo version | Never |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Side Eye Pink | Mono-black | A pink iris on pink |
| Tab Red | Mono-black | Pink |
| Tab Blue | Mono-white | Pink, black |
| Neon Orange, Acid Yellow, Pale Cyan, Kraft | Mono-black | Pink |
| Toner Black or Deep Navy | Reverse (cream and pink) | Positive |
| Warm Cream | Positive (black and pink), 132 mm wide or more | Reverse |
| Busy photo or art | The plate file (reverse on a black box) | Any version straight on the image |

**Rule 6.7.** The iris always glances right. Never flip, rotate, stretch, outline, shadow, glow or texture the logo.

---

## 7. Tagline, issue title and thesis line

**Rule 7.1. Tagline.** The tagline is "HISTORY, ALLEGEDLY." It is part of the masthead lockup file. Do not set it again anywhere on the front. The old tagline "History, with receipts." is retired; never use it.

**Rule 7.2. Issue title.** Copy the issue title exactly as written in the Issue Hub (for example THE MONEY IS FAKE ISSUE). Check that it matches the KDP listing title letter for letter, apostrophes included. Vol. 1 prints "THE LOSERS ISSUE" on the cover and is listed on Amazon as "The Loser's Issue"; do not repeat that mismatch.

**Rule 7.3. Issue title down one edge.** Set the issue title vertically down the edge opposite the hero, in Druk Condensed (Anton fallback), all caps. Read it top to bottom. (Design System v3 and v2; Vol. 1 printed it as a small horizontal label instead. See the open question in section 14.)

**Rule 7.4. Thesis line on every cover.** Every cover carries the issue thesis in one line, set in the body serif italic (Tiempos Text Italic, Source Serif Italic fallback), directly with the issue title. Copy it word for word from the Issue Hub. Vol. 1 printed without its thesis line; that was a miss.

| Vol. | Issue title | Thesis line (from the Issue Hub or plan) |
| :- | :- | :- |
| 1 | The Losers Issue | History belongs to whoever keeps the files. |
| 2 | The Money Is Fake Issue | Money is a promise. Someone always enforces it. (default in the Issue Hub until Heather says otherwise) |
| 3 | The Mating Issue | Who gets to choose? |

**Rule 7.5.** Type on the cover is 7 pt or larger, and every word is set in type or real scanned hand lettering. No "handwriting" fonts.

---

## 8. Cover lines

**Rule 8.1. Four at most.** The front carries four cover lines or fewer. A "Plus:" kicker or a banner line counts as one of the four. Past plans broke this every time: Addendum A planned six blurbs plus a kicker for Vol. 1, the printed Vol. 1 front has five lines, and the Mating Issue headline pass lists five lines plus a "Plus" line. Heather picks which four.

**Rule 8.2. Lines come after the thesis.** Write cover lines only after the thesis is locked. They come from the issue's headline pass.

**Rule 8.3. Short.** Keep each line to about 8 words and 40 characters. (The longest Vol. 1 line, RUPERT MURDOCH'S DAD AND THE EMU WAR, is 35 characters.) A line that needs two sentences is a deck, not a cover line.

**Rule 8.4. Typewriter mono on torn strips.** Set each line in typewriter mono (Courier Prime is the free stand-in) on a torn paper strip. Print or photograph the strip so the torn edge, paper grain and shadow are real, then paste it on in layout. Never generate the strip's words.

**Rule 8.5. Placement.** Put strips on the side opposite the hero, at angles of a few degrees, never across the hero's eyes or face, never in the logo's clear space, and at least 0.25 in inside the trim (0.375 in from the spine fold).

**Rule 8.6. True and in voice.** Every line is a bold, arguable claim backed by the piece's own text and logged in the fact-check log. No overstatement (the first Mating draft said Nettie Stevens "found the sex chromosomes," which overstates her work). No lines that announce the research ("We Read the Footnotes"). No "receipts" as a metaphor, no "It's not X, it's Y" lines, and no promises of things KDP can't print (perforations, flaps, inside covers, fold-outs, scratch-and-sniff).

**Rule 8.7. Case.** Use the wording and capitalization Heather approved in the headline pass. Do not restyle them on the cover.

---

## 9. Volume number, date, price

**Rule 9.1. Volume number.** Volumes are numbered in release order. Take the number from the Issue Hub on the day you build the cover. Write it as VOL. N (Vol. 1 spine: "VOL. 1").

**Rule 9.2. Where it goes.** Put the volume number on the spine when the spine carries text (section 10) and in the back-cover identity line (section 11). The front has no agreed place for it yet; see Gaps in the notes and 13 Open Questions for Heather.

**Rule 9.3. Cover date.** If a cover date is used, copy it from the Issue Hub (for example Feb/Mar 2027 for Vol. 3). Never guess one from the content calendar, which was written before the renumbering.

**Rule 9.4. No price on the cover.** Amazon sets and shows the price. Vol. 1 printed no price. Do not add one unless Heather asks.

---

## 10. Spine

The spine width comes from the final interior page count. Premium color on white paper: page count × 0.002347 in.

**Rule 10.1. KDP's text limit.** KDP allows spine text only on books with more than 79 pages. Under that, the spine is a solid color with no text at all, not even a number.

**Rule 10.2. Spine margins.** Keep at least 0.0625 in between spine text and each fold. Nothing on the spine may need to line up exactly with a fold, because the fold can drift 0.0625 in either way.

**Rule 10.3. Text size.** Spine text is 7 pt or larger. Take the room for text as the spine width minus 0.125 in. If that room is under about 0.1 in, 7 pt caps won't fit cleanly; leave the spine without text.

| Interior pages | Spine width (in) | Room for text (in) | Spine text? |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| Under 80 | under 0.188 | none | No (KDP) |
| 80 | 0.188 | 0.063 | Allowed by KDP, too narrow for 7 pt; no text |
| 96 | 0.225 | 0.100 | Just fits 7 pt caps |
| 112 | 0.263 | 0.138 | Yes |
| 120 | 0.282 | 0.157 | Yes |
| 132 | 0.310 | 0.185 | Yes |

The planned range for an issue is 116 to 132 pages.

**Rule 10.4. What the spine says.** Until Heather decides (open question, section 14), follow the printed Vol. 1 spine: SIDE EYE · VOL. N · THE <TITLE> ISSUE in mono caps, cream on a Toner Black field, reading top to bottom, centered on the spine. Never place the logo lockup on a spine this narrow; it falls under its minimum size.

**Rule 10.5. Color across the fold.** Run the front and back backgrounds at least 0.0625 in past each fold into the spine, or pick a spine color that matches the neighboring cover edge, so fold drift never shows a sliver of the wrong color.

---

## 11. Back cover

The back cover is one of the "money pages": everyone reads it, including people who never open the issue. It must read in under 15 seconds, make one point, and have no text block over 120 words.

**Rule 11.1. Concept.** Until Heather decides (open question, section 14), the back cover is the full-page parody ad planned for that volume in Front and Back Matter, Vols 1-3 (The Money Pages): Vol. 2, The First Bank of Yap; Vol. 3, The Train. Vol. 1 printed a toy-ad parody ("TIRED OF WINNING? CALL THE LOSERS SQUAD!") instead of the planned catalog ad. Design System v3's recipe for the back cover is a RELIEF colorway sheet (one motif repeated in every ink combination); use a colorway sheet only if Heather picks it.

**Rule 11.2. Art on the back.** Build the ad's art in one of the five processes (most often RELIEF or WOOD TYPE), with no generated lettering. Historical figures may appear as generated caricature or toy-style likenesses (Vol. 1 did this with Napoleon, Hatshepsut and Hannibal). Living people may not appear in a way that suggests they did something the issue can't prove.

**Rule 11.3. Identity line.** Set one small mono line near the bottom: SIDE EYE Vol. N · The <Title> Issue · History, allegedly. (Vol. 1 did this.) Copy the title exactly as on the front.

**Rule 11.4. Elevator pitch.** Brand Identity: Name, Tagline and Pitch says the pitch belongs on the back cover: "You were right to suspect the story you were told was bullshit. The good news is the truth is more interesting." Vol. 1 left it off. Include it unless Heather says otherwise (open question, section 14).

**Rule 11.5. Barcode zone.** KDP prints the barcode on the back cover in a white box 2 in wide by 1.2 in tall, in the lower right corner, beside the spine. Keep that zone empty: no text, no faces, no key art.

| Item | Value |
| :- | :- |
| Barcode box | 2.0 in wide × 1.2 in tall |
| Position | Lower right corner of the back cover, next to the spine |
| From the spine fold | 0.25 in (Vol. 1 measured 0.26 in) |
| From the bottom trim | 0.25 in (Vol. 1 measured 0.27 in) |
| What wins | The barcode box on KDP's downloaded template for your page count |

Vol. 1 drew its own white box in this zone. If you draw one, match the template box exactly and put nothing under its edges.

**Rule 11.6. Bleed on the back.** Run the back cover's background 0.125 in past the top, bottom and outside trim. On Vol. 1 the black field stopped at the trim and the bleed was cream, so any cutting drift shows a cream sliver.

**Rule 11.7. Text safety.** All back-cover text sits at least 0.25 in inside the trim and 0.375 in from the spine fold. Minimum 7 pt.

**Rule 11.8. No inside covers.** KDP paperbacks don't print the inside of the cover. Never plan art, answers, collectibles or a star chart there. There are no flaps either.

---

## 12. Cover file spec (KDP full wrap)

One PDF holds the back cover, spine and front cover as one spread. Full rules for export and upload are in 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP).

**The size formula.** Trim is 8.5 × 11 in. Bleed is 0.125 in.

- Width = 0.125 + 8.5 (back) + spine + 8.5 (front) + 0.125 = **17.25 in + spine**
- Height = 0.125 + 11 + 0.125 = **11.25 in**
- Spine = **interior page count × 0.002347 in** (premium color on white)

| Interior pages | Spine (in) | Full wrap (in) | Pixels at 300 dpi |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| 96 | 0.2253 | 17.4753 × 11.25 | 5,243 × 3,375 |
| 112 | 0.2629 | 17.5129 × 11.25 | 5,254 × 3,375 |
| 116 | 0.2723 | 17.5223 × 11.25 | 5,257 × 3,375 |
| 120 | 0.2816 | 17.5316 × 11.25 | 5,259 × 3,375 |
| 124 | 0.2910 | 17.5410 × 11.25 | 5,262 × 3,375 |
| 128 | 0.3004 | 17.5504 × 11.25 | 5,265 × 3,375 |
| 132 | 0.3098 | 17.5598 × 11.25 | 5,268 × 3,375 |

**Rule 12.1. Template first.** Download KDP's cover template for the exact final page count, trim and paper, and build on it. If the template and this table disagree, the template wins. If the page count changes by even two pages, download a new template and rebuild the wrap.

**Rule 12.2. Front panel art.** The front panel with bleed is 8.625 × 11.25 in, so front art needs at least 2,588 × 3,375 px. Use 300 dpi at placed size.

**Rule 12.3. Zones on each panel.**

| Zone | Front | Back |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Bleed | 0.125 in past top, bottom and outside trim | Same |
| Text and faces | 0.25 in inside top, bottom and outside trim | Same |
| Spine side | 0.375 in from the spine fold (0.25 in plus 0.125 in for spine shift) | Same |
| Barcode | none | 2 × 1.2 in box, lower right by the spine |

**Rule 12.4. Export.**

- CMYK only. Convert every RGB image before export. No spot colors.
- 300 dpi images, flattened transparency, all fonts embedded.
- No crop marks, registration marks, color bars or template guides.
- Matte finish.
- One PDF. KDP suggests 40 MB or less.
- Open the font list in the PDF before upload. Only house fonts or their listed stand-ins may appear. Vol. 1's cover PDF came out of a browser in RGB and carried a fallback font (DejaVu Sans Mono) for a missing glyph.

**Rule 12.5. Keep a clean art file.** Save the cover art with no type in it. Vol. 1's cover art image has an old "SIDE EYE" masthead and an old cover-line strip baked into the picture, with new type pasted over them. That locks old words into the art. Keep type on its own live layers.

**Rule 12.6. File names and handoff.** Name and file the cover as 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs says. The precedent is SIDE-EYE-Vol1-Cover-KDP-final.pdf in the issue's "to print" folder. The final cover PDF in that folder is the only cover anyone may use downstream (YouTube end screens, listings, ads). Never use a draft cover file.

---

## 13. Midjourney prompts for covers

The house prompt templates, settings and fixes live in 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards). This section gives the cover pattern only.

**The cover is two generations, then a composite.** Generate the background (no hero) and the hero separately. Cut the hero out in Photoshop and composite it in layout.

**Standing rules for every cover prompt:**

- End with `--style raw --v 8.2` and the template's settings. Add `--hd` (covers are full-page art).
- Background: `--ar 17:22` (the trim's shape), `--s 250`, the approved emu cover as `--sref` at `--sw 30`.
- Hero: `--ar 1:2`, no style reference, on a plain white background.
- Never name an artist, studio, shop or magazine. Describe materials and methods.
- Never ask for text. The background prompt keeps `--no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, animals, people`.
- No prompt may ask for the SIDE EYE logo, the eye mark or the words SIDE EYE. If a result shows lettering or an eye logo, reject it.
- Give two prompts per image: A is the safer read, B is the bolder joke.

**Background pattern** (Design System v3 template, with the cover add-on in brackets):

```
photorealistic mixed-media collage magazine cover background, [one surreal scene], flat saturated blue sky, a flat fluorescent pink color block across the top third for the masthead, torn newsprint and found ephemera pasted along the edges, a loose hand-painted fluorescent pink brush border, a thin wobbly hand-drawn black ink frame, coarse halftone grain, open space on the [hero side] for a figure, [empty space down the opposite edge for a vertical title] --ar 17:22 --style raw --s 250 --v 8.2 --hd --sref [approved emu cover URL] --sw 30 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, animals, people
```

Swap "fluorescent pink color block" for "flat red" or "flat blue" when the issue's top field is Tab Red or Tab Blue. The flat field is replaced with a vector fill in layout anyway (Rule 4.3).

**Hero pattern** (Design System v3 template):

```
photorealistic [animal] head and neck leaning in from the [right/left] side of the frame as if peeking around a corner, exaggerated oversized round eyes swiveled toward the viewer, detailed feathers or fur, isolated on a plain white background --ar 1:2 --style raw --v 8.2 --hd --no text, full body
```

**Worked A/B example (Vol. 1, for practice only).** These rebuild the approved emu cover. Do not reuse them for a new issue.

Background A (safer read):

```
photorealistic mixed-media collage magazine cover background, an empty golden outback wheat field with a silver second-place winners podium and a vintage open-top truck parked beside it, flat saturated blue sky, a flat fluorescent pink color block across the top third for the masthead, torn newsprint and found ephemera pasted along the edges, a loose hand-painted fluorescent pink brush border, a thin wobbly hand-drawn black ink frame, coarse halftone grain, open space on the right for a figure, empty space down the left edge for a vertical title --ar 17:22 --style raw --s 250 --v 8.2 --hd --sref [approved emu cover URL] --sw 30 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, animals, people
```

Background B (bolder joke):

```
photorealistic mixed-media collage magazine cover background, a silver second-place winners podium standing alone in an empty golden outback wheat field with a tiny 1930s army truck parked on the top step, flat saturated blue sky, a flat fluorescent pink color block across the top third for the masthead, torn newsprint and found ephemera pasted along the edges, a loose hand-painted fluorescent pink brush border, a thin wobbly hand-drawn black ink frame, coarse halftone grain, open space on the right for a figure, empty space down the left edge for a vertical title --ar 17:22 --style raw --s 250 --v 8.2 --hd --sref [approved emu cover URL] --sw 30 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, animals, people
```

Hero A (safer read):

```
photorealistic emu head and neck leaning in from the right side of the frame as if peeking around a corner, exaggerated oversized round eyes swiveled toward the viewer, detailed shaggy feathers, isolated on a plain white background --ar 1:2 --style raw --v 8.2 --hd --no text, full body
```

Hero B (bolder joke):

```
photorealistic emu head and neck leaning in from the right side of the frame as if peeking around a corner, exaggerated oversized round eyes swiveled hard toward the viewer in a skeptical side-eye, one feather sticking straight up, detailed shaggy feathers, isolated on a plain white background --ar 1:2 --style raw --v 8.2 --hd --no text, full body
```

**If a cover prompt misbehaves:** writing appears, add "completely blank" to the object (a blank newspaper, a blank sign). The collage looks digital-clean, raise `--sw` and add "paper edges lifting with soft shadows." Neon turns to glow, add "flat ink, printed on paper, no glow, no gradient." A period prop drifts, generate it alone and composite it.

**Log every prompt.** Record the final A and B prompts, the chosen job, and the upscale used, in the issue's art-direction doc (template in 11 Templates).

---

## 14. Open questions that touch covers

Until Heather decides, follow the interim rule. Each is also listed in 13 Open Questions for Heather.

| Question | Interim rule (follow until Heather decides) |
| :- | :- |
| Back cover: v3 RELIEF colorway sheet, or a parody ad per The Money Pages (as printed on Vol. 1)? | Parody ad per The Money Pages (Rule 11.1) |
| Spine: v2 Addendum A says a pink field with the volume number only, or no text. The printed Vol. 1 spine has SIDE EYE · VOL. 1 · THE LOSERS ISSUE on black. Which is the series spine? | Follow the printed Vol. 1 spine when the book has 96 pages or more |
| Cover-line type: v3 and the hub say typewriter mono on torn strips. Vol. 1 printed most lines in condensed display type on solid label strips. | Typewriter mono on torn strips (Rule 8.4) |
| Issue title: v3 says vertical down one edge. Vol. 1 printed a small horizontal label under the tagline. | Vertical down the edge opposite the hero (Rule 7.3) |
| Banner line: Vol. 1 ran a fifth line in a black bar above the masthead. Allowed? | It counts as one of the four lines (Rule 8.1) |
| Pink: print keeps one pink focus per page, but the v3 cover template uses a pink masthead field and a pink brush border. Does the one-focus rule apply to covers? | Follow the v3 cover template; give pink to no other element except the one thing to see first |
| Elevator pitch on the back cover (Brand Identity) versus Vol. 1, which left it off | Include it (Rule 11.4) |
| The Nothing Issue plan calls for an almost blank cover with "Open it." Does a special issue get to break the COLLAGE recipe? | Follow the COLLAGE recipe until Heather approves an exception |

---

## 15. Cover approval steps

1. **Brief.** Pull the issue title, volume number, thesis line, cover lines (four or fewer) and cover date from the Issue Hub. Confirm Heather has picked the lines.
2. **Concept.** Write concept A and concept B: the one-sentence idea, the tie to the thesis, the hero, the hero side (by volume), the top field color and the joke. Check each against the Scorecard's "Must NOT claim" list.
3. **Heather picks a concept.** No generating at full scale before this.
4. **Generate.** Run A and B prompts for the background and the hero. Log them.
5. **Comp.** Build the front at full size: art layers, flat vector fields, placed logo file, set type, torn-strip cover lines.
6. **Self-check.** Run the cover checklist (section 17).
7. **Independent review.** A second reviewer (person or agent) checks the comp against this document and the Scorecard. Covers are high-stakes pieces.
8. **Heather reviews the front.** Show it at full size and at thumbnail size, the way a shopper sees it on Amazon. Heather approves or sends changes.
9. **Build the wrap.** After the interior page count is final, download the KDP template, build spine and back cover, and export the PDF.
10. **Preflight.** Run KDP's online previewer and fix every flag. Order a proof copy when Heather has approved one for the issue (neon builds can't be judged on screen).
11. **Final yes.** Heather approves the final cover PDF before upload. File it in the issue's "to print" folder.

---

## 16. Common mistakes (from past covers)

| What went wrong | Where it happened | Do this instead |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Type baked into the cover art image, then covered with new type | Vol. 1 cover art | Keep the art file free of all lettering (Rule 12.5) |
| Masthead field color taken from the generated art; it printed salmon | Vol. 1 front | Lay a flat vector field in the house build (Rule 4.3) |
| Masthead typed in Anton instead of placing the logo | Vol. 1 front (before the logo pack) | Place the lockup file (Rule 6.1) |
| No thesis line on the cover | Vol. 1 front | Every cover carries the thesis (Rule 7.4) |
| Too many cover lines | Addendum A plan (6 plus a kicker); Vol. 1 printed (5); Mating headline pass (5 plus a Plus line) | Four, total (Rule 8.1) |
| Back cover background stopped at the trim; cream bleed | Vol. 1 back | Run fields 0.125 in past the trim (Rule 11.6) |
| Cover PDF exported in RGB with a fallback font | Vol. 1 cover PDF | CMYK, house fonts only, check the font list (Rule 12.4) |
| Other teams used the draft emu cover instead of the printed one | YouTube end screens and frames, November 2026 | The final PDF in the "to print" folder is the only source (Rule 12.6) |
| Issue title spelled two ways | Vol. 1 cover vs Amazon listing | Match the listing letter for letter (Rule 7.2) |
| Wrong volume number after renumbering | Mating and Money Is Fake manuscripts and teases | Take the number from the Issue Hub on build day (Rule 9.1) |
| Copy promised things KDP can't print (perforations, back flap, inside back cover, fold-out) | Mating plan; content calendar | No promises of print tricks (Rules 8.6, 11.8) |
| Cover line overstated the facts or announced the research | Mating first cover lines | Fact-check every line; no research announcements (Rule 8.6) |
| Old tagline ("History, with receipts.") | Addendum A before October 7 | HISTORY, ALLEGEDLY. from the lockup file (Rule 7.1) |
| Cover art generated at 4:5 from the v2 prompts | Design System v2 cover prompts | `--ar 17:22` for the front (section 13) |

---

## 17. Cover checklist

Run it on the front alone, then on the full wrap. Every box must be ticked before Heather sees the final.

**Concept**
- [ ] One surreal idea, written in one sentence
- [ ] The picture argues the issue's thesis
- [ ] Nothing in the art implies a claim the Scorecard rates below Proven
- [ ] Hero is an animal, an object or a historical caricature; no living person as hero

**Art**
- [ ] Six COLLAGE layers, each on its own layer or group
- [ ] Hero has oversized eyes looking at the reader
- [ ] Hero breaks the frame and enters from the correct side for this volume (odd right, even left)
- [ ] Hero's eyes and face 0.25 in inside the trim and 0.375 in from the spine fold
- [ ] Flat color fields are vector fills in house CMYK builds
- [ ] No generated lettering or logo anywhere in the art
- [ ] Art is 300 dpi at placed size; front art at least 2,588 × 3,375 px
- [ ] Total ink under 240% everywhere

**Logo and type**
- [ ] Masthead is the placed horizontal-tagline file from the current logo pack
- [ ] Logo version matches the field color (section 6 table)
- [ ] Clear space of one eye height on every side; nothing inside it
- [ ] Issue title matches the Issue Hub and the KDP listing exactly
- [ ] Issue title runs down the edge opposite the hero
- [ ] Thesis line present, word for word, in serif italic
- [ ] Four cover lines or fewer, counting any banner or Plus line
- [ ] Each line about 8 words or fewer, in typewriter mono on a torn strip
- [ ] Every line is on the fact-check log and passes the voice rules
- [ ] No strip crosses the hero's eyes or face or the logo's clear space
- [ ] All type 7 pt or larger, 0.25 in inside the trim, 0.375 in from the spine fold
- [ ] No price on the cover

**Spine**
- [ ] Spine width = final page count × 0.002347 in, matching the KDP template
- [ ] No spine text under 80 pages, or when the room for text is under about 0.1 in
- [ ] Spine text 0.0625 in clear of both folds, reading top to bottom
- [ ] Volume number on the spine matches the Issue Hub

**Back cover**
- [ ] Concept matches the approved plan for this volume
- [ ] Reads in under 15 seconds; no block over 120 words
- [ ] Identity line present; elevator pitch present unless Heather said no
- [ ] Barcode zone (2 × 1.2 in, lower right by the spine) empty
- [ ] Background bleeds 0.125 in past top, bottom and outside trim
- [ ] Nothing planned for inside covers or flaps

**File**
- [ ] Built on KDP's template for the final page count
- [ ] Width = 17.25 in + spine; height 11.25 in
- [ ] CMYK, no spot colors, transparency flattened, fonts embedded, house fonts only
- [ ] No crop marks, color bars or template guides
- [ ] KDP previewer shows no flags
- [ ] Every image on the cover has a credit line in the colophon
- [ ] Heather's written yes recorded before upload
