# 03 Page and Spread Standards
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Theo Banks, Art Director, Spreads and Page Recipes
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review
**Who this is for:** Any designer, art director, outside vendor or AI agent who lays out pages of a SIDE EYE issue.
**Use it when:** You plan an issue's page order, build a flatplan, design any interior page or spread, or check a spread before handoff.
**Related:** 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files · 02 Cover Standards · 04 Grid and Typography · 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards) · 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits · 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP) · 08 Quality Checklists · 11 Templates (issue brief, flatplan, art-direction doc, handoff note) · 13 Open Questions for Heather · Brand Standards hub · SIDE EYE Design System v3: Neon Zine Print · SIDE EYE Design System v2 Core · v2 Addendum A, KDP Production Spec 8.5 x 11 · Front and Back Matter, Vols. 1-3 (The Money Pages) · Content Calendar, Volumes 2-12

---

## 1. How to use this document

This document tells you what goes on every page of a SIDE EYE issue, in what order, and how each kind of page is built.

- **Section 2** is the issue's running order and page budget.
- **Sections 3 to 6** are the systems that run through every page: LOUD and QUIET, filing tabs, recurring furniture and the money-page rules.
- **Section 7** has one recipe per page type. Every recipe uses the same ten rows.
- **Section 8** lists what Amazon KDP can't print and what to build instead.
- **Section 9** is the method for planning a flatplan. The blank flatplan lives in 11 Templates.
- **Section 10** is the spread-level checklist.

Rules about the grid, type sizes and styles live in 04 Grid and Typography. Rules about making the art and writing prompts live in 05. Rights and credit lines live in 06. Ink limits, file export and KDP upload live in 07. This document restates only what you need at hand while you lay out a page. Where it points to another document, that document wins on its own subject. Where anything here seems to disagree with the Brand Standards hub, the hub wins and you should flag it.

**Issue numbers changed on November 24, 2026 (Heather's call).** The Money Is Fake Issue is now Vol. 2 and The Mating Issue is now Vol. 3. Older documents, including The Money Pages and the Content Calendar, still use the old numbers. This document names issues by title and new number: Vol. 1 (The Loser's Issue), Vol. 2 (The Money Is Fake Issue), Vol. 3 (The Mating Issue).

### Short names used in the recipes

| Short name | Means |
| :- | :- |
| Display | Druk Condensed, or its stand-in Anton |
| Wood | Licensed Hamilton wood-type faces, or the stand-ins Alfa Slab One, Rye and Ultra (two faces per plate at most) |
| Body | Tiempos Text, or its stand-in Source Serif 4 |
| Mono | GT America Mono, or its stand-in IBM Plex Mono |
| Typewriter | Courier Prime, typed, printed, torn and pasted |
| Hand | Real handwriting, scanned. Never a handwriting font |
| Pink, Cyan, Orange, Yellow | Side Eye Pink, Pale Cyan, Neon Orange, Acid Yellow |
| Navy, Peach | Deep Navy, Peach (period skin only) |
| Cream, Kraft, Newsprint | Warm Cream, Kraft and Newsprint Gray paper grounds |

Which face set is live (paid or free stand-ins) is set in 04 Grid and Typography. The newest issue plans use the free stand-ins only.

## 2. Anatomy of an issue

### 2.1 Fixed facts

| Item | Rule | Source |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Trim | 8.5 x 11 in, KDP paperback, premium color | v2 Addendum A |
| Interior page count | Even number. Plan for 116 to 132 pages including front and back matter | v2 Addendum A |
| Recent counts | Vol. 3 flatplan estimate: 118 pages. Vol. 2 plan: about 124 pages | Vol. 3 art briefs flatplan; Vol. 2 Issue Hub |
| Page 1 | A right-hand page. Even pages are left-hand pages, odd pages are right-hand pages | Vol. 3 art briefs flatplan |
| Spreads | Pages 2-3, 4-5, 6-7 and so on. Page 1 and the last interior page stand alone | Follows from the page 1 rule |
| Inside covers | KDP prints nothing on the inside front or inside back cover. Plan no content there | Vol. 3 art briefs |
| Spine | At about 120 pages the spine is about 0.28 in, too narrow for reliable text. Solid pink field with the volume number only, or no text | v2 Addendum A |
| One theme | Every issue has one theme, a thesis line and three filing tabs | Content Calendar; Brand Standards hub, The magazine |

### 2.2 The standard running order

Every issue uses the same running order so readers learn where things live (Content Calendar, "The standard issue template"). The page counts below come from the newest full flatplan (Vol. 3, 118 pages) with the range seen across Vols. 1 to 3. Use them as a starting budget, then adjust to the word count (section 2.3).

Notes on the order:

- **The Prank is retired for now.** Heather, November 24, 2026: "No more pranks for now." Neither Vol. 2 nor Vol. 3 has one. If it returns, build it with the tabloid recipe (7.21).
- **Corrections must print every issue** (Brand Standards hub, The magazine). Vol. 1 used its last spread for them. The Vol. 2 and Vol. 3 plans give the last spread to other pieces and have no corrections slot. Until Heather decides where corrections live, reserve one page for them in Out Back and mark it OPEN in the flatplan (open question).
- **The collectible** depends on Heather's open call on extras (section 8). Hold the two pages in the plan and mark them PENDING.
- **The newsprint tabloid** named in Design System v2 and v3 has no slot in the Vol. 2 or Vol. 3 plans. Out Back carries the newsprint look. See 7.21 (open question).

### 2.3 Turning words into pages

Budget pages from the manuscript's word count. In the Vol. 3 flatplan, features planned at **about 130 to 220 words per page**, art and headers included. Plan at about 170 words per page for a mixed feature. Plan toward 150 for QUIET pages (wider margins, 10/15 body) and art-heavy openers. Plan toward 200 for interior pages with one spot illustration.

| Piece type | Words seen in Vols. 1 to 3 manuscripts | Pages seen |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Opening letter | 370 to 590 | 1 to 3 |
| Letters to the AI Dead (all letters) | 930 to 1,170 | 6 to 12 |
| Still Wrong | 140 to 155 | 1 to 2 |
| Apocrypha Index | 460 to 575 | 3 to 8 |
| Ranked | 280 to 380 | 2 to 6 |
| Diptych opener | 115 to 255 | 2 |
| Feature (short) | 450 to 800 | 2 to 6 |
| Feature (long) | 1,100 to 1,900 | 6 to 14 |
| Box Score | 240 to 340 (up to 1,020 when it is the whole feature) | 2 (up to 8) |
| Catalog sidebar | 250 to 640 | 2 to 4 |
| Map spread | 210 to 330 plus pins | 2 |
| Timeline | 550 to 830 | 2 to 4 |
| Out Back column | 210 to 410 | 1 to 4 |
| Sources section | 430 to 970 | 2 to 6 |
| Coming Up Next | 90 to 200 | 1 to 2 |

If the plan runs past 132 pages, cut pieces in the order the issue's Issue Hub lists. Never shrink type below the 04 sizes to fit.

## 3. LOUD and QUIET

Every page is one register or the other. Pick the register by subject, before you pick a process.

| | LOUD | QUIET |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Use for | Comedy, myth-busting, most features, columns, Out Back | Deaths, atrocities, real suffering, real harm done to real people |
| Paper | Cream or kraft (Out Back: newsprint) | Newsprint gray |
| Ink | Black plus two inks per image. One pink focus per page | Black, gray and one muted tab tint. No neon, ever. No pink |
| Art | RELIEF, COLLAGE, WOOD TYPE, HYBRID | Archival images, plain halftone photos, type. No collage, no jokes, no jokes in captions |
| Type | Headlines may run on every axis on openers (one diagonal per spread) | Horizontal only. Body 10/15 in two columns. Wide margins |
| Furniture | Stamps, corner bars, wood-type plates, windows | Gray hairlines, torn-edge dividers in gray, the tab as a muted tint |
| Dark fields | Navy on single opener pages only | An 85 percent black tint, never a solid |

**Rules for using the two registers:**

1. Every issue has at least one QUIET feature (Content Calendar). Vol. 2 plans three; Vol. 3 plans several, plus QUIET pages inside LOUD features.
2. A feature can change register partway through. Change at a section break and start the new register on a new page. Example: Carthage in Vol. 1 opened LOUD, went QUIET at "How It Actually Ended," then lightened again. Several Vol. 3 features open LOUD and close on a QUIET page.
3. When a LOUD page faces a QUIET page, print the LOUD page's RELIEF art in the **cyan + orange** variant, with no pink (Design System v3, RELIEF variants).
4. Never put a joke, a pun headline or a comic spot on a QUIET page or in its captions.
5. QUIET pages get no illustrations of the people harmed. Real people on QUIET pages appear only in real period images or public records.
6. A generated image on a QUIET page is allowed only as a plain NEWSPRINT still life of an object or an empty place, with no people, no pink and no color, captioned "Illustration" so nobody takes it for a real record. Design System v3 says QUIET features get no illustrations but also keeps a NEWSPRINT prompt for QUIET pages, so follow this narrow reading until Heather decides (open question).
7. Never use WOOD TYPE, COLLAGE or type on a diagonal on a QUIET page.

**How to alternate.** Vary density from spread to spread so turning pages has rhythm (Design System v2, Pillar 1). Section openers are the densest pages in the book. Interior LOUD pages are lighter. Group QUIET features into runs where the subject allows, and give the run a clear start and end. Do not scatter single QUIET pages between jokes.

## 4. Filing tabs

Each issue sorts its stories under three labels. The labels change every issue. The colors do not.

| Position | Color | Build | Vol. 1 (The Loser's Issue) | Vol. 2 (The Money Is Fake Issue) | Vol. 3 (The Mating Issue) |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| First tab | Tab Red | See hub print builds | Lost the Fight | Promised | Chose |
| Second tab | Tab Blue | See hub print builds | Lost the Credit | Broke | Wasn't Chosen |
| Third tab | Tab Gray | K48 (v2 Addendum A) | Lost the Story | Paid For | Had the Choice Taken |

Vol. 2's tabs come from its manuscript, taken as the default in its Issue Hub over the Content Calendar's older set. Confirm each issue's tabs in its Issue Hub.

**What a tab means.** A tab tells the reader which kind of story this is, inside the issue's theme. A piece can earn more than one tab (Carthage earned all three in Vol. 1).

**Rules:**

1. Put the tab on every page of a tabbed piece, on the evidence rail at the page's outside edge. Readers are told in the contents to "look for the tab in the corner of the page."
2. Explain all three tabs on the contents spread, with a large key (label, color, one-line meaning).
3. Restate the tab key on The Files divider.
4. Up Front columns, money pages, section openers, the sources section and the colophon carry no tab. Out Back columns carry a tab only when the issue plan gives them one.
5. On QUIET pages, print the tab as a muted tint, never at full strength. Vol. 1 practice was a 30 to 60 percent tint.
6. Map pins and timeline icons take the color of their tab.
7. When a piece carries two or three tabs, no source says how to show them. Proposed default, not yet approved: stack the tabs on the rail in red, blue, gray order (gap, see 13).
8. Tab Gray has no swatch in the Brand Standards hub. Use the Addendum A build (K48) until the hub adds one (gap).

## 5. Recurring furniture

These pieces appear in every issue. Build them once in vector and reuse them. Never generate them.

| Piece | What it is | Where it goes | Rules |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| FILE NO. stamp | A rectangular rubber-stamp mark reading SIDE EYE / FILE NO. 00X, where X is the volume number (Vol. 2 is FILE NO. 002) | Cover, section openers, the collectible | Slightly rotated, never over body text. If the page's one pink is already the focus, print the stamp in black. It never carries a verdict word or a proof rating |
| Corner bar | A solid black bar with the column name reversed out | Every recurring column: Letters to the AI Dead, Still Wrong, Apocrypha Index, Ranked, Field Guide, Report Card, Last Words | Mono, 7 pt, all caps, tracked +80 (v2 Addendum A). Design System v2 places it at the top outer corner; Addendum A puts it in the deeper bottom margin with the folio. Follow Addendum A until Heather decides: bottom margin, outer corner, mono label (open question; style name and grid detail in 04, section 10.5) |
| Coda mark | A small closing mark at the end of a column | End of every recurring column | The flat eye logo for most columns. A hand-drawn pixel icon (24-pixel grid) for each AI Dead letter. A tiny tombstone for Still Wrong |
| Drop cap | A large display capital | Feature openings, A-to-Z catalogs, Apocrypha entries | 4 lines deep, 52 pt (v2 Addendum A). In A-to-Z lists: big display capital, entry name set vertically beneath it in mono, one-line subtitle in body serif beside it |
| Evidence rail | The narrow outside strip of each page | Every interior page except full-bleed art | Carries, top to bottom, the filing tab, the running section title in mono and the folio. Also holds host asides and footnotes in mono 7 pt. Rotated type runs bottom-to-top on left pages and top-to-bottom on right pages (Vol. 1 practice). Width and position: see 04 |
| Host asides | Short bracketed notes signed [H.C.] or [E.G.] | Footnotes and the rail | Host facts must be true. Real personal details come only from the hosts |
| Paper panel | A flat cream or newsprint panel pasted over art | Wherever body text meets collage or busy art | Body text always sits on a clean panel, never on busy layers |
| Folio | Page number | Bottom margin | Mono, 7 pt, all caps, tracked +80 |
| Isometric headline | One three-dimensional, architectural headline | One per volume, on a LOUD feature | Built in vector |
| Typographic pun | One visual wordplay title | At most one per LOUD feature | Built by hand in layout |

---

## 6. Money-page rules

The money pages are the two front spreads, the classifieds, the last spread and the back cover. Everyone reads them, including people who put the issue down after four minutes.

1. Make every item readable in under 15 seconds.
2. Make every infographic one point. One chart, one idea, one kicker.
3. Keep every block of text to 120 words or fewer.
4. Set the kicker large, in open space, away from the chart.
5. Label charts in mono. Keep the art to one process and black plus two inks.
6. Set the classifieds in 7 pt mono, in two or three narrow columns with hairline rules, with no images. Place them directly before the last spread.
7. Set any tiny legal or disclaimer line at 7 pt or larger. The Money Pages ask for 6 pt in places, but the hub's minimum is 7 pt and KDP softens type below that.
8. Take the copy from Front and Back Matter, Vols. 1-3 (The Money Pages) or the issue's Issue Hub. Don't rewrite it in layout. Em-dashes in this copy are an open question in the hub. Set the approved copy as written and don't add or remove dashes yourself.

---

## 7. Page recipes

Every recipe has the same ten rows. "Inks" counts black plus the named inks. "Image count" is per page or per spread as stated. Word counts come from the Vols. 1 to 3 manuscripts and The Money Pages.

Rules that apply to every recipe, so they are not repeated:

- One process per image. Never mix RELIEF and COLLAGE in one frame except by the HYBRID recipe.
- One pink focus per page in print. If two things are pink, one is wrong.
- No generated lettering anywhere. Every word is set in type or hand-lettered.
- Minimum type size 7 pt.
- Generated likenesses of historical figures are allowed. Living people follow the sourced-public-facts-only rule. Letters to the AI Dead figures are always skulls wearing a prop.
- An illustration never imitates a period document or sits inside one. Illustrations carry the argument; period images carry the proof.
- KDP prints on white paper, so every cream, kraft or newsprint ground is a printed flood. Bleed it off every outside edge.
- Nothing essential within 0.5 in of the gutter.

### 7.1 Cover

Full standards: 02 Cover Standards.

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Stop a shopper at a shelf with one surreal idea that states the theme |
| Process | COLLAGE, six layers |
| Inks | Full set allowed. Masthead in a flat neon field |
| Paper | Cream or kraft ground |
| Type | Display masthead; tagline "HISTORY, ALLEGEDLY." in small mono caps, wide tracking, under the masthead; thesis line in body italic under the issue title; cover lines in Typewriter on torn strips; theme title vertical down one edge |
| Grid use | Full bleed. Text 0.25 in inside trim, allowing up to 0.125 in spine shift |
| Image count | One background scene plus one cut-out hero |
| Word count | Four cover lines at most, plus masthead, tagline, title and thesis |
| Must-haves | Hero with oversized eyes breaking the frame; hero side alternates by volume; air around the masthead |
| Never | More than four cover lines; the word "perforated" (KDP can't perforate); generated lettering |

### 7.2 Title page and colophon (page 1)

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Open the book quietly; carry the credits KDP won't print inside the cover |
| Process | Type only |
| Inks | Black, plus the logo's pink iris |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Logo or masthead small; thesis line in body italic; colophon in body 8.5/12 or mono |
| Grid use | Centered title block; colophon on the lower half, 6 to 12 columns |
| Image count | 0 (the flat eye logo only) |
| Word count | 80 to 200 |
| Must-haves | One Midjourney credit for all generated art; image credits (or a pointer to where they run); the influence credit line from Design System v3; volume number and date |
| Never | Influence names anywhere else in the book; a tab; a joke that spoils a later page |

The hub says the magazine carries one Midjourney credit at the front. The Vol. 3 plan puts the whole colophon on page 1. Exact credit wording: 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits.

### 7.3 Front spreads (pages 2-3 and 4-5)

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Make the issue's argument in one look, before the contents |
| Process | One house process per spread. Examples: a sports results page in display type (Vol. 1), a listings page in NEWSPRINT (Vol. 3), a silhouette scale in RELIEF (Vol. 2) |
| Inks | Black plus two. Vol. 1 spread one used Tab Red and Tab Blue only |
| Paper | Cream, copy paper or newsprint, per the piece |
| Type | Display headline; mono labels; kicker large in open space |
| Grid use | Full 12 columns across both pages. The chart may cross the gutter; no label, number or icon within 0.5 in of it |
| Image count | One chart or one set of matching icons |
| Word count | Under 120 words per block; each item readable in 15 seconds |
| Must-haves | One point per infographic; a kicker; the money-page rules in section 6 |
| Never | A second chart; body paragraphs; a tab; anything that needs the gutter to read |

### 7.4 Contents ("What's In Here")

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Show the three sections and teach the three tabs |
| Process | WOOD TYPE + RELIEF: a poster-style contents page |
| Inks | Black plus two; one split fountain on the wood-type plate; tab red, blue and gray in the key |
| Paper | Kraft or cream |
| Type | Wood section names (UP FRONT, THE FILES, OUT BACK); mono page numbers and labels; body for the short intro |
| Grid use | Both pages. Tab key large on the left page. Nothing within 0.5 in of the gutter |
| Image count | One RELIEF icon per section as bullets, plus a colorway repeat border of one motif |
| Word count | 240 to 590 |
| Must-haves | Every piece with its page number and tabs; the tab key; one wood-type plate for the spread |
| Never | Spoilers; a second wood-type plate; listing a piece that was cut (Vol. 3's prank was cut and must come off) |

### 7.5 Opening letter

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Heather and Edwin welcome the reader and tease the issue |
| Process | RELIEF spot |
| Inks | Black plus two |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Body in two columns; title and byline at the end; scanned signatures |
| Grid use | Two text columns of 6 grid columns, or three of 4. One big display word may run vertically down the outside rail (Vol. 1) |
| Image count | 0 or 1 |
| Word count | 370 to 590 |
| Must-haves | Byline and title at the end; no spoilers |
| Never | Imagery that spoils a later joke; editor personas |

### 7.6 Section openers and dividers

Three per issue: the Up Front opener, The Files divider and the Out Back opener.

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Mark the turn into a new section and restate the theme in one short line |
| Process | Up Front: RELIEF. The Files: WOOD TYPE plate (or COLLAGE). Out Back: NEWSPRINT |
| Inks | Black plus two; one split fountain on a wood-type plate; Out Back: black on gray with one flat pink shape |
| Paper | Up Front: cream. The Files: kraft. Out Back: newsprint |
| Type | Section name huge in Wood or Display; theme line in body or mono; tab key in mono on The Files divider |
| Grid use | The Files: two pages, name stacked on the left, cut and tab key on the right. Up Front and Out Back: one page each in Vol. 3 |
| Image count | 1 (plus the split-fountain texture on a wood-type plate) |
| Word count | Under 40 |
| Must-haves | Restate the theme in one line; the FILE NO. stamp where there is room; The Files divider carries the tab key |
| Never | Body text; a second wood-type plate on the spread |

### 7.7 Letters to the AI Dead

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Letters to AI versions of historical figures, answered in character, clearly labeled parody |
| Process | RELIEF throughout |
| Inks | Pink + cyan. Pink marks each skull's prop joke, one per page |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Letters in body on a cream paper panel; correspondent names in mono ("AI GREGOR MENDEL:") |
| Grid use | Opener as a spread with the dek in open space. Each letter: a round window of about 2.25 in beside a text panel |
| Image count | 1 opener plus 1 skull per letter (four to five letters) |
| Word count | 930 to 1,170 for all letters; about 180 to 250 per letter |
| Must-haves | Each figure is a skull wearing one defining prop, in a round window; corner bar; pixel-icon coda after each letter; a parody label |
| Never | A face or portrait for an AI Dead figure; invented quotes presented as real |

### 7.8 Still Wrong

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Bust one myth fully |
| Process | RELIEF |
| Inks | Pink + cyan |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Display headline stating the myth; body for the bust |
| Grid use | One page, or a spread when a period image carries the proof on one side |
| Image count | 1 prop (plus a period image if used) |
| Word count | 140 to 155 |
| Must-haves | Corner bar; one prop as the joke; tombstone coda |
| Never | Two props; a period image recolored |

### 7.9 The Apocrypha Index

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Six to nine myths, each scored 0 to 5 |
| Process | RELIEF, one prop per entry |
| Inks | Pink + cyan. Pink on one prop per page only |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Numbered entries, each with a drop cap and a vertical mono label; body for the entry |
| Grid use | Catalog boxes of 4 or 6 grid columns; two to three entries per page |
| Image count | 1 prop per entry |
| Word count | 460 to 575 in all; about 50 to 80 per entry |
| Must-haves | Corner bar; a 0-to-5 score for each entry. Show it as a row of five small squares filled to the score, in black (Vol. 1 meter), so the page keeps its one pink |
| Never | Pink score chips on every entry (that breaks one pink per page) |

### 7.10 Ranked

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Historical figures ranked by a modern, adult criterion |
| Process | RELIEF, one prop per entry, in windows or cards |
| Inks | Pink + cyan |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Rank numbers in Display; entries in body; stats in mono |
| Grid use | Cards of 4 or 6 grid columns; Vol. 3 fit six cards on one spread |
| Image count | 1 prop per entry (five to six) |
| Word count | 280 to 380 |
| Must-haves | Corner bar; the ranking criterion stated in the dek |
| Never | Portraits of living people; a prop that implies something the research can't prove |

### 7.11 Diptych opener

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Open The Files with two stories that answer each other (Two Libraries, Two Contracts, Heavy and Light) |
| Process | Set by subject. QUIET: archival images or a plain NEWSPRINT still life. LOUD: RELIEF |
| Inks | QUIET: black and gray. LOUD: black plus two |
| Paper | Newsprint for QUIET; cream for LOUD |
| Type | Matching headline treatment on both pages; a few lines of text under each |
| Grid use | One spread, left page and right page mirrored |
| Image count | 1 per page |
| Word count | 115 to 255 |
| Must-haves | The two pages built as mirror images so the comparison reads at a glance |
| Never | A joke if either story is QUIET |

### 7.12 Feature opener with a historical person (HYBRID)

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Make the feature's argument in one picture built around a real historical person |
| Process | HYBRID: public-domain period figure cut out and reduced to black line and halftone with peach skin only; RELIEF hands and props make the argument |
| Inks | Black plus two; on navy, lines that must read go in cyan or pink |
| Paper | Flat navy or cream field. A navy opener page faces a cream page carrying the dek |
| Type | Display headline, one word set vertically; dek in body; drop cap where the text starts |
| Grid use | Opener spread: art page plus dek page. Start on a left (even) page |
| Image count | 1 composite (period figure plus generated scene) |
| Word count | Headline, dek and pull line; text may start on the facing page |
| Must-haves | Headline, deck and one pull line that tell the story on their own; caption naming the period source |
| Never | Recoloring the period figure; two navy pages facing each other; art implying a crime, deal or motive the research rates below proven |

### 7.13 Feature opener without a person

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Open a feature with a visual joke or a big scene |
| Process | COLLAGE for the issue's one full-bleed opener (its biggest feature). RELIEF visual joke for all other openers |
| Inks | COLLAGE: full set allowed. RELIEF: black plus two |
| Paper | Cream ground |
| Type | Display headline; headlines may run on every axis, one diagonal per spread at most |
| Grid use | COLLAGE: full-bleed spread with a clean panel for the dek. RELIEF: one page or a spread with lots of empty paper |
| Image count | 1 |
| Word count | Headline, dek and pull line |
| Must-haves | One idea; pink on the punchline only |
| Never | A second full-bleed COLLAGE opener in the same issue; type on the art without a panel |

### 7.14 Feature interior (LOUD)

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Carry the body of the feature with the argument in pictures and the proof in period images |
| Process | RELIEF spots plus archival images, kept apart |
| Inks | Black plus two per image; one pink focus per page |
| Paper | Cream; text on clean paper panels |
| Type | Body 9.5/13; captions mono 7.5/10; sidebar 8.5/12; pull quote in body or Display |
| Grid use | Three text columns of 4 grid columns; sidebar 4 columns; pull quote 5 or 6 columns hung into the outside margin; full-width art 12 |
| Image count | About 1 spot or period image per spread |
| Word count | 130 to 220 per page |
| Must-haves | The fixed feature frame (7.16); tab on the rail; credit line for every period image |
| Never | An illustration inside a period image or document; body text over busy art; body text on an axis |

### 7.15 QUIET feature pages

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Tell a story of real harm with care |
| Process | NEWSPRINT, quiet; archival images and type |
| Inks | Black, gray, one muted tab tint. No neon, no pink |
| Paper | Newsprint gray |
| Type | Body 10/15; headline in Display, horizontal; captions mono |
| Grid use | Two text columns of 6 grid columns; wide margins |
| Image count | 0 to 1 per spread; real period images or public records. A generated still life only as in section 3, rule 6 |
| Word count | About 150 per page |
| Must-haves | Plain captions; credit lines; the fixed feature frame |
| Never | Collage, wood type, windows, stamps, diagonal type, jokes in art or captions, illustrations of the people harmed |

### 7.16 The fixed feature frame and the What We Can Prove / What We Can't box

Every feature runs in this order (Brand Standards hub, The magazine):

1. Headline and subhead (deck)
2. An opening that breaks the familiar story
3. The body
4. The twist
5. The What We Can Prove / What We Can't box
6. The other side, stated fairly
7. Why the gap matters
8. A sources line

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Show the reader exactly how sure SIDE EYE is |
| Process | Type only |
| Inks | Black on LOUD and QUIET pages; a thin rule in the tab color on LOUD pages is allowed |
| Paper | Same as the page; on LOUD pages a clean paper panel |
| Type | Two labeled lists, WHAT WE CAN PROVE and WHAT WE CAN'T, in mono labels with body or sidebar text (8.5/12) |
| Grid use | Sidebar width (4 columns) or full text width; boxed in a plain rule |
| Image count | 0 |
| Word count | Up to 120 per box is a good ceiling (no source sets one) |
| Must-haves | Both halves filled; every line true on the evidence and matched to the research ratings; the other side follows it |
| Never | Proof stamps, verdict badges or rating labels (the hub retires them on screen; print follows the same rule until Heather decides, open question); a joke inside the box |

### 7.17 Box Score

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | A matchup between two sides, scored, with a Ruling |
| Process | WOOD TYPE fight-card poster |
| Inks | Black plus two; one split fountain on the headline |
| Paper | Kraft |
| Type | Names stacked in Wood, "VS." biggest; figures in Display 24 to 36 pt; table in mono; Ruling in a black box |
| Grid use | A spread (or one page inside a feature); the table at sidebar width (4 columns) or wider |
| Image count | 2 woodcut cuts (one per side) plus the split-fountain field |
| Word count | 240 to 340 (up to about 1,000 when the Box Score is the whole feature) |
| Must-haves | A table and a Ruling that are true on the evidence; tab |
| Never | A second wood-type plate on the spread; scoring a claim the research can't support; use on a QUIET page |

### 7.18 Catalog sidebar (A to Z)

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | One list-based section per issue, rebuilt as an alphabetical reference (The Loser's Library, The Lonely Hearts Catalog, Money, A to (Almost) Z) |
| Process | RELIEF cameos or props, or type and photographed cards if the list is QUIET |
| Inks | Black plus two (QUIET: black and gray) |
| Paper | Cream (QUIET: newsprint) |
| Type | Drop-cap format: big Display capital, entry name vertical beneath it in mono, one-line body subtitle beside it |
| Grid use | Entries in boxes of 4 or 6 columns |
| Image count | 0 to 1 per entry |
| Word count | 250 to 640 |
| Must-haves | Entries A to Z where the letters allow; tab |
| Never | Japanese or other non-English subtitles unless a fluent reader writes and checks them |

### 7.19 Map spread

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Pin every place in the issue on one map, colored by tab |
| Process | Base map drawn in layout from public-domain data; pins are RELIEF icons |
| Inks | Tab red, tab blue, tab gray pins; land in a light tint; coastlines soft black |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Pin labels in mono with leader lines; a short legend strip |
| Grid use | Full spread. Choose a projection that puts the gutter in open ocean. Insets for crowded places |
| Image count | 1 base map plus 1 pin per place (18 in Vol. 1, 29 in Vol. 3) |
| Word count | 210 to 330 plus pin labels |
| Must-haves | Every place in the issue; each pin in its tab color; insets where pins crowd |
| Never | A generated map (Midjourney can't place locations); a pin, label or coastline detail within 0.5 in of the gutter |

### 7.20 Timeline

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | The issue's theme across time, one entry per event |
| Process | RELIEF icons on a long rule |
| Inks | Black plus one tab ink per icon; the connecting rule may carry the page's one pink |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Years in Display; entries in mono |
| Grid use | One spread, or two consecutive spreads (four pages) for a long timeline. The rule may cross the gutter; no icon, date or label within 0.5 in of it |
| Image count | 1 icon per entry (17 in Vol. 1, 36 in Vol. 3) |
| Word count | 550 to 830 |
| Must-haves | Entries in date order; icon colors by tab; the word "Timeline," never "fold-out" |
| Never | A gatefold or fold-out (KDP can't print one) |

### 7.21 The newsprint tabloid section

Design System v2 put a folded 24-page newsprint tabloid inside the back cover. KDP can't bind in an insert, so v2 Addendum A turns it into a bound-in section.

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | A cheap-paper section that mixes large photos and paintings and asks to be cut up |
| Process | NEWSPRINT interiors; COLLAGE for the one tabloid poster |
| Inks | Interiors: soft black. Poster: full set allowed |
| Paper | Newsprint gray tint with a 12 percent halftone-dot overlay |
| Type | Mono-heavy type mix; display headlines |
| Grid use | Narrower margins than the main book (see 04) |
| Image count | Large, few: about 1 per page |
| Word count | Set by content |
| Must-haves | Its own clear start and end in the flatplan |
| Never | A separate paper stock, a fold-out poster or a loose insert |

The Vol. 2 and Vol. 3 plans have no tabloid section, and the prank that filled it is retired for now. Use this recipe only when an issue plan includes a tabloid (open question).

### 7.22 Paste Here pages, cut-out pages and the collectible

Design System v2 and v3 keep Paste Here pages: a dotted outline, a small scissors icon and a number matching a cut-out elsewhere in the book. The collectible (the Sacko in Vol. 1, the Valentines in Vol. 3, the SIDE EYE Bucks in Vol. 2) is a cut-out too.

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Invite the reader to cut the book up |
| Process | RELIEF cards or cut-outs; type for the backs |
| Inks | Black plus two |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Display and Typewriter on the front; mono for "To / From" lines on the back |
| Grid use | Cut-out page on a right-hand page. Printed tear line: 1 pt dashed rule, 0.08 in dashes, small scissors glyph at upper left, the whole piece 0.5 in inside the trim on every side |
| Image count | 1 per card |
| Word count | Short labels only |
| Must-haves | A sacrificial verso: the back of every cut-out page holds nothing a reader would want to keep. Generous margins inside each card, since front-to-back registration can drift. Paste Here outlines numbered to match their cut-outs |
| Never | The words "perforated" or "tear along the dotted line"; a cut-out backed by body text; a flap |

Whether collectibles print as cut-out pages plus QR printables, get dropped, or become downloads only is Heather's open call. Vol. 2's SIDE EYE Bucks become a download only if the design comes close to U.S. currency (Vol. 2 Issue Hub, Heather's decision).

### 7.23 Out Back pages (Field Guide, Report Card, Quiz, Nostalgia column, Last Words)

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Short, funny back-of-book columns |
| Process | NEWSPRINT |
| Inks | Black on gray, plus one flat pink shape per page at most (Design System v3) |
| Paper | Newsprint gray |
| Type | Display heads; body and mono; Quiz answers move to the last interior page |
| Grid use | One spot image at the head of the page; text in two or three columns |
| Image count | 1 per column (0 for the Quiz) |
| Word count | 210 to 410 per column |
| Must-haves | Corner bar on Field Guide, Report Card and Last Words; coda mark |
| Never | More than one pink shape per page; collage |

The hub's process table reads NEWSPRINT as "no neon, no collage, no jokes," which fits QUIET pages but would take the jokes out of a comedy section. Follow Design System v3's Out Back recipe above until Heather decides (open question).

### 7.24 Sources section

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Where every fact came from |
| Process | QUIET, type only |
| Inks | Black |
| Paper | Newsprint gray or plain cream |
| Type | Body or sidebar 8.5/12 in two columns; feature names as mono subheads |
| Grid use | Two columns of 6 grid columns |
| Image count | 0 |
| Word count | 430 to 970 |
| Must-haves | Sources grouped by feature and matched one to one with the fact-check log; the note naming the published scholarship each section leans on, where the issue uses one |
| Never | A title or design built on the "receipts" metaphor (no receipt-roll layout); jokes |

The section's current name is under review (open question in the hub's Decision log). Use a working title from the issue's headline pass until Heather picks one.

### 7.25 Coming Up Next

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Tease the next issue |
| Process | RELIEF |
| Inks | Black plus two (Vol. 3 used pink + acid yellow) |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Display title; one cover line from the next issue |
| Grid use | One page, or a spread |
| Image count | 1 |
| Word count | 90 to 200 |
| Must-haves | The right next issue after any renumbering |
| Never | Promising a date Heather hasn't approved |

### 7.26 Classifieds

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | A newspaper back page of joke ads that are all true to the issue |
| Process | Type only |
| Inks | Black |
| Paper | Newsprint gray or copy paper |
| Type | Mono, 7 pt, ad category in caps |
| Grid use | Two or three narrow columns with hairline rules |
| Image count | 0 |
| Word count | About 10 to 14 ads, each under 35 words |
| Must-haves | The money-page rules; placed directly before the last spread |
| Never | Images; type under 7 pt |

### 7.27 Last spread and Corrections

The last spread is a money page. Each issue fills it with its own piece: a pre-emptive corrections column (Vol. 1), SIDE EYE Bucks (Vol. 2), The Rejection File (Vol. 3).

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Send the reader off with one last laugh, readable in seconds |
| Process | Set by the piece. Build parody pages (fake letters, fake ads, fake banknotes) from type and house art, never from scanned real letterheads, seals or logos |
| Inks | Black plus two |
| Paper | Set by the piece |
| Type | Set by the piece; Corrections in mono inside a boxed rule |
| Grid use | Full spread; nothing within 0.5 in of the gutter (the last spread may not open flat) |
| Image count | 0 to 4 |
| Word count | Under 120 per block; Vol. 3's letters are under 40 words each |
| Must-haves | Money-page rules; parody that reads as parody at a glance |
| Never | Anything that could pass for real currency (Vol. 2 rules: no U.S. portraits, seals, serial numbers, Treasury or Federal Reserve wording, no U.S. note size or green, NOT LEGAL TENDER on the face) |

**Corrections, every issue.** Print real corrections in the same size type as the original error (Brand Standards hub). Heather approved publishing Vol. 1's known corrections on November 24, 2026; where they appear is still hers to say. Until she decides the slot, reserve one Out Back page and mark it OPEN (section 2.2).

### 7.28 Last interior page

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | Hold what Vol. 1 printed inside the back cover: quiz answers and the next-issue note |
| Process | Type only |
| Inks | Black |
| Paper | Cream or newsprint |
| Type | Quiz answers in mono, set upside down |
| Grid use | One left-hand page standing alone |
| Image count | 0 |
| Word count | 100 to 250 |
| Must-haves | It is the last interior page, an even number |
| Never | Content on the inside back cover |

### 7.29 Back cover

| | |
| :- | :- |
| Purpose | A money page: one joke readable from across the room |
| Process | RELIEF colorway sheet: one motif repeated in every ink combination (Design System v3). The issue's back-cover copy comes from The Money Pages |
| Inks | Every ink pairing in the set (the colorway sheet is the exception to black plus two) |
| Paper | Cream |
| Type | Mono, small; copy from The Money Pages |
| Grid use | Full bleed; KDP's barcode box lower right per the cover template; text 0.25 in inside trim |
| Image count | 1 motif, repeated |
| Word count | Under 120 per block |
| Must-haves | Barcode area left clear; money-page rules |
| Never | Anything in the barcode area |

The Money Pages describe each back cover as a period-style ad (a mail-order catalog, a fashion ad, a bank ad), while Design System v3 sets the back cover as a RELIEF colorway sheet. Vol. 3's plan built its ad as a RELIEF colorway image. Follow that approach (ad copy, RELIEF colorway art) until Heather decides (open question). Full cover specs: 02 and 07.

---

## 8. What Amazon KDP can't print, and what to build instead

Earlier plans called for print tricks that KDP print-on-demand can't do. The Vol. 2 and Vol. 3 work flagged these pages. Build the replacement in the right-hand column.

| Planned in older docs | Why it fails on KDP | Build instead | Status |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| Gatefold timeline (Vol. 1 six-panel; Vol. 3 fold-out) | No gatefolds | One printed spread, or two consecutive spreads. Rule may cross the gutter; nothing essential within 0.5 in of it | Applied in Vol. 2 and Vol. 3 plans |
| Pull-out newsprint tabloid insert | No inserts or loose pieces | A bound-in section on a Newsprint Gray tint with a 12 percent halftone-dot overlay, narrower margins and a mono-heavy type mix (v2 Addendum A) | Addendum A rule; no current issue has one |
| Perforated collectible (Sacko, Valentines, Bucks) | No perforation | Printed tear line on a right-hand page with a sacrificial verso (7.22) | Valentines and extras: Heather's open call |
| Back flap | No flaps | Move the collectible to an interior cut-out page | Same open call |
| Inside front and back cover content (masthead, colophon, quiz answers) | KDP prints nothing inside the covers | Colophon on page 1; quiz answers upside down on the last interior page | Applied in Vol. 3 plan |
| Fluorescent spot pink and neon inks | CMYK only, one profile | CMYK builds used at full strength in large fields; proof on paper where possible (07) | Builds in the hub |
| Spot gloss, die cuts, deckle edges, stickers | Not offered | Printed trompe-l'oeil: drawn perforations, drawn tape, drawn stamp edges | Addendum A rule |
| Heavier stock for a certificate or card | One interior stock | Same stock; design for it | Addendum A rule |
| Cream, kraft or manila paper | Premium color prints on white | Print the paper color as a flood that bleeds off every outside edge | Follows from the white stock |
| Navy or dark full-bleed pages facing each other | Show-through on this stock | Navy openers on single pages, never facing navy; QUIET dark fields as an 85 percent black tint | Addendum A rule; Vol. 3 plan |
| Type under 7 pt (some Money Pages lines are 6 pt) | Letterforms soften | 7 pt minimum | Hub minimum |
| Art that depends on the first or last spread lying flat | Perfect binding won't open flat there | Keep those spreads simple; nothing essential near the gutter | Addendum A rule |
| Spine text at about 120 pages | Spine is about 0.28 in | Solid pink field, volume number only, or no text | Addendum A rule |
| Signatures of 16 pages | Not a KDP constraint | Any even page count | KDP plan |

Proofing: Heather set Vol. 2's on-sale date with no printed proof, so KDP's online previewer is the last check for that issue. Whether a later issue gets a proof copy is in 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP).

---

## 9. How to plan a flatplan

The blank flatplan is in 11 Templates. This is the method. Work in this order.

1. **Start from the issue's lineup.** Take the pieces, tabs, registers and word counts from the issue's Issue Hub and manuscript. Drop anything marked cut.
2. **Place the fixed anchors first.** Page 1 (title and colophon), 2-3 and 4-5 (front spreads), 6-7 (contents), The Files divider, the map, the timeline, the Out Back opener, the sources section, the classifieds, the last spread and the last interior page. These stay in the same order every issue.
3. **Budget pages from words.** Use section 2.3. Write the planned page count next to each piece.
4. **Start every spread-sized piece on a left (even) page.** Every Vol. 3 feature starts on an even page, so its opener reads as a full spread. Use one-page pieces (Still Wrong, the opening letter, section openers, the Field Guide) to fill odd gaps.
5. **Set the register of every page.** Group QUIET features into runs. When a LOUD page must face a QUIET page, mark the LOUD page's art as the cyan + orange variant.
6. **Pair the spreads and check each pair against these limits:**
   - One wood-type plate per spread.
   - One diagonal headline per spread.
   - One pink focus per page.
   - No two navy pages facing each other.
   - No WOOD TYPE, COLLAGE, stamps or diagonal type on a QUIET page.
7. **Count the issue-wide limits:**
   - One full-bleed COLLAGE feature opener per issue (the biggest feature).
   - One isometric headline per volume.
   - One typographic pun per LOUD feature at most.
   - At least one QUIET feature.
   - One A-to-Z catalog section.
8. **Check KDP.** No gatefold, insert, flap, perforation or inside-cover content (section 8). Every cut-out has a sacrificial verso. Total pages even and within 116 to 132.
9. **Record each page** in the flatplan template with these columns: pages, piece, recipe, process, register, tab, inks and paper, image count, headline treatment, word count, status (including OPEN and PENDING).
10. **If it runs long,** cut in the order the Issue Hub lists. If it runs short, give space back to the longest features first.

---

## 10. Spread-level checklist

Run this on every spread before handoff. Every item must be a yes. 08 Quality Checklists holds the full issue-level list.

**Plan**
- [ ] The spread matches its flatplan row: piece, recipe, register and tab.
- [ ] The spread starts where planned (spread-sized pieces open on a left page).

**Register and color**
- [ ] One register per page. QUIET pages have no neon, no pink, no collage, no wood type, no stamps, no jokes.
- [ ] One pink focus per page. A LOUD page facing a QUIET page uses cyan + orange with no pink.
- [ ] Black plus two inks per image (except the cover, the tabloid poster and the back-cover colorway sheet).
- [ ] Period art is not recolored. Peach appears only on period cut-out skin.
- [ ] No navy page faces another navy page.

**Type and furniture**
- [ ] No generated lettering. All type set or hand-lettered. Nothing under 7 pt.
- [ ] One wood-type plate and one diagonal headline at most on this spread.
- [ ] Body text horizontal, on the grid, on a clean paper panel.
- [ ] Tab on the rail for tabbed pieces; corner bar and coda for recurring columns; folio present.

**Art and proof**
- [ ] One process per image. Illustrations and period images kept apart.
- [ ] No AI Dead figure shown as a face. No living person shown doing something the research can't prove.
- [ ] Every feature has its What We Can Prove / What We Can't box and the other side, with no stamps or badges.
- [ ] Every image has a credit line or is covered by the colophon credit (06).

**Money pages (if this spread is one)**
- [ ] Each item reads in under 15 seconds, one point per infographic, no block over 120 words.

**KDP**
- [ ] Nothing essential within 0.5 in of the gutter. Text 0.25 in inside the trim.
- [ ] Paper floods bleed 0.125 in off every outside edge.
- [ ] No fold-out, perforation or flap wording. Cut-outs have a sacrificial verso.

# 04 Grid and Typography
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Ines Kerner, Art Director, Typography and Grid
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review
**Who this is for:** Any designer, vendor or AI agent who lays out SIDE EYE magazine pages, from a single caption to a full issue.
**Use it when:** You set up a layout file, build master pages, create paragraph and character styles, place type or images on the grid, or check a page before handoff.
**Related:** 03 Page and Spread Standards · 02 Cover Standards · 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards) · 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP) · 08 Quality Checklists · 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs · 11 Templates · 13 Open Questions for Heather · Brand Standards hub (sections Visual identity, The magazine, Decision log) · SIDE EYE Design System v3: Neon Zine Print · SIDE EYE Design System v2 Core · SIDE EYE Design System v2 Addendum A: KDP Production Spec, 8.5 x 11

---

## 1. How to use this document

This document gives you the page geometry, the grid and the full type system for the printed magazine. Doc 03 tells you which page recipe to use. Doc 05 tells you how to make the art. Doc 07 owns press detail (color builds, ink limits, PDF export). This doc tells you where everything sits and how every word is set.

Where this doc and the Brand Standards hub disagree, the hub wins. Tell Heather. Where a rule waits on Heather, it says "until Heather decides" and the question is logged in 13 Open Questions for Heather.

### The rules at a glance

1. Build every page on the 12-column grid and the 13 pt baseline grid in this doc.
2. Body text is always horizontal, always on the grid, always on a clean paper panel.
3. Print with the free stand-in fonts until Heather records a license for the paid faces.
4. Never set type under 7 pt. Never set body text in caps.
5. No generated lettering anywhere. Every word is set in type or hand-lettered by a real hand and scanned.
6. One pink focus per page. Pink type is used only for that focus.
7. Headlines may run on every axis on LOUD openers only. One diagonal per spread at most. Never on QUIET pages.
8. Wood-type plates: two faces at most, one split fountain at most, set in layout.
9. Curly quotes and apostrophes everywhere. No em-dashes in headlines, decks, captions or Heather's copy.
10. Use the style names in section 15 exactly, so files hand off cleanly.

---

## 2. Page geometry

These numbers come from Addendum A and the hub's Print spec. Doc 07 owns how they print. This section owns how you lay out inside them.

| Item | Measure | Notes |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Trim | 8.5 × 11 in (216 × 279 mm) | Facing pages, perfect bound |
| Bleed | 0.125 in on top, bottom and outside edges | No bleed on the gutter (spine) edge |
| Document size with bleed | 8.625 × 11.25 in per page | Export single pages, not spreads (doc 07) |
| Margin, top | 0.5 in | Measured from the trim |
| Margin, bottom | 0.625 in | The extra room carries the folio |
| Margin, outside | 0.5 in | Holds the evidence rail band (section 5) |
| Margin, gutter (inside) | 0.375 in | Up to 150 pages. At 151 to 300 pages the gutter becomes 0.5 in and the grid must be rebuilt |
| Live area | 7.625 × 9.875 in | 549 × 711 pt |
| Text safety | 0.375 in inside the trim on top, bottom and outside | KDP's rule for books with bleed. Doc 07 explains why this replaces the 0.25 in in older docs |
| Gutter-clear zone | 0.5 in each side of the spine | Faces, headline letterforms and box-score figures never enter it |

### Set up the document

**InDesign:** File > New > Document. Width 8.5 in, height 11 in, Facing Pages on. Margins: top 0.5, bottom 0.625, inside 0.375, outside 0.5. Columns 12, gutter 0.125 in. Bleed: top 0.125, bottom 0.125, inside 0, outside 0.125. Slug 0.

**Affinity Publisher:** File > New > Print. Same page size, Facing Pages on, Start on right. Margins and bleed as above (inside bleed 0). Columns are set in Document > Guides Manager: 12 columns, gutter 0.125 in.

**Anything else** (Scribus, or an HTML and CSS build like Vol. 1): reproduce the same numbers exactly. Doc 07 lists which tools are accepted.

---

## 3. The 12-column grid

Every layout is built from groupings of 12 columns. Each column is 0.5208 in (37.5 pt). Each gutter is 0.125 in (9 pt).

### Spans

Width of a span of *n* columns = (n × 37.5 pt) + ((n − 1) × 9 pt).

| Columns | Width (in) | Width (pt) | Pixels wide at 300 dpi | Typical use |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: | :- |
| 1 | 0.5208 | 37.5 | 157 | Icons, rail art |
| 2 | 1.1667 | 84 | 350 | Wide rail, small spots |
| 3 | 1.8125 | 130.5 | 544 | Spot art, mug shots, Cast cameos |
| 4 | 2.4583 | 177 | 738 | LOUD text column, sidebar, Box Score |
| 5 | 3.1042 | 223.5 | 932 | Pull quote, wide-rail text column |
| 6 | 3.7500 | 270 | 1,125 | QUIET text column, half-page art, pull quote |
| 8 | 5.0417 | 363 | 1,513 | Two-thirds art, deck |
| 9 | 5.6875 | 409.5 | 1,707 | Deck, wide art |
| 12 | 7.6250 | 549 | 2,288 | Full-width headline or image |

**Correction to Addendum A.** Addendum A gives a 4-column text column as 2.4167 in. The grid math gives 2.4583 in (4 × 0.5208 + 3 × 0.125). Three columns at 2.4167 in would leave 0.125 in of the live area unused. Use 2.4583 in. (Logged in 13.)

Full-bleed art sizes stay as Addendum A gives them: a full single page needs 2,588 × 3,375 px. A full spread with outside bleed on both sides is 17.25 × 11.25 in, which is 5,175 × 3,375 px. Doc 07 owns resolution rules.

### Column positions

Columns are counted 1 to 12 from left to right on every page, so column 1 always starts at the left margin (this matches doc 09). Positions are the left edge of each column, measured from the page's left trim edge.

| Column | Left page (verso) | Right page (recto) |
| :-: | :-: | :-: |
| 1 | 0.5000 | 0.3750 |
| 2 | 1.1458 | 1.0208 |
| 3 | 1.7917 | 1.6667 |
| 4 | 2.4375 | 2.3125 |
| 5 | 3.0833 | 2.9583 |
| 6 | 3.7292 | 3.6042 |
| 7 | 4.3750 | 4.2500 |
| 8 | 5.0208 | 4.8958 |
| 9 | 5.6667 | 5.5417 |
| 10 | 6.3125 | 6.1875 |
| 11 | 6.9583 | 6.8333 |
| 12 | 7.6042 | 7.4792 |
| Live area right edge | 8.1250 | 8.0000 |

On a left page the outside margin is on the left. On a right page it is on the right.

---

## 4. The baseline grid

Body text, captions and sidebars all key to one 13 pt baseline grid. Display type (headlines, decks, plates, cover lines, drop caps) is exempt.

### Set it up

- **InDesign:** Preferences > Grids. Baseline Grid Start 0 pt, Relative To: Top Margin, Increment Every 13 pt. View Threshold 75%.
- **Affinity Publisher:** Document > Baseline Grid Manager. Use baseline grid on, Spacing 13 pt, Start position at the top margin.
- The first grid line sits on the top margin (0.5 in from the top trim). The first body baseline is 13 pt below it.
- A full column holds **54 body lines** (54 × 13 pt = 702 pt, inside the 711 pt live depth).

### What sits on it

| Text | Size/leading | How it keys to the grid |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Body, LOUD | 9.5/13 | Every line on the 13 pt grid |
| Section heads, table rows, box headers | various/13 or /26 | Every line on the grid |
| Pull quotes | 18/26 or 16/26 | Every line on every second grid line |
| Captions | 7.5/10 | First line only on the grid |
| Sidebars | 8.5/12 | First line only on the grid |
| Marginalia and footnotes | 7/9 | First line only on the grid |
| Body, QUIET | 10/15 | Every line on a 15 pt grid (see below) |
| Classifieds | 7/9 | Every line on a 9 pt frame grid (see below) |

**First line only.** Captions, sidebars and marginalia have leading that does not divide into 13 pt. Pin their first line to the grid and let the rest run at their own leading. InDesign: Paragraph Styles > Indents and Spacing > Align to Grid: First Line Only. Affinity: Paragraph > Baseline Grid > Align first line only.

**QUIET pages get a 15 pt grid.** QUIET body is 10/15 (Addendum A), which cannot sit on 13 pt. Give QUIET text frames their own grid: same start (the top margin), 15 pt increment. InDesign: Object > Text Frame Options > Baseline Options > Use Custom Baseline Grid. Affinity: Text Frame panel > Baseline Grid > Use custom. A full QUIET column holds **47 lines**. Build this into the QUIET master pages so nobody sets it by hand.

**Classifieds get a 9 pt grid.** Classifieds are 7 pt mono set dense, like a newspaper back page. Their frames use a custom 9 pt grid starting at the top of the frame.

**Display type is exempt, but the body below it is not.** After a headline, deck or opener art, the next body line restarts on the 13 pt grid. Columns that sit side by side must share baselines.

---

## 5. The evidence rail

The evidence rail is the narrow outside strip that makes a SIDE EYE page recognizable at a glance. Design System v2 and v3 keep it. It carries, top to bottom: the filing tab (or tabs), the running section title, and on rail-variant spreads the marginalia, rail icons and vertical timelines.

The rail's width is an open question. The Vol. 1 guide gave it a 22 mm outside margin. Addendum A later set the outside margin to 0.5 in and filled the whole live area with the 12 columns. KDP's 0.375 in text safety (doc 07) leaves a 0.125 in band between the safety line and the live area. **Follow Addendum A's margins until Heather decides.** That gives two versions of the rail:

### 5.1 Standard rail (every feature page)

| Part | Geometry | Type |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Rail band | The outside margin. Type stays between 0.375 in and 0.5 in from the outside trim | Only rotated labels live here |
| Filing tab | A solid color block, 1.0 in tall, from the bleed edge to the live-area edge (0.625 in wide including bleed). Top edge on the top margin line (0.5 in from top trim). Two or three tabs stack downward with 0.125 in between | Label in SE-Tab-Label, rotated, centered in the band |
| Running section title | Starts 0.25 in below the last tab, rotated, in the band | SE-Rail-Title |
| Folio | Bottom margin, outside corner. Baseline 0.42 in above the bottom trim, aligned flush to the outside edge of the live area | SE-Folio, format `FILE 002 / P. 042` |

- **Tab colors:** Tab Red, Tab Blue and the gray tab, by the issue's tab legend. On LOUD pages, the tab is the full ink and the label is reversed in cream or white. On QUIET pages, the tab is a 30 to 60% tint of its color and the label is K100. The gray tab ink is not in the hub palette yet; use Addendum A's Tab Gray (K48) until the hub lists one (doc 07).
- **Rotated label direction:** labels read bottom to top on left pages and top to bottom on right pages, so they always read toward the spine (Vol. 1 guide, still in force).
- **Leave the rail off** covers, full-bleed image pages and full-bleed openers. Keep the folio off those pages too.

### 5.2 Wide rail (only when the art-direction doc calls for it)

Some recipes need room on the rail for real content: rail icons (the Vol. 1 kit has dozens), a vertical timeline strip, a strip of dates, or 7 pt marginalia with the hosts' initials. When a spread's art-direction doc asks for any of these:

- Columns 11 and 12 (right page) or 1 and 2 (left page) become the rail: 1.1667 in wide.
- The remaining 10 columns carry the body as **2 text columns of 5 grid columns** (3.1042 in each). This applies to LOUD and QUIET alike.
- Marginalia set in SE-Marginalia align their first line with the body line they comment on.
- Rail icons are 1 or 2 columns wide and sit on the baseline grid.
- Note the page count cost: 2 × 5 columns holds about 15% less text per page than 3 × 4.

Do not invent a third rail. If a page needs more room than this, it is a sidebar (4 columns) instead.

---

## 6. Column patterns by register and page type

Pick the page recipe from doc 03 first. Then build it from these patterns.

| Page or element | Pattern | Width | Body style |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| LOUD feature body | 3 text columns of 4 grid columns | 2.4583 in each | SE-Body-LOUD 9.5/13 |
| QUIET feature body | 2 text columns of 6 grid columns | 3.75 in each | SE-Body-QUIET 10/15 |
| Wide-rail spread (either register) | Rail 2 columns + 2 text columns of 5 | 3.1042 in each | Same as register |
| Sidebar, Box Score, case notes | 4 grid columns | 2.4583 in | SE-Sidebar-Body 8.5/12 |
| What We Can Prove / What We Can't box | 12 columns, split 6 + 6 | 3.75 in each half | SE-Proof-Item 8.5/12 |
| Full-width headline or image | 12 grid columns | 7.625 in | |
| Deck | 6 to 9 grid columns | 3.75 to 5.6875 in | SE-Head-Deck 16/20 |
| Pull quote | 5 or 6 grid columns, hung toward the outside margin | Hang no farther than the 0.375 in safety line (0.125 in past the live area) | SE-PullQuote |
| Classifieds | 3 text columns of 4 grid columns, 0.75 pt K100 rules in the gutters | 2.4583 in each | SE-Classified-Body 7/9 |
| Sources section | 2 text columns of 6, or the existing narrow center column (see 10.7) | 3.75 in | SE-Sources-Entry 7.5/10 |
| Colophon | 2 text columns of 6 | 3.75 in | SE-Colophon 7.5/10 |
| Contents | Poster layout per doc 03; entries on 4- or 6-column measures | | SE-Contents-Entry |
| AI Dead letter and reply | 2 text columns of 6 (letter left, reply right) | 3.75 in | SE-Body-LOUD |
| A-to-Z catalog entries | Drop cap and vertical label in 1 to 2 columns, entry text in the next 4 | | SE-Body-LOUD |

**QUIET pages get their white space from the type, not the margins.** Don't shrink the master margins or drop to one column on your own. The space comes from the 10/15 leading, a deeper sink on opener pages (start the text at least a quarter of the way down), no art in the outside margin, and no stacked furniture. If an art-direction doc asks for a single QUIET column, use 8 grid columns centered on the page and log it in the handoff note.

---

## 7. Images and captions on the grid

### Placing images

- Image frames start and end on column edges. Never stop a frame in the middle of a gutter.
- The top and bottom edges of a frame sit on baseline grid lines. Snap them; don't eyeball them.
- Standard sizes: spot (2 to 4 columns), half page (6 columns, any depth, or 12 columns, half depth), full page in the live area (12 columns, full depth), full bleed (to the bleed edge on top, bottom and outside), spread.
- **Full-bleed and spread art** goes to the bleed edge on the outside, top and bottom. Nothing bleeds into the gutter except art that crosses it on purpose.
- **Art across the gutter:** only where the middle of the image is unimportant, with 0.25 in of duplicated image on each side (Addendum A, doc 07). Faces, headline letters and box-score figures stay 0.5 in clear of the spine.
- **Body text never sits on art.** It sits on a clean paper panel (SE-Obj-Paper-Panel) pasted over the collage. The panel edge sits at least one gutter (0.125 in) outside the text.
- Illustrations never sit inside a period image, and period images never sit inside an illustration (hub).

### Placing captions

- Every image gets a caption in SE-Caption, starting with its figure number: `FIG. 014.` (Vol. 1 guide, still in force).
- Put the caption under its image. Its first line sits on the first grid line at least 6 pt below the image's bottom edge.
- Caption width: the image width, up to 4 columns. Under a wider image, keep the caption 4 columns wide and align it to the image's outside edge.
- Side captions are allowed in the column next to the image. Align the caption's first line with the image's top edge on the grid.
- **Captions for full-bleed art** go on a paper panel inside the live area, or on the facing page keyed by figure number: `FIG. 014 (OPPOSITE).` Keep them inside the 0.375 in safety.
- **Cast captions** (the playbill cameos) use SE-Caption-Cast: the prop name in caps, then one line. Example: `THE BOOT. Arthur Wellesley, later Duke of Wellington.`
- **Credit lines** use SE-Caption-Credit as the caption's last line when doc 06 says the image needs one on the page. The magazine's single Midjourney credit lives in the front matter and colophon (hub).
- QUIET captions: plain facts, no jokes (hub, v3).

---

## 8. The type system

### 8.1 Faces and roles

| Role | Print face (paid) | Free stand-in (use now) | Weights you need | Where it goes |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| Masthead | The logo file | The logo file | n/a | Never typeset. Place the outlined logo from the logo pack (hub, Logo) |
| Headlines | Druk Condensed | Anton | Druk Condensed Heavy / Anton Regular | Feature headlines, openers, A-to-Z caps, drop caps, box-score figures, vertical theme title |
| Section heads | Druk | Big Shoulders Display | Black | Section heads inside features, sidebar titles, Classifieds head |
| Wood-type plates | Hamilton Wood Type faces, licensed (for example HWT Van Lanen, HWT American Chromatic) | Alfa Slab One, Rye, Ultra | Pick two per plate | Title plates, dividers, Box Score, contents, Case File and promo posters, back cover |
| Body | Tiempos Text | Source Serif 4 | Regular, Italic, Semibold | Body, decks, pull quotes, sidebars, the issue thesis line |
| Captions, labels, folios, tables | GT America Mono | IBM Plex Mono | Regular, Bold | Captions, case headers, tabs, rail, folios, classifieds, sources, colophon, tables |
| Cover lines | A licensed typewriter mono | Courier Prime | Regular, Bold | Cover lines on torn strips, photographed and pasted |
| Margin notes | Real hand lettering, scanned | None | n/a | Margin notes, arrows, circled words, signatures. Never a "handwriting" font |

**About the mono.** The hub allows IBM Plex Mono or Space Mono. Use IBM Plex Mono. Design System v3 names it, Vol. 1 printed with it, and the logo's tagline is drawn in it. Use one mono per issue, never both.

**About Source Serif 4.** If your copy has optical sizes, use the text size (SmText) for 8 to 11 pt, Caption below 8 pt and Subhead or Display at 16 pt and up. The variable font does this on its own.

### 8.2 Font licensing rule

**Use the free stand-ins until licenses are recorded.** The hub records no print license for Druk, Tiempos Text, GT America Mono or the Hamilton wood-type faces.

- All stand-ins are free for commercial print and PDF embedding: Anton, Big Shoulders Display, Source Serif 4, IBM Plex Mono, Alfa Slab One, Rye and Courier Prime under the SIL Open Font License, and Ultra under the Apache License 2.0. Keep each license file with the fonts (doc 09).
- **Switch a whole issue at once.** Never mix a paid face and its stand-in in one issue (Druk on one page, Anton on the next).
- When a license is recorded, the paid face takes the stand-in's style slot. Style names don't change (section 15).
- **Never use a face that isn't in the table above** without Heather's yes (hub, What always needs Heather's yes). That includes Archivo Black, which appears in the Vol. 1 files, and any system font that slips in as a fallback (doc 07).
- Never use a typeface cut from a named print shop's own blocks, or any shop's or magazine's lettering (v3).
- Buying fonts is spending money, so it needs Heather's yes. Cost notes are in section 18.

---

## 9. Paragraph styles

All sizes in points. Leading follows the slash. Tracking is in 1/1000 em (InDesign and Affinity use the same unit). "Grid" says how the style keys to the baseline grid. K100 means 100% black only, never rich black, and set to overprint (doc 07).

Stand-ins in brackets. When a paid face is licensed, swap the face and keep everything else.

### 9.1 Base styles (every other style is based on one of these)

| Style | Face | Size/leading | Tracking | Case | Align | Color | Grid |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| SE-00-Base-Serif | Tiempos Text Regular [Source Serif 4] | 9.5/13 | 0 | Sentence | Justify, last line left | K100 | All lines |
| SE-00-Base-Mono | GT America Mono Regular [IBM Plex Mono] | 7.5/10 | 0 | As set | Flush left | K100 | First line |
| SE-00-Base-Display | Druk Condensed Heavy [Anton] | 72/65 | -5 | All caps | Flush left | K100 | None |

Set the language to English: USA in all three.

No hyphenation. One headline size per page |
