## 10. Character styles and special elements

### 10.1 Character styles

| Style | What it does | Use |
| :- | :- | :- |
| SE-Char-DropCap | Druk Condensed Heavy [Anton], K100 | The drop cap. Pink only if it is the page's one focus. Black on QUIET |
| SE-Char-Italic | Tiempos Text Italic | Titles of works, foreign words, emphasis |
| SE-Char-Semibold | Tiempos Text Semibold | Rare emphasis. Never a whole sentence |
| SE-Char-Pink | Color Side Eye Pink only | The one pink focus. Using a style makes stray pink easy to find |
| SE-Char-Reversed | Color cream (or paper white) | Type on dark fields |
| SE-Char-FigNo | Mono Bold, caps | `FIG. 014.` |
| SE-Char-CastName | Mono Bold, caps | `THE BOOT.` |
| SE-Char-HostAside | Mono Bold, K100 | `[H.C.]` and `[E.G.]` asides (hub) |
| SE-Char-ClassLead | Mono Bold, caps | `FOR SALE`, `LOST`, `PERSONAL` |
| SE-Char-Tabular | Tabular lining figures | Tables, contents page numbers, box scores |
| SE-Char-NoBreak | No break | Names, dates, measurements, `5 ft 7 in`, `April 1, 1821` |
| SE-Char-Mono-Inline | Mono Regular at the size of the surrounding text minus 1 pt | IDs and codes inside body text |
| SE-Char-Foreign | Tiempos Italic, language set to the phrase's language | Latin and French phrases, checked by the fact-checker |

### 10.2 Drop caps

- Size: 4 lines deep, about 52 pt (Addendum A). Let the drop-cap setting size it; don't set the point size by hand.
- Face: Druk Condensed Heavy [Anton], all caps.
- Use one at the start of each feature's body, and one per letter in A-to-Z catalogs.
- A-to-Z format (v2): a big Druk capital, the entry name set vertically beneath it in SE-AtoZ-Label, and a one-line subtitle in SE-AtoZ-Subtitle beside it.
- QUIET pages may use drop caps. They are black only.

### 10.3 What We Can Prove / What We Can't box

Every feature has one (hub). It comes after the twist and before the other side.

- 12 columns wide, split into two 6-column halves. Left half: WHAT WE CAN PROVE. Right half: WHAT WE CAN'T.
- Each half opens with SE-Box-Header. Items in SE-Proof-Item.
- Frame: SE-Obj-Proof-Box, a 0.75 pt K100 stroke, 9 pt inset, on a clean paper panel.
- Rating words may appear as plain words in the text when the fact check uses them. Never as a stamp, badge or graphic.
- LOUD: black only unless the box is the page's one pink focus. QUIET: black and gray only.

### 10.4 FILE NO. stamp

- The stamp frame is art (a generated or drawn rubber-stamp impression with a blank center). The wording is always set in type inside it: `SIDE EYE` on one line, `FILE NO. 002` on the next, in SE-Stamp-FileNo.
- Set SIDE EYE here as plain capitals. Never make it look like the logo (hub, Logo).
- Rotate the stamp 4° to 8° counterclockwise (SE-Obj-Stamp). Apply the rubber-stamp texture as a mask over live type. Keep an unmasked copy on a hidden layer for edits.
- Pink only when the stamp is the page's one pink focus (covers may carry more color, doc 02). Otherwise K100.
- Never place a stamp over body text. Never on QUIET pages.
- Keep the wording at 9 pt or larger, because the texture eats letter edges.

### 10.5 Corner bars and coda marks

- **Corner bar:** a solid K100 bar for each recurring column (Letters to the AI Dead, Still Wrong, Apocrypha Index, Ranked, Field Guide, Report Card, Last Words). Its position and label face are an open question. Design System v2 puts it around the top outer corner with the column name in Druk. Addendum A, which says it wins over v2, puts the corner bar in the bottom margin with the folio, in 7 pt mono caps tracked +80. **Follow Addendum A until Heather decides:** bottom outer corner, label in SE-CornerBar-Label. Doc 03 shows the placement.
- **Coda marks:** every recurring column ends with its mark (the eye for most columns, the pixel icon for AI Dead letters, a tiny tombstone for Still Wrong). Place it as an inline graphic 1 em tall, one en space after the last word.

### 10.6 Classifieds

- 7 pt mono in three narrow columns, 0.75 pt K100 rules in the gutters, no images (hub, The Money Pages).
- No block over 120 words. Each ad readable in under 15 seconds.
- Head: CLASSIFIEDS (or the issue's title, such as PERSONALS) in SE-Head-Section.
- The classifieds are the last thing before the final spread.

### 10.7 Sources section

- One entry per claim, numbered to match the fact-check log one to one (hub).
- Grouped by feature, each group under SE-Sources-Head.
- Layout: the Vol. 1 guide sets it as a narrow center column in mono, black only. That look is tied to the section's current name, which is under review in the hub's Decision log. Keep the narrow center column until Heather rules on the name. If the name changes, use 2 text columns of 6.
- QUIET register: black only, no pink, no jokes.

### 10.8 Colophon and corrections

- **Colophon:** SE-Colophon. It carries the one Midjourney credit, image credits and the influence credit (hub). It is a QUIET page.
- **Corrections:** printed every issue, in the same size type as the original error (hub). A correction to body copy is set at 9.5 pt. A correction to a caption is 7.5 pt. A correction to a headline is set at the headline's size in its face. This is the only place a style's size may be overridden.

### 10.9 Host asides

Print uses short bracketed asides: `[H.C.]` for Heather and `[E.G.]` for Edwin (hub). Set the initials in SE-Char-HostAside, K100. The Vol. 1 guide set them in pink, but the one-pink-focus rule (Decision log) is newer, so they stay black.

---

## 11. Minimum sizes and legibility

| Rule | Minimum | Source |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Any type, anywhere, interior or cover | 7 pt | Hub, Addendum A |
| Reversed type (cream or white out of a dark field), regular weight | 8 pt | House setting |
| Reversed type, bold weight | 7 pt | House setting |
| Reversed type on rich black | 9 pt, sturdy weight | Doc 07 |
| Type in pink or any other color build | 9 pt | House setting: two-ink type blurs if the plates shift |
| Type in a tint of black (gray) | 9 pt, and never under 60% | House setting |
| Type in rich black or a four-color build | 18 pt | Doc 07 |
| Rules and lines | 0.75 pt | Doc 07 (KDP) |
| Text on a photo or collage | Display type only, on a clear area or solid field | Hub, v3 |

- Older documents call for 6 pt mono lines (a back cover rule and a disclaimer band in The Money Pages, whose volume labels predate the November 24, 2026 renumbering, and microtext on the SIDE EYE Bucks spread). Set them at 7 pt. The hub's minimum wins.
- Vol. 1 printed folios and tab labels below 7 pt. Don't copy the Vol. 1 files as a model for small type.
- Small black type overprints (doc 07). Never set text in rich black.

---

## 12. Type on every axis (LOUD openers)

This comes from the zines (v2, v3). It is one of the strongest signals in the book, so it is rationed.

- **Where:** LOUD feature openers, feature title pages and covers only. Never on QUIET pages. Never on interior body pages.
- **What may rotate:** headlines, section titles on openers, the theme title, rail labels and drop-cap labels.
- **Axes:** horizontal, vertical (90°) and diagonal. **One diagonal headline per spread, at most.**
- **What never rotates:** body text, decks, captions, sidebars, pull quotes. They stay horizontal and on the grid.
- Headlines may overlap images and each other. They never overlap body text, captions or another element's text.
- A vertical display headline may read up or down (the Napoleon opener runs NOT SHORT. up the right page). Small rotated labels follow the rail rule: toward the spine.
- Rotated and diagonal type stays inside the 0.375 in safety and 0.5 in clear of the gutter.
- **Across a spread:** a headline that spans both pages must break at the gutter, with each side at least 0.5 in from the spine. Split between words, never inside a word.
- **One typographic pun per LOUD feature** (v2), built by hand in layout.
- **One isometric headline per volume** (v2), built in vector.
- **One headline size per page.** Get contrast from scale jumps, not from mixing display faces. The exception is a WOOD TYPE plate, which mixes two wood faces by design.
- Use object styles SE-Obj-Rotate-90 and SE-Obj-Rotate-270 for vertical type, so rotations are exact and easy to find.

---

## 13. Wood-type plates

Plates are feature title plates, section dividers, the Box Score, the contents page, Case File posters, promo posters and the back cover (v3).

1. **Set every letter in layout from real wood-type digital fonts.** Never generated. Midjourney makes only the ink textures, split-fountain fields and woodcut cuts (doc 05).
2. **Two faces per plate, at most.** Mix a condensed face with a fat or extended one. Free pairs: Ultra with Alfa Slab One, or Rye with Alfa Slab One. Use Rye for one or two words only, because it is very ornamental.
3. **All caps.** Stacked lines, each a different size, each set to fill the plate's width. The one key word is biggest. On a Box Score, "VS." is biggest.
4. **One split fountain per plate, at most.** Pink to orange to yellow, or cyan to pink. Apply it in layout by placing the split-fountain field inside the letters. Split fountains appear only on WOOD TYPE plates.
5. **Ornaments:** heavy rules, stars, pointing hands, arrows and dingbat borders, from the house ornament library. No ornaments with letters in them.
6. **Ink texture:** add uneven inking, wood grain and edge squash in layout as a texture mask over the type. The type underneath stays live.
7. **Stock:** cream or kraft.
8. **One plate per spread.** Never on QUIET pages.
9. **Keep live type.** When the split fountain or texture needs outlined letters, outline a copy. Keep the live text on a hidden layer named `LIVE TYPE` so the words can be edited and checked.
10. Ink totals for split fountains are checked in doc 07.

---

## 14. Cover lines and hand lettering

### Cover lines

- Four cover lines at most, short, in typewriter mono on torn paper strips (v3, hub). Doc 02 owns the cover layout and sizes.
- **Preferred method:** set each line in SE-Cover-Line, print it on plain paper, tear the strip, photograph or scan it at 600 dpi, and place it.
- If you can't print and tear, set the line in live type over a torn-paper strip texture (a scan, or generated texture with no letters on it). The words must still be real type. Note the method in your handoff note.
- Never generate a cover line. Never use a "typewriter" effect font in place of a real typewriter mono.

### Hand lettering

- Margin notes, arrows, circled words and signatures are real handwriting, scanned. Never a handwriting font (hub, v3).
- Scan at 600 dpi grayscale (Addendum A), clean it up, and place it as one ink: K100, or pink if it is the page's one focus.
- Signatures are scanned from the hosts' own handwriting. Never draw or fake one.
- On QUIET pages, hand lettering is black only and never a joke.

---

## 15. Naming styles so files hand off cleanly

Use the style names in sections 9 and 10 exactly as written, including capitals and hyphens. A vendor or agent who opens your file should find the same names in the same order.

- **Prefix:** every style starts with `SE-`. Paragraph styles, character styles (`SE-Char-`) and object styles (`SE-Obj-`) each live in their own group.
- **Groups (InDesign style groups, Affinity style folders):** `00 Base`, `Heads`, `Body`, `Boxes`, `Captions and Rail`, `Back of Book`, `Cover`, `Characters`, `Objects`.
- **Based on:** every paragraph style is based on one of the three base styles. Change the face in a base style and the issue follows.
- **No local overrides.** Before handoff, clear overrides on body text (InDesign: Paragraph Styles panel > Clear Overrides; Affinity: Text Styles > Reset Formatting). The only allowed overrides are the corrections size (10.8) and manual tracking within -10 to +10 on a single line to fix a widow.
- **Object styles:** SE-Obj-Paper-Panel, SE-Obj-Caption, SE-Obj-Tab, SE-Obj-Rail-Title, SE-Obj-PullQuote, SE-Obj-Proof-Box, SE-Obj-Stamp, SE-Obj-Rotate-90, SE-Obj-Rotate-270, SE-Obj-Image-Grid, SE-Obj-Image-Bleed.
- **Swatches:** name them exactly as the hub names the inks (Toner Black, Side Eye Pink, Pale Cyan and so on). Doc 07 gives the builds.
- **Master pages (InDesign parent pages, Affinity master pages):** `A-LOUD` (3 × 4 columns), `B-QUIET` (2 × 6, 15 pt frame grid), `C-WideRail` (rail + 2 × 5), `D-Bleed` (no rail, no folio), `E-Classifieds` (3 × 4, 9 pt frame grid).
- **Affinity Publisher** opens IDML. Style names survive the trip only if they match exactly, so don't rename on import.
- **HTML and CSS builds** (Vol. 1 was built this way): name each class after its style in lower case, for example `se-body-loud`, `se-caption`, `se-char-figno`. CSS has no true baseline grid, so check every page against the grid by eye before export.
- Doc 09 owns file names, folders and packaging. Doc 11 holds the template files once they are built.

---

## 16. Hyphenation and justification

### Body text (SE-Body-LOUD, SE-Body-QUIET)

| Setting | Value |
| :- | :- |
| Alignment | Justify with last line aligned left |
| Composer | InDesign: Adobe Paragraph Composer. Affinity: default justification |
| Word spacing | Minimum 85%, desired 100%, maximum 133% |
| Letter spacing | Minimum -2%, desired 0%, maximum +2% |
| Glyph scaling | 100% (no scaling) |
| Hyphenation | On. Words of 6 letters or more. At least 3 letters before and 3 after the break |
| Hyphen limit | 2 hyphens in a row, at most |
| Don't hyphenate | Capitalized words, the last word of a paragraph, across a column or page break |
| Keep options | Keep first 2 and last 2 lines together. No single word alone on a paragraph's last line. No single line of a paragraph alone at the top of a column |

Turn on InDesign's Highlight H&J Violations (Preferences > Composition) while you set. Fix loose lines by asking the editor for a small rewrite, or by manual tracking within -10 to +10 on one line. Never stretch a whole paragraph.

### Everything else

- **Ragged right, no hyphenation:** headlines, decks, pull quotes, captions, credit lines, cover lines, classifieds, case headers, labels.
- **Ragged right, light hyphenation** (words of 7 letters or more, 1 hyphen in a row at most): sidebars, proof boxes, marginalia, sources.
- Never break a name, a date, a number with its unit, or a figure number across lines. Use SE-Char-NoBreak.

---

## 17. Numerals and punctuation

### Numerals

- **Body text:** lining figures, proportional (the font's default).
- **Tables, box scores, contents page numbers, folios:** tabular lining figures, numbers right-aligned in columns.
- **Druk and Anton:** lining only.
- **Fractions:** use the font's true fraction glyphs (5 ft 4½ in). Never type 1/2 in body text.
- **Ranges:** en dash, closed up, no spaces: `1769–1821`, `pp. 2–3`, `60–96 pt`.
- **Thousands:** commas: `20,000 emus`.
- **Money:** in the original currency with its symbol (£200). Check that every face you use has the symbol.
- **Ordinals:** never superscript.
- **Dates:** American style, `April 1, 1821` (hub).
- Set numbers as the edited copy gives them. Don't change words to figures or figures to words.

### Punctuation

- **Quotes and apostrophes:** curly in every face, including the mono and the typewriter mono. Turn on typographer's quotes. Check apostrophes at the start of a word: `’90s`, not `‘90s`.
- **Em-dashes:** the hub's no em-dash rule covers Heather's copy. The Vol. 1 guide (still in force) bans them in display type and captions. Whether the rule covers print design copy such as the classifieds and money pages is an open question in the hub's Decision log. **Until Heather decides:** no em-dashes in headlines, decks, captions, pull quotes or any of Heather's copy. In the classifieds and money pages, set the copy exactly as the editor supplies it. Never add or remove a dash yourself. If supplied copy has an em-dash, set the true character (Unicode 2014), spaced as supplied, and flag it in your handoff note.
- **En dash:** ranges only. **Hyphen:** compound words only. Never two hyphens in place of a dash.
- **Ellipsis:** the single ellipsis character.
- **Spaces:** one space after a full stop. No double spaces anywhere. Search for them before handoff.
- **Measurements:** written out (`5 ft 7 in`) with no-break spaces. Use true prime marks (′ ″) only if the copy uses symbols.
- **Brackets:** host asides only, as `[H.C.]` and `[E.G.]`.
- **Ampersand:** only inside a proper name.
- **Special letters:** the ʻokina in Liliʻuokalani, ʻIolani and Aloha ʻOe is its own character (Unicode 02BB). Never use an apostrophe in its place. Check accented letters (Résumé, Mémorial) in every face. If a glyph is missing, your layout tool will quietly swap in a fallback font. Fix it before export (doc 07).
- **Case:** all caps only in display faces and mono labels. Never set body text in caps. Decks and captions are sentence case.
- **Non-English text:** Latin and French phrases are checked by the fact-checker before setting. Japanese text only when a fluent reader writes and checks it. Never generated.

---

## 18. Font licensing: status and cost notes

| Face | Status | Cost note (checked November 24, 2026; confirm before buying) |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Druk Condensed (headlines), Druk (section heads) | No recorded license | One reseller lists a desktop license for a single Druk Condensed style at about $60. The foundry's direct price was not confirmed. The issue needs at least Druk Condensed Heavy, plus a Druk weight for section heads |
| Tiempos Text | No recorded license | No public price found. The foundry quotes desktop licenses by number of users. The issue needs Regular, Italic and Semibold |
| GT America Mono | No recorded license | No public price found. The foundry sells one-time desktop licenses priced by user count, in US dollars. The issue needs Regular and Bold |
| Hamilton Wood Type faces | No recorded license | Sold by several resellers. For example, HWT Van Lanen is listed from about $68 for two fonts. Hamilton Wood Type faces are also listed in the Adobe Fonts library, which Adobe says is cleared for commercial print and PDF embedding for Creative Cloud subscribers. Check which faces are included before relying on it |
| Typewriter mono for cover lines | None chosen | Courier Prime is free and fits the role |
| All free stand-ins | Free for commercial print | SIL Open Font License (Ultra: Apache 2.0). No cost |

The rule stays the same until Heather records a license: print with the free stand-ins. Buying fonts needs Heather's yes. When a license is bought, record the face, the license type, the number of users and who holds it in the issue's font list (doc 09). Paid font files never go into a package that leaves the team (doc 07, doc 09).

---

## 19. Where screen type touches print

The web, the brand hub and the internal tools use Anton for titles, IBM Plex Sans for reading text and IBM Plex Mono for labels (hub, Internal tools). Only three points touch print:

- **The logo** is drawn from Anton and IBM Plex Mono SemiBold, and the files are already outlined. Never retype the wordmark or tagline in any font, print or screen (hub, Logo).
- **IBM Plex Sans is screen-only.** Never use it in the magazine.
- **The Case File poster** is a WOOD TYPE plate that also serves as the YouTube thumbnail background (v3). Build it to the plate rules in section 13, even when its first use is on screen.

The darker screen pink and blue for small text on screens are screen values only. Print uses the CMYK builds in doc 07.

---

## 20. Open questions (summary)

These are logged in 13 Open Questions for Heather. Until she decides, follow the interim rule in the section named.

1. **Evidence rail width** (section 5). Interim: Addendum A margins, standard rail in the margin band, wide-rail variant on request.
2. **Paid font licenses** (sections 8.2 and 18). Interim: free stand-ins.
3. **Em-dashes in print design copy** (section 17). Interim: none in display type, decks, captions or Heather's copy; classifieds set as supplied.
4. **Corner bar position and label face** (section 10.5). Interim: Addendum A.
5. **Archivo Black,** used in the Vol. 1 files but not in the hub (section 8.2). Interim: don't use it.

---

## 21. Checklist before handoff

- [ ] Document set up to section 2: trim, facing pages, margins, bleed (none on the gutter), 12 columns with 0.125 in gutters.
- [ ] Baseline grid 13 pt from the top margin. QUIET frames on a 15 pt grid. Classifieds on a 9 pt grid.
- [ ] Body lines in neighboring columns share baselines.
- [ ] Every image frame starts and ends on column edges and grid lines.
- [ ] Nothing essential within 0.5 in of the gutter. All text at least 0.375 in inside the trim.
- [ ] Rail on every feature page except bleed pages and covers. Tabs, running title and folio in place.
- [ ] Only the faces in section 8.1. All free stand-ins unless a license is recorded. No fallback fonts.
- [ ] No paid face and its stand-in in the same issue.
- [ ] Every text block uses an SE- style. No stray overrides.
- [ ] No type under 7 pt. Pink and tinted type 9 pt or larger. Rules 0.75 pt or heavier.
- [ ] One pink focus per page. Pink type only through SE-Char-Pink.
- [ ] Headlines in caps, one headline size per page, one diagonal per spread at most, nothing rotated on QUIET pages.
- [ ] Wood-type plates: two faces at most, one split fountain at most, live type kept on a hidden layer.
- [ ] No generated lettering anywhere: plates, stamps, cover lines and labels are all set in type.
- [ ] Curly quotes and apostrophes. No double spaces. No em-dashes in headlines, decks, captions or Heather's copy.
- [ ] Every image has a FIG. caption. Every feature has a What We Can Prove / What We Can't box.
- [ ] Corrections set in the same size as the original error.

# 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards)
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Hollis Fenn, Art Director, Illustration and Midjourney
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review

**Who this is for:** Any art director, illustrator, designer, AI agent or outside vendor who makes or reviews a picture for SIDE EYE magazine: covers, openers, spot art, column art, plates, posters and the art in magazine promos.

**Use it when:** You plan the art for an issue or a feature, write a Midjourney prompt, pick a generation, write an art-direction doc, or check art before layout.

**Related:** 00 Start Here: How to Use the SIDE EYE Art Direction Standards · 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files · 02 Cover Standards · 03 Page and Spread Standards · 04 Grid and Typography · 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits · 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP) · 08 Quality Checklists · 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs · 11 Templates (issue brief, flatplan, art-direction doc, handoff note) · 13 Open Questions for Heather · Source docs: SIDE EYE Design System v3: Neon Zine Print · the Brand Standards hub (Visual identity) · Napoleon Art Direction

---

## 1. How to use this document

- Design System v3: Neon Zine Print is the visual source of truth. The Brand Standards hub is the source of truth for color, type and brand rules, and its Decision log says which rule wins. This document turns both into working rules for art. If this document and the hub ever disagree, follow the hub and log the conflict.
- Each rule here is meant to be checked. "Do" rules use the imperative. Numbers come from the sources.
- Where a rule says "until Heather decides," the question is open. It is listed in 13 Open Questions for Heather. Follow the stated default until it is settled.
- Rights, credits and archival sourcing live in 06. Ink builds, proofs and preflight live in 07. Type sizes and the grid live in 04. This document covers what the picture is and how it gets made.

---

## 2. The house look in one paragraph

SIDE EYE looks like a 1990s Japanese art zine that ran into an American letterpress show-poster shop: cut-and-paste collage, photocopy grain, neon silkscreen color, stacked wood type, split-fountain ink blends and carved-block illustration. Every image must look printed, pasted or carved by hand, never rendered on a screen. That means flat ink, coarse halftone dots, speckle, slight misregistration, paper you can see, and no glow, chrome, gradient or glossy 3D light. Each picture makes one visual joke with a point, uses black plus two inks, and puts pink on the one thing the reader should see first. The influences are studied for methods and materials only. SIDE EYE never borrows anyone's name, logo, layouts or artwork.

---

## 3. The five processes

Pick one process per image. Never mix RELIEF and COLLAGE inside one frame, except by the HYBRID recipe.

| Process | Use it for | Signature in one line |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| RELIEF | Spot art, visual jokes, column art (Letters to the AI Dead, Still Wrong, Apocrypha, Ranked), cast cameos, icons, timeline pins, the back-cover colorway sheet | Three-ink relief print: thick wobbly black line, pink punchline, pale cyan fills, coarse halftone, bare cream paper, one joke |
| COLLAGE | Covers, section openers, the tabloid poster, the issue's one full-bleed opener | Six pasted layers with a photoreal cut-out hero breaking the frame |
| WOOD TYPE | Feature title plates, dividers, Box Score fight cards, contents, Case File posters, promo posters, the back cover | Stacked wood-type caps set in layout, rules and stars, one split fountain, a small woodcut cut, cream or kraft |
| HYBRID | Feature openers about a real historical person | Period figure cut out, reduced to black line, halftone and peach skin; RELIEF hands and props make the argument |
| NEWSPRINT | QUIET pages, Out Back, tabloid interiors | Gray newsprint, soft black, halftone photos |

### 3.1 RELIEF (the house illustration)

**Use when:** the page needs a joke, an icon, a column portrait or any small picture. If you are unsure, it is RELIEF.

**Signature traits:**
- Line: thick, wobbly black outline, as if carved into a block.
- Inks: black plus two. Default is fluorescent pink plus pale cyan (see section 4 for variants).
- Shading: coarse halftone dots only.
- Texture: speckled ink, small spatters, slight misregistration so color slips past the line.
- Paper: warm cream, left bare as the background.
- Composition: one visual joke, bold shapes, lots of empty paper.
- People are replaced by props, hands, hats and objects in the wrong place (a bicorne in a teacup, a skull in a powdered wig).

**Never:**
- Never use a gradient, glow, drop shadow, 3D render look or photo shading.
- Never fill the frame. Leave empty paper.
- Never put two jokes in one picture.
- Never draw a detail thinner than about 1/4 inch at final size on spot art. It will fill in.
- Never let the style reference's subject leak in (a skull where no skull belongs).

### 3.2 COLLAGE (covers and big openers)

**Use when:** it is the cover, a section opener, the tabloid poster, or the one full-bleed opener the issue allows.

**Signature traits (build in this order, modeled on the Vol. 1 emu cover):**

| Layer | What goes in it |
| :-- | :-- |
| 1. Ground | Cream or kraft paper, scraps of old print at the edges |
| 2. Scene | One photoreal scene, colored in flat silkscreen fields |
| 3. Color fields | Flat neon blocks meeting in hard edges (the masthead field on a cover) |
| 4. Marks | Loose painted neon brush border, a thin wobbly hand-drawn black ink frame, real scanned margin notes |
| 5. Hero | A photoreal cut-out subject with exaggerated, oversized eyes, breaking the frame from one side |
| 6. Cover lines | Set in type on torn paper strips, photographed and pasted on |

**Never:**
- Never put more than one surreal idea in one collage.
- Never generate the hero and the background together. Generate them apart and composite.
- Never let the collage look digitally clean. Paper edges lift and cast small shadows.
- Never generate the masthead, cover lines or margin notes. They are set or hand-lettered.
- Cover-specific rules (hero side by volume, masthead air, four cover lines at most) live in 02 Cover Standards.

### 3.3 WOOD TYPE (plates and posters)

**Use when:** the page needs a title plate, a divider, a fight card, a contents page, a poster or the back cover.

**Signature traits:**
- Letters: condensed and extended wood-type faces, all caps, two faces per plate at most, set in layout from real digital wood-type fonts (see 04).
- Hierarchy: stacked lines, each a different size, set to fill the width. The one key word is biggest.
- Ornaments: heavy rules, stars, pointing hands, arrows, dingbat borders.
- Ink: uneven coverage with wood grain, ink squash at letter edges, slight bite into the paper.
- Split fountain: two or three inks blended across one run (pink to orange to yellow, or cyan to pink). One per plate, maximum.
- Picture: a small carved woodcut "cut" (a RELIEF idea flattened to one or two inks) sitting inside the type.
- Stock: cream or kraft.

**Midjourney makes only:** ink textures, split-fountain fields, ornament sheets and woodcut cuts. Everything with letters is built in layout.

**Never:**
- Never generate a letter, number or word.
- Never use more than one split fountain per plate, or a split fountain outside WOOD TYPE.
- Never use more than one plate per spread.
- Never use WOOD TYPE on a QUIET page.
- Never copy a real shop's poster, carved block, layout or typefaces cut from its blocks.

### 3.4 HYBRID (the historical-person opener)

**Use when:** a feature opener is built around a real historical person. Follow the Napoleon opener.

**Signature traits:**
- Cut the period figure out of a public-domain print or painting (rights per 06).
- Reduce the figure to black line and halftone, with one flat peach (#F4B393) for skin and nothing else.
- Build the scene around it in RELIEF: the hands, props and objects that make the argument. In the Napoleon opener, a chain of hands identified only by their cuffs passes the man along.
- Set a flat Deep Navy or Warm Cream field. On navy, anything that must read (strings, threads, rules) goes pale cyan or pink, never black.
- Caption the composite as set out in 06.

**Never:**
- Never recolor the period figure pink or neon.
- Never use peach anywhere except cut-out skin.
- Never place an illustration inside the period image, or the period image inside an illustration that imitates a document.
- Never use HYBRID for a living person or for anyone who is not real.

**Pending variant (until Heather decides):** the Art Briefs v1 for the Mating Issue shows real portraits as old photographic prints taped to the page (white border, slight curl, paste shadow, no outline, no peach) and calls this "Heather's update." No Heather decision confirming it is on record in the hub. Use the v3 cut-out method above unless the issue's brief or Heather approves the taped-print method for that issue (open question).

### 3.5 NEWSPRINT (QUIET pages and Out Back)

**Use when:** the page is QUIET (deaths, atrocities, real suffering), on Out Back, or inside the tabloid.

**Signature traits:**
- Gray newsprint stock, soft black ink, coarse halftone, generous empty space, wide margins.
- QUIET: black, gray and one muted tab tint only.
- Out Back: one flat pink shape per page, maximum.

**Never:**
- Never put neon, collage or a joke on a QUIET page, in the picture or the caption.
- Never illustrate the people harmed in a QUIET story. Use real archival images, or nothing.
- On QUIET pages, prefer real archival photographs. A generated NEWSPRINT image is allowed only as a plain, joke-free picture of an object or place that could not be mistaken for a record, captioned as an illustration. v3's rules also say "QUIET features get no illustrations," so treat any generated image on a QUIET page as needing Heather's yes until she decides (open question).

---

## 4. Inks and the black-plus-two rule

**The rule:** each image uses black plus two inks, three inks maximum. Covers and the tabloid poster may use more. Pink marks the one thing to notice first. If two things in one image are pink, one is wrong.

**RELIEF ink variants (swap the second ink for one feature or column that needs its own identity):**

| Variant | Use for | Prompt words |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Pink + pale cyan | House default | "fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink" |
| Pink + acid yellow | Money stories | "fluorescent pink and acid yellow ink" |
| Pink + neon orange | Fire, war, emergencies | "fluorescent pink and neon orange ink" |
| Cyan + orange (no pink) | A LOUD page that faces a QUIET page | "pale cyan and neon orange ink" and add `pink` to the `--no` list |

Keep one variant for a whole feature or column. Do not change variants image to image inside a feature.

**Inks and papers (screen values; print builds and proofing are in 07):** Toner Black #1A1A1A, Side Eye Pink #FF48B0, Pale Cyan #7FD6E8, Neon Orange #FF7A1A, Acid Yellow #F2E81E, Tab Red #E5484D, Tab Blue #0078BF, Deep Navy #1C2B5A, Peach #F4B393 (period skin only). Papers: Warm Cream #F2EBDD, Kraft #C9A27A, Newsprint Gray #D9D6CF. See the Brand Standards hub, Visual identity > Color.

**Print reality:** KDP prints CMYK only, so there is no true fluorescent. Every neon is a bright CMYK build and prints duller than the screen. Flag every neon-heavy image for proof review per 07.

**Period art:** never recolor it pink or neon. It gets grayscale plus coarse halftone, or full color on openers only.

---

## 5. The joke rule and what pink marks

**The joke rule.** Every RELIEF image (and every HYBRID scene) is one visual joke with a point. The point is the article's argument made visible. The joke comes from objects in the wrong place, at the wrong size, or in the wrong hands.

Write the joke in one sentence before you write a prompt: "He thought he was playing a king. He was one piece on someone else's board." If you cannot write that sentence, you do not have a picture yet.

**The joke must be true on the evidence.** See section 13.

**What pink marks.**
- Pink goes on the punchline object: the thing the reader should see first (the hat, the coin, the padlock, the one envelope).
- Name the pink object in the prompt: "the [object] printed in fluorescent pink." Do not leave pink to chance.
- Print keeps one pink focus per page (Brand Standards hub, Decision log). Pink in video can be used freely; that does not carry over to print.
- Repeat sets (an icon row, a cameo strip, the AI Dead windows) have so far given each item its own pink prop. Keep doing that, with pink playing the same role in every item of the set, until Heather decides whether a matched set counts as one focus (open question).
- The cover and the tabloid poster may carry more pink (masthead field, brush border), but the image still has one pink focus inside it.
- The cyan-plus-orange variant has no pink. Its focus is carried by size and placement.

---

## 6. People in pictures

These rules come from the Brand Standards hub (Visual identity > People in pictures, and the Decision log). They replace older rules.

1. **Historical figures: generated likenesses are allowed** (Heather's decision). Draw them as caricature in a house process (RELIEF, HYBRID, woodcut) or use real period art. Never make a generated likeness look like a photograph or a period record.
   - Name only documented clothing and objects for the time and role ("First Consul in 1800, dark coat").
   - The joke is the action, never the face or body. Reject takes that exaggerate the face or body.
   - Show the figure doing only what the evidence rates Proven (section 13).
   - Always write a no-likeness version too (props, hands, cuffs) as the fallback if Midjourney refuses the name, the likeness comes out off-model, or review reads it as mockery beyond the proven act.
   - You may attach a cleared public-domain portrait as an image reference to steer a likeness (rights per 06). Never attach anyone's modern artwork.
2. **Living people: sourced public facts only.** Never show a living person doing anything we cannot prove. The hub does not say whether a generated likeness of a living person is allowed. Until Heather decides, do not generate one: show living people through props, hands or a cleared real photo, and only doing what is documented (open question).
3. **Letters to the AI Dead figures are always skulls wearing one defining prop** (a wig, a wreath, a wimple, a bicorne). Never a portrait, even though historical likenesses are now allowed elsewhere.
4. **People harmed in a QUIET story are never illustrated.** Individual issues may add named exclusions (for example, a person or community who gets no generated likeness at all). The issue brief lists them; they bind every image in the issue.
5. **Old rule, now superseded:** the side-eye-art-direction skill and older docs (Addendum B, the Napoleon Art Direction) say "no faces or likenesses of real people, living or dead." That rule is out of date for historical figures. Follow points 1 to 4.

---

## 7. How to write a concept for each page

Write a short brief for every page or spread that carries art, before any prompt. Use these fields (they match the art-direction doc in section 14 and the template in 11):

| Field | What to write | Example |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Page purpose | What the page must do for the reader, in one line | "Open the feature and state who held the power." |
| The one image idea | The visual joke in one or two sentences | "His hat is one small chess piece facing a row of ships, a teapot and a moneybag." |
| Thesis link | The article claim it argues, and its proof rating | "Bankers financed both sides (Proven)." |
| Process | One of the five | RELIEF |
| Inks and pink | The variant, and the one thing pink marks | Pink + cyan; pink = the hat |
| Paper or field | Cream, kraft, newsprint, navy | Warm Cream |
| Register and tab | LOUD or QUIET; filing tab | LOUD; Lost the Story |
| Composition | Where the image sits, what space it leaves for type, gutter clearance | "Full spread; hat on the right page, at least 0.5 in from the gutter; empty top left for the headline." |
| Type notes | What is set in layout over or near it | "Headline stacked, one word vertical." |
| Image source | Generated, period art, composite, or built in layout | Generated |
| Rights and people check | Any real person; any sensitivity rule; any rights line needed | "No faces of the bankers; hands by cuffs." |
| Prompts | A and B, copy-ready | See section 9 |

Rules for the concept:
- One idea per image. If the brief needs "and" to describe the joke, cut one half.
- Every illustration ties to a claim in the article. No decoration-only art on LOUD feature pages.
- Check props for accuracy before prompting. Readers will check (Nelson's diamond plume, Wellington's hat worn front to back, nobody gets an eye patch).
- Plan open space for type in the image (say where, in the prompt).

---

## 8. The Midjourney v8.2 prompt standard

### 8.1 Set up once per session

- Use Midjourney on the web, model version 8.2.
- Turn Personalization off for SIDE EYE jobs, so every image matches the house look. Do not use `--p` or moodboards.
- Upload the two style references once (section 8.6) and keep their image links handy.
- Midjourney names downloaded files after the account and the prompt. Rename every file you keep per 09 before it goes anywhere shared.

### 8.2 Order of a prompt

Write the parts in this order:

1. The subject and the joke, in plain concrete words.
2. The pink object: "the [object] printed in fluorescent pink."
3. The process words from the template (exactly as written).
4. Paper, field and empty space: where the plain area for type goes ("top-left corner plain").
5. Finishing words: "evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper" (RELIEF, HYBRID) or "paper edges lifting with soft shadows" (COLLAGE).
6. Parameters, in this order: `--ar`, `--s`, `--c`, `--sref`, `--sw`, `--hd`, `--no ...`, then the required ending `--raw --v 8.2`.

Keep prompts concrete. Say what is there, not what is absent, in the description. Avoid words with two meanings (a "bill" can be money or a beak; write "a paper banknote"). Give counts only when the count matters, and keep them small.

### 8.3 Required ending and parameters

**Every prompt ends with `--raw --v 8.2`.**

Design System v3, Addendum B and the skill write raw mode as `--style raw`. Midjourney's current parameter list documents raw mode as `--raw`, and the hardened Midjourney Prompt Workbook v2 already uses `--raw`. Write `--raw`. If you are handed an older prompt with `--style raw`, change it to `--raw`; it is the same switch.

| Parameter | SIDE EYE use |
| :-- | :-- |
| `--raw` | Always. Turns off Midjourney's default beautifying so the print look holds. |
| `--v 8.2` | Always. |
| `--ar W:H` | Always. Pick from section 8.4. Whole numbers only (17:22, never 8.5:11). |
| `--s` | Always. Values in section 8.5. Range 0 to 1000; default 100. |
| `--c` | `--c 0` on Prompt A, `--c 10` on Prompt B. Keeps A steady and lets B explore. |
| `--sref` and `--sw` | RELIEF and HYBRID: RELIEF reference at `--sw 60`. COLLAGE backgrounds: COLLAGE reference at `--sw 30`. Nothing else uses a style reference. See 8.6. |
| `--hd` | Renders at higher resolution (about 2048 px on the long side instead of 1024). Add it to covers, full-page art, spread art, and any image that will print wider than about 3.4 in (1024 px at 300 dpi). Small spots under that size may skip it. |
| `--no` | A short tail of things to keep out (text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, the style reference's own subject, glow, gradient). It backs up the positive description; it never replaces it (8.8). |
| `--seed` | Optional. Use it to make a matching before-and-after pair from one image. A seed does not guarantee an identical result. |

Print resolution check: art must reach 300 dpi at its final placed size. A full-bleed page needs 2,588 × 3,375 px. Even `--hd` output will often need upscaling for full-bleed use; check the pixel size against the placed size and record it in the art log (section 12). The prepress check is in 07.

### 8.4 Aspect ratios by page type (8.5 × 11 trim)

| Use | `--ar` |
| :-- | :-- |
| Cover background, full page | 17:22 |
| Spread, horizontal half page | 17:11 |
| Square spot, icon, cameo, AI Dead portrait, woodcut cut | 1:1 |
| Tall spot | 2:3 |
| Part-page portrait spot | 4:5 |
| Banner, timeline strip | 21:9 |
| Cover hero cut-out (head and neck) | 1:2 |
| Split-fountain field | 3:1 |
| Ornament sheet | 3:2 |
| Promo video, poster for screen, YouTube plate | 16:9 |
| Vertical promo (Shorts, Reels) | 9:16 |

Full-bleed art must cover the bleed (0.125 in past trim on outside edges). Keep anything that matters inside the 0.25 in safety area and clear of the gutter (spec in 04 and 07).

### 8.5 Stylize values

| Process or job | `--s` | Notes |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| RELIEF (all: spots, icons, cameos, AI Dead) | 150 | Raise to 250 (the v3 template value) if results look too plain. |
| HYBRID scene | 150 | Same range as RELIEF. |
| COLLAGE background | 200 | Raise to 250 if the collage reads too clean. |
| COLLAGE hero cut-out | 100 | Photoreal subject; higher values gloss it up. |
| Woodcut cut | 150 | |
| Split-fountain field, ornament sheet | 100 | |
| NEWSPRINT, LOUD (Out Back, tabloid) | 80 | |
| NEWSPRINT, QUIET | 60 | |

High stylize is what makes images glossy and over-lit. When in doubt, go lower.

### 8.6 Style references

SIDE EYE uses exactly two style references. Both are SIDE EYE's own approved images.

| Reference | What it is | Use with | Weight |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| RELIEF reference | The powdered-wig skull print, one of the Vol. 1 Letters to the AI Dead skull prints (the others are the laurel wreath and the wimple) | RELIEF, AI Dead, cast cameos, HYBRID scenes | `--sw 60` |
| COLLAGE reference | The approved Vol. 1 emu cover draft (the emu peeking in from the right) | COLLAGE backgrounds only | `--sw 30` |

**How to find them.** Search Drive by title, not by link. The emu cover draft is the Vol. 1 cover draft image in the Side Eye Issue 1 images folder (file name begins "cover_issue1_draft"). The skull prints are PNG files whose names start with the Midjourney prompt text for a three-ink relief print of a human skull; open them and use the one wearing the powdered wig. The skull print currently sits outside the Side Eye folders; v3's production checklist asks for both references to be saved as the house style references, and 09 names the folder they belong in.

**How to attach them.** In Midjourney on the web, drag the image into the prompt bar and drop it on Style Reference, or upload it once and paste its image link where the template says `[RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL]` or `[COLLAGE STYLE REFERENCE URL]`. That token is the only thing you edit in a finished prompt.

**Rules:**
- Use only these two images, or a future reference Heather approves and the Decision log records. Never upload anyone else's artwork, a magazine page, a poster or a stock image as a style reference or image reference.
- Keep weights at 60 and 30. Higher weights drag skulls and emus into new pictures.
- Put the reference's subject in the `--no` tail: `skull` with the RELIEF reference (except on AI Dead portraits, which want a skull), `emu` with the COLLAGE reference.
- Addendum B removed all style references. Design System v3 brought them back, and v3 wins (Decision log, 2026-09-29).
- Run hero cut-outs, woodcut cuts, textures, ornaments and NEWSPRINT with no style reference.

### 8.7 Two prompts per image: A and B

- Write two prompts for every generated image. **A** is the safer, clearer read. **B** is the bolder joke.
- Both use the same process, inks and aspect ratio, so either one fits the layout.
- A gets `--c 0`; B gets `--c 10`.
- For any image that names a real historical person, A is the no-likeness version (props, hands, cuffs) and B may be the caricature. Keep A ready as the fallback.
- Pick one per image (section 12). Do not mix A and B halves in one image.

### 8.8 The "completely blank" rule

Midjourney writes fake letters on anything that usually carries text. Prevent it in the description, not only in `--no`.

- Describe every paper, ledger, letter, envelope, sign, label, card, tag, book spine, banknote, poster, screen and plaque as "completely blank" or "plain and unmarked."
- Never ask for a stamp, label, sign, poster or brass plate with words. Ask for a blank one; set the words in layout.
- Keep the `--no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark` tail on every prompt as a backup.
- No generated lettering anywhere, in any language. If letters appear, regenerate. Never paint over generated letters and keep the image.

### 8.9 No names of artists, studios, shops or magazines

- Never name an artist, illustrator, studio, print shop, poster shop, zine, magazine or brand in a prompt. Describe materials and methods only ("condensed wood-type letters, split-fountain ink, carved woodcut").
- This includes "in the style of" any person or publication, living or dead.
- The influences appear only in the colophon credit (see 06), never as implied affiliation.
- Historical figures may be named in a prompt as the subject of a caricature (section 6). That is a subject, not a style.

### 8.10 Generate period props alone, then composite

- If a period prop drifts (a bicorne turns into a pirate hat), describe it plainly by shape and material ("a black felt hat with two stiff points, worn sideways, with a small round cockade") and generate it alone on plain paper.
- Composite it into the scene in layout.
- Do the same for any object the scene keeps getting wrong: a plunger, a specific tool, a uniform detail.

### 8.11 When a prompt misbehaves

| Problem | Fix |
| :-- | :-- |
| Writing appears | Add "completely blank" to the object that grew text. Regenerate. |
| Faces appear on the owners of hands | Add "only hands and sleeves visible, no people." Add `faces` to `--no`. Crop to the hands if needed. |
| Neon turns into glow or chrome | Add "flat ink, printed on paper, no glow, no gradient." Keep `glow, gradient` in `--no`. Lower `--s`. |
| Collage looks digitally clean | Raise the COLLAGE `--sw` a little (stay at or under 60), add "paper edges lifting with soft shadows." |
| Skulls or emus leak in | Put `skull` or `emu` in `--no`. Lower `--sw` by 10. |
| Period prop drifts | Describe it plainly and generate it alone (8.10). |
| Hands or animals come out wrong (extra fingers, wrong legs) | Simplify the pose, reduce how many hands, regenerate. Reject any image with bad anatomy. |
| Wrong count of objects | Use a small number (three or fewer) or drop the count. |
| Anachronism (wrong century object) | Name the period and material ("a 1790s wooden counter"). |
| Pink lands on the wrong thing | Name the pink object once, early: "the [object] printed in fluorescent pink." Remove other color words for that object. |
| Too busy | Cut the prompt down. Add "wide empty paper margins, plain background." |
| Midjourney refuses a real person's name | Run the no-likeness Prompt A (the fallback). |

---

## 9. Copy-ready prompt templates

Replace only the words in [brackets]. Keep everything else as written. Each template is Prompt A. To make Prompt B, change the joke to the bolder version and change `--c 0` to `--c 10`.

### 9.1 RELIEF (spot art, visual jokes, icons)

```
flat three-ink relief print of [subject doing the visual joke], the [one punchline object] printed in fluorescent pink, bold simple shapes, thick uneven carved black outline, fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink, coarse halftone dot shading, speckled ink texture, slight misregistration, printed on bare warm cream paper, wide empty paper margins, [where the plain space for type goes], evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper --ar [1:1 / 2:3 / 4:5 / 17:11] --s 150 --c 0 --sref [RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 60 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, skull, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

- Add `--hd` before `--no` for full-page or spread art, or any image printing wider than 3.4 in.
- For an ink variant, swap "fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink" for the words in section 4.
- If hands appear, add "only hands and sleeves visible" to the description and `faces` to `--no`.

### 9.2 Letters to the AI Dead portrait

```
flat three-ink relief print of a human skull wearing [the figure's one defining prop], the [prop] printed in fluorescent pink, centered inside a plain circular cameo frame, bold simple shapes, thick uneven carved black outline, fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink, coarse halftone dot shading, speckled ink texture, slight misregistration, printed on bare warm cream paper, plain background, evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper --ar 1:1 --s 150 --c 0 --sref [RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 60 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

- Do not put `skull` in `--no` here.
- One defining prop only. A second small object is allowed only if the prop alone cannot identify the figure.
- Run all portraits for one issue in one sitting and pick them as a set.

### 9.3 Cast cameo (a real person told through a prop)

```
flat three-ink relief print of [the prop that stands for the person], the [prop] printed in fluorescent pink, centered inside a plain circular cameo frame, bold simple shapes, thick uneven carved black outline, fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink, coarse halftone dot shading, speckled ink texture, slight misregistration, printed on bare warm cream paper, plain background, evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper --ar 1:1 --s 150 --c 0 --sref [RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 60 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, skull, faces, people, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

- Prompt B: add "held by a hand in [the cuff that identifies the group]" after the prop, add "only hands and sleeves visible," and change `--c 0` to `--c 10`.
- The caption (set in layout, playbill style) names the person. The art never does.

### 9.4 COLLAGE background (cover or big opener, no hero)

```
photorealistic mixed-media collage magazine cover background, [one surreal scene], colored in flat silkscreen fields, flat saturated blue sky, a flat fluorescent pink color block across the top third for the masthead, torn newsprint and found ephemera pasted along the edges, a loose hand-painted fluorescent pink brush border, a thin wobbly hand-drawn black ink frame, coarse halftone grain, paper edges lifting with soft shadows, open space on the [left/right] for a figure, evenly lit --ar 17:22 --s 200 --c 0 --sref [COLLAGE STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 30 --hd --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, animals, people, emu, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

- For an opener, write "magazine opener background" instead of "magazine cover background," replace the masthead block with "a flat [ink] color block where the headline will sit," and use `--ar 17:11` for a spread.
- Put the open space on the side the hero will enter from (cover side rules in 02).

### 9.5 COLLAGE hero (to composite in layout)

```
photorealistic [animal or object] head and neck leaning in from the [right/left] side of the frame as if peeking around a corner, exaggerated oversized round eyes swiveled toward the viewer, [detailed feathers / fur / surface texture], soft even daylight, isolated on a plain white background --ar 1:2 --s 100 --c 0 --hd --no text, full body --raw --v 8.2
```

- No style reference on heroes.
- Photoreal heroes are animals or objects. A real historical person on a cover is period art or a caricature in a house process, never a photoreal generated face (section 6).
- Cut the hero out in layout and let it break the ink frame.

### 9.6 Split-fountain field (WOOD TYPE)

```
letterpress ink print texture, a split-fountain blend of fluorescent pink into neon orange into acid yellow across a wide band, uneven ink coverage with wood grain showing through, slight ink squash at the edges, printed on warm cream stock, completely blank, no letters, evenly lit --ar 3:1 --s 100 --c 0 --no text, letters, words, numbers --raw --v 8.2
```

- For the second approved blend, write "pale cyan into fluorescent pink."
- For kraft stock, write "printed on kraft paper."
- One split fountain per plate.

### 9.7 Woodcut cut (the small picture inside a plate or poster)

```
small carved woodcut printing block image of [subject], two-ink letterpress print in black and fluorescent pink, the [object] printed in fluorescent pink, bold chunky carved lines, visible wood grain in the ink, slight misregistration, on [kraft paper / warm cream stock], plain background --ar 1:1 --s 150 --c 0 --no text, letters, numbers, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

### 9.8 Ornament sheet

```
set of carved wood-type printer's ornaments, stars, heavy rules, pointing hands, arrows and small borders, black letterpress ink with uneven coverage on cream stock, isolated, evenly spaced, evenly lit --ar 3:2 --s 100 --c 0 --no text, letters, numbers --raw --v 8.2
```

- Build an ornament library once per year and reuse it. Cut ornaments apart in layout.

### 9.9 HYBRID scene (around a cut-out period figure)

```
flat three-ink relief print of [the joke: hands, props, objects], the [punchline object] printed in fluorescent pink, leaving an empty space in the [center / left / right third] for a cut-out figure, bold simple shapes, thick uneven carved black outline, fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink on a flat deep navy field, coarse halftone dot shading, speckled ink texture, slight misregistration, only hands and sleeves visible, evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper --ar 17:11 --s 150 --c 0 --sref [RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 60 --hd --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, faces, people, skull, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

- For a cream field, write "on bare warm cream paper" instead of "on a flat deep navy field."
- On navy, strings, threads and rules go pale cyan or pink.
- Composite the archival figure in layout (black line, halftone, peach skin only).

**HYBRID figure, generated likeness (Prompt B option for a historical person):**

```
a caricature of [historical figure, role, year], in [documented clothing only], [doing the documented action], drawn only in black line and coarse halftone with flat peach skin, the [punchline object] printed in fluorescent pink, flat three-ink relief print with a thick uneven carved black outline, fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink, coarse halftone dots, slight misregistration, on a flat deep navy field, evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper --ar 17:11 --s 150 --c 10 --sref [RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 60 --hd --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, skull, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

- Always pair it with the no-likeness Prompt A (9.9 scene) as the fallback.
- Whether peach skin reads well on the navy field is an open question in the Workbook. Until Heather decides, prefer the cream field for generated caricatures and keep navy for cut-out period figures.

### 9.10 NEWSPRINT

LOUD (Out Back, tabloid interiors):

```
[one plain subject, concrete and specific], printed on gray tabloid newsprint, soft black ink, coarse halftone, generous empty space, evenly lit --ar [ratio] --s 80 --c 0 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, glow --raw --v 8.2
```

QUIET (only where section 3.5 allows a generated image):

```
[one plain object or empty place, no people], printed on gray tabloid newsprint, soft black ink, coarse halftone, generous empty space, plain and quiet, no collage, no bright color --ar [ratio] --s 60 --c 0 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, pink, color, people --raw --v 8.2
```

- Out Back adds its one flat pink shape in layout, never in the prompt.

---

## 10. Using a generator other than Midjourney

If a vendor uses another image generator, the same standard applies. Translate it; do not loosen it.

- Describe materials and methods in the same words as the templates (relief print, coarse halftone, misregistration, warm cream paper, flat silkscreen fields, paper edges lifting).
- Keep every ban: no artist, studio, shop or magazine names; no generated lettering; "completely blank" on every paper and sign; no glow or gradient; the people rules in section 6.
- Match the aspect ratios and the print resolution (300 dpi at final size).
- Use only SIDE EYE's own two approved images as style or image references, if the tool takes references. Never upload outside artwork.
- Switch off any feature that adds a personal or "house" style from the tool.
- Write A and B prompts and log them the same way (section 12), with the tool's name and version in place of "Midjourney v8.2."
- Credit the actual tool used (06 has the credit format).
- Any tool that cannot produce flat ink without glow or gloss, or keeps writing letters, is not fit for SIDE EYE art. Tell the art director before using it.

---

## 11. AI video in magazine promos

These rules come from the Brand Standards hub (Visual identity > AI-generated video, Decision log 2026-11-24). They apply to every promo for the magazine: trailers, social clips, Shorts, animated covers.

- Generated footage illustrates an idea. It never stands in for real footage of a real person.
- Use it for scenes, objects and moments: a magazine page turning, a letter being written, a newspaper being read, a fire in a fireplace, trees being cleared.
- Never show a real person, living or historical, acting on screen in generated footage that a viewer could take for real film of them.
- Hands, objects, settings, silhouettes and clearly stylized art are fine. Generated stills and stylized likenesses of historical figures stay allowed.
- **The pause test:** pause any frame. If a viewer could believe it is real archival or news footage of that person, restyle it or cut it.
- Keep the house look in motion: animate RELIEF, COLLAGE or WOOD TYPE art, never a glossy rendered scene.
- No generated lettering in video either. Titles and type are added in the edit.
- The Midjourney Prompt Workbook notes Midjourney video clips run 5 seconds (extendable to 21) at 720p. Check the current limits before planning a shot around them.
- Video thumbnails and end screens are not covered here; see the Brand Standards hub, The YouTube show > Packaging.

---

## 12. Generating, selecting and approving

### 12.1 How many to generate

- Lock the look first. For each feature, generate its opener (or its most important image) first, pick it, then run the rest to match.
- Run Prompt A and Prompt B once each. That gives two grids of four.
- If neither grid works, apply one fix from section 8.11 and rerun. Stop after four grids for one image. If it still fails, rewrite the concept or switch to a period image, and note it in the art-direction doc's open items.
- Run sets (icons, cameos, AI Dead portraits, Apocrypha markers) in one sitting with the same settings, and pick them together so weight and color match.

### 12.2 How to pick

Pick the image that passes every line below. Zoom to 200% before you decide.

- [ ] The joke reads at final printed size in under three seconds.
- [ ] Pink sits on the punchline object and nowhere else.
- [ ] Black plus two inks (count them). No glow, gradient, chrome or gloss.
- [ ] No stray letters, numbers or symbols anywhere, including on paper edges and spines.
- [ ] Hands, animals and counts are correct. No extra fingers or legs.
- [ ] Props are accurate for the period.
- [ ] No style-reference leakage (stray skulls, emus).
- [ ] Paper and print texture visible; it looks printed, pasted or carved.
- [ ] The open space for type is where the layout needs it.
- [ ] It matches the rest of its set.
- [ ] It implies nothing beyond Proven (section 13).
- [ ] It passes the people rules (section 6).

Upscale the pick inside the generator, download it, and rename it per 09.

### 12.3 What to log

Log every image in the issue's art log (11 Templates, section 4 gives the full header row; 09 sets file names and folders). One row per image slot:

| Field | What to record |
| :-- | :-- |
| Image ID | The same ID as the rights log, for example SE-V02-I07 (06 and 09 set the pattern) |
| Slot ID and page | The art-direction doc code and the page or spread |
| Process and inks | For example, RELIEF, pink + cyan, pink = the hat |
| Prompt as run | The exact text pasted, with the reference token left as `[RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL]`, not the link |
| A or B | Which prompt won, and any fix applied |
| Generator and version | Midjourney v8.2 (or the other tool) |
| Seed | If the tool shows one; otherwise "not shown" |
| Job ID | The generator's job ID for the picked image, and its grid position (1 to 4) |
| Date | The date generated |
| File name | The renamed file, per 09 |
| Pixel size and 300 dpi check | Pixels, placed size, pass or fail |
| Edits in layout | Cut-out, composite, color shift to ink builds, cleanup |
| Source and license | "Generated," or the period source and rights line per 06 |
| Credit line | Per 06 |
| Status | To run · Running · Picked · Needs rerun · Placed · Skip |
| Approved by and date | Filled only by Heather or the person she names |

### 12.4 Who approves

- The art director picks. Picking sets status to Picked.
- AI agents and vendors never mark art Approved or Published (Brand Standards hub, What always needs Heather's yes).
- Heather approves the cover, every opener, every image of a real person, and anything flagged in the art-direction doc's open items. She may approve the rest in one pass on the art-direction doc.
- For covers and for any opener about a living person, have an independent reviewer (a second agent or person) check the plan against section 15 and the Scorecard before it goes to Heather.

---

## 13. The evidence rule

Art makes arguments, so art is held to the same proof standard as the text.

- **Art never implies a claim rated below Proven.** Use the research scale: only Documented (script label PROVEN) claims may be drawn as fact. Likely, Contested, Unsupported and Refuted claims are never drawn as if true.
- Check each image against the article's Scorecard and its Must NOT claim list. That list binds the art as much as the words.
- Watch for implied claims: a hand pulling strings implies control; a leash implies ownership; a figure first in line or larger than the rest implies leadership; a crown implies rule. If the control is only Likely, no strings, leash or hand above him.
- Never show a myth happening as if it were true. Apocrypha and Still Wrong art shows the prop, not the myth.
- **Illustrations carry the argument; period images carry the proof.** An illustration never imitates a period print, map, letter, newspaper or document, and never sits inside one. A period image never sits inside an illustration made to look like a document (a fake scroll, ledger page or certificate frame around it).
- Generated images are illustrations, never records. Real records come from archives (06).
- If no Scorecard came with the article, draw only what the fact-checked text states plainly, and log the gap as an open item.

---

## 14. The art-direction doc (the deliverable)

Every feature or issue gets one art-direction doc, modeled on the Napoleon Art Direction. The fill-in template is in 11. Save it where 09 says, as a Google Doc, with a local Markdown copy.

| Section | What it holds |
| :-- | :-- |
| 1. Concept | The one visual idea for the feature, tied to the thesis in a sentence. The recurring stand-ins (Napoleon is his hat; the people who used him are hands, known by their cuffs). The joke, and what pink marks. Placement (volume, tab, case header). |
| 2. Print style table | For each image or page: process, inks and the pink object, paper or field, type faces. |
| 3. Flatplan | Page by page: spread number, page recipe (03), images by code, headline treatment, register (LOUD or QUIET), filing tab. Note rhythm (where illustrations lead and where evidence leads) and any page that must stay a single page (a centered image that would fall in the gutter). |
| 4. Prompts | A and B for every generated image, copy-ready in code blocks, with the style reference and weight named. Image codes: C cast, I illustration, H historical, plus the page-brief fields from section 7. Include the misbehavior fixes specific to this feature (the prop that drifts). |
| 5. Historical images | Each with artist, title, date, holding institution, license or public-domain line, and a link (format and rights rules in 06). Mark each "checked" with the date, or "not yet cleared." |
| 6. Built in layout (not generated) | A table of everything made by hand or in layout: lettering, wood-type plates, cover lines, captions, charts, timelines, maps, composites, ornaments, cut lines. |
| 7. Credit lines | One for every image: generated, composite and historical (formats in 06). |
| 8. Open items | Checks before print: rights still open, proof review of neon builds, prop accuracy, expert reads for sensitive spreads, anything the art could imply that the Scorecard cannot prove, and every "until Heather decides" item that touches this feature. |
| 9. Sources | Titles of the docs and research it was built on. |

Optional section, when sources disagree: a short table of "where the skill, v3 and older docs disagree, and what this doc followed." Never settle a disagreement yourself; follow the hub and list it.

---

## 15. Before you deliver: art checklist

Run this on every prompt and every page plan. 08 Quality Checklists holds the full issue checklist.

- [ ] One process per image; HYBRID only for real historical people.
- [ ] Black plus two inks (covers and tabloid excepted); one pink focus, named in the prompt.
- [ ] Every prompt ends `--raw --v 8.2`; `--ar`, `--s` and `--c` set; `--hd` where 8.3 requires it.
- [ ] Style reference only on RELIEF, HYBRID (`--sw 60`) and COLLAGE backgrounds (`--sw 30`), using SIDE EYE's own approved images only.
- [ ] No artist, studio, shop or magazine names in any prompt.
- [ ] No generated lettering; every paper and sign described as "completely blank."
- [ ] People rules met: historical likenesses as caricature only, with a no-likeness fallback; no generated likeness of a living person until Heather decides; AI Dead are skulls with one prop; no one harmed in a QUIET story is illustrated.
- [ ] Period art never recolored; peach only on cut-out skin.
- [ ] QUIET pages: no neon, no collage, no jokes.
- [ ] The joke is true on the evidence; nothing implies a claim below Proven.
- [ ] No illustration imitates or sits inside a period document.
- [ ] Every image logged (prompt, seed, job ID, date, file name, pixel size).
- [ ] Every image has a credit line and, for period art, a cleared rights line (06).
- [ ] Neon-heavy art flagged for proof review (07).
- [ ] Any AI video passes the pause test.

---

## 16. Skill rules that are out of date

The installed side-eye-art-direction skill is the tool most agents will load. Until it is updated, this document wins where they differ.

| The skill says | Current rule | Why |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| "No faces or likenesses of real people, living or dead"; checklist "No generated faces of real people" | Generated likenesses of historical figures are allowed as caricature or period art; living people follow sourced-public-facts-only | Heather's decision, recorded in the hub's Decision log and Skills section |
| Every prompt ends `--style raw --v 8.2` | Every prompt ends `--raw --v 8.2` | Midjourney's current parameter list documents `--raw`; the Workbook already uses it |
| RELIEF template at `--s 250`, no `--c`, no `--no` tail on RELIEF | RELIEF at `--s 150` (250 if too plain), `--c 0` A and `--c 10` B, `--no` tail with `skull` | Workbook v8.2 hardening (style-reference leakage, stylize range) |
| Silent on Personalization | Personalization off | Workbook settings |
| Silent on AI video | Pause test applies to promo video | Decision log, 2026-11-24 |
| Per-image "generated with Midjourney" credit | The hub's magazine section calls for one Midjourney credit in the colophon, plus image credits; see 06 for the exact rule | Hub, The magazine > What's in every issue |
| Links to source docs by Drive id | Refer to docs by title; links are added by the portal | Portal is public |

The hub's own list of out-of-date skill rules says the skill's "pink marks the one thing to see first" is out of date. That is true for video only. For print, the Decision log keeps one pink focus per page, so the skill's print rule still stands.
