# 08 Quality Checklists
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Gus Pressley, Art Director, Production and Prepress
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review

**Who this is for:** Every designer, art director, editor, vendor and AI agent working on a SIDE EYE issue, and anyone who signs off a stage.

**Use it when:** You finish a stage of the issue and need to prove it's done before the next stage starts. Each checklist is a gate.

**Related:** 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files · 02 Cover Standards · 03 Page and Spread Standards · 04 Grid and Typography · 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards) · 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits · 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP) · 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs · 11 Templates (issue brief, flatplan, art-direction doc, handoff note) · 12 Decision Log · 13 Open Questions for Heather · Source docs: SIDE EYE Brand Standards hub · SIDE EYE Design System v3: Neon Zine Print · SIDE EYE Design System v2 Addendum A: KDP Production Spec, 8.5 x 11 · SIDE EYE art-direction skill

---

## How to use these checklists

1. The stages match 01 Issue Design Process: **0 Issue Brief, 1 Flatplan, 2 Art Direction, 3 Art Production, 4 Layout, 5 Review and Legal Read, 6 Prepress, 7 Proof Copy, 8 Release and Archive.**
2. Every item is a yes or no. Tick it only if it's true now, for every page or image it covers.
3. **Any "no" stops the gate.** Fix it, or write it down as an exception with the reason and get Heather's yes for that exception. An exception never covers a hard rule (CMYK, 240% ink, 300 ppi, rights cleared, no generated lettering).
4. The "Who" column says who checks. The person who made the work never signs off their own work alone. Where both a maker and a checker are listed, both tick.
5. Copy the checklist into the issue's working folder (see 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs), fill it in, and keep it with the stage's files. A gate isn't passed until the filled checklist is saved.
6. **Only Heather approves a gate.** AI agents never mark work Approved or Published, and never send, post, upload, order or buy anything on their own (hub, "What always needs Heather's yes").
7. When a rule here and a source doc disagree, the Brand Standards hub's Decision log wins, then the newest Heather decision. Report the clash in the issue's open items; don't decide it yourself.

### Who checks: roles

These are the role names from 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files, section 2.

| Role | Checks |
|---|---|
| Heather (Publisher / Editor in Chief) | Approves every gate, every spend, the upload and the release |
| Art Director (AD) | The brief draft, flatplan, art-direction docs, every image and the look of every spread |
| Designer / Layout | Pages, cover, captions and credits as built |
| Illustrator / Image Generator | Prompts, selects, upscales, edits and the Prompt Log |
| Rights Checker | Every image, font and reproduced text cleared and credited; the Rights Log |
| Fact-check / Research | Facts, proof levels, display text, the Must NOT list and the legal read checklist |
| Production | Color, files, preflight, KDP previewer, proof checks and the archive package |

One person may hold more than one role, but nobody checks their own work. Where two roles are listed on a line, both tick. Sensitivity and expert reads are run by the readers the Issue Brief names.

## Stage 0: Issue Brief sign-off

Use the issue brief template in 11 Templates.

| # | Check | Who | Yes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1 | The brief names the volume number, issue title and one theme | AD, Heather | [ ] |
| 0.2 | The issue thesis is one clear sentence, settled before any title or cover line | AD, Heather | [ ] |
| 0.3 | The lineup lists every feature, column and money page, with a working page count for each | AD | [ ] |
| 0.4 | Target page count is even and inside the house plan of 116 to 132 pages (or the brief says why not) | Production | [ ] |
| 0.5 | Each feature is marked LOUD or QUIET. Serious subjects (deaths, atrocities, real suffering) are QUIET | AD, Heather | [ ] |
| 0.6 | Filing tabs (red, blue, gray) are named for the issue's sections | AD | [ ] |
| 0.7 | The cover hero side is set by volume: odd volumes right, even volumes left (Vol. 1 right, Vol. 2 left) | AD | [ ] |
| 0.8 | Any planned extra that KDP can't print (gatefold, pull-out, perforation, die cut, sticker, insert) is already redesigned as a printed page (07, section 13) | Production | [ ] |
| 0.9 | Living people in the issue are listed (the Living Person Register), so the sourced-public-facts-only rule can be checked later | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 0.10 | Schedule shows dates for each stage, the KDP upload date and a proof-copy round, or the brief records Heather's waiver of the printed proof with its date | Production | [ ] |
| 0.11 | The budget lines are filled: number of proof copies (or waived), any font spend, and zero paid image licenses | Production, Heather | [ ] |
| 0.12 | Heather has approved the brief in writing, with the date | Heather | [ ] |

| 1.9 | No two full-bleed dark pages face each other, and no full-bleed dark page backs a QUIET page of small type | AD, Production | [ ] |
| 1.10 | Every cut-out page is a right-hand page with a sacrificial page behind it | AD | [ ] |
| 1.11 | Each spread has at most one wood-type plate | AD | [ ] |
| 1.12 | The issue has one full-bleed collage opener at most, for the biggest feature | AD | [ ] |
| 1.13 | No artwork on the first and last spreads depends on the book opening flat | AD | [ ] |
| 1.14 | No more than 4 blank pages in a row anywhere | Production | [ ] |
| 1.15 | Heather has approved the flatplan | Heather | [ ] |

| # | Check | Who | Yes |
|---|---|---|---|

| 2.2 | Every page says what pink marks (one thing per page in print) | AD | [ ] |

| 2.4 | Each image has exactly one process: RELIEF, COLLAGE, WOOD TYPE, HYBRID or NEWSPRINT. RELIEF and COLLAGE mix only by the HYBRID recipe | AD | [ ] |
| 2.5 | HYBRID is used only for openers about a real historical person | AD | [ ] |
| 2.6 | Each image uses black plus two inks at most (covers and the tabloid poster may use more) | AD | [ ] |
| 2.7 | QUIET pages plan no neon, no collage, no jokes and no pink | AD, Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 2.8 | Every generated image has two prompts, A (safer) and B (bolder), copy-ready | AD | [ ] |
| 2.9 | Every prompt ends `--style raw --v 8.2` plus its template settings, with `--hd` on full-page and spread art | AD | [ ] |
| 2.10 | Style references are only SIDE EYE's own approved images, at the weights in 05 | AD | [ ] |
| 2.11 | No prompt names an artist, studio, shop or magazine | AD (second reviewer) | [ ] |
| 2.12 | No prompt asks for text; paper, ledgers, letters and signs are described as "completely blank" | AD | [ ] |
| 2.13 | Every historical image is listed with artist, title, date, holding institution, license or public-domain line and source | Rights Checker | [ ] |
| 2.14 | The "Built in layout" table lists everything made by hand or in layout: lettering, wood-type plates, cover lines, captions, composites, ornaments | AD | [ ] |
| 2.15 | Every image has a credit line in the house format | Rights Checker | [ ] |
| 2.16 | The doc was checked against each article's Scorecard "Must NOT claim" list | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 2.17 | Neon builds and any new tint are flagged for the proof | Production | [ ] |
| 2.18 | For covers and openers about living people, an independent reviewer checked the plan | AD (independent) | [ ] |
| 2.19 | Heather has approved the art-direction doc | Heather | [ ] |

## Stage 3: Art Production

### 3A. Per-image check

Run this on every finished image before it goes into layout. It expands the checklist in the art-direction skill and applies the hub's newer decisions.

| # | Check | Who | Yes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3A.1 | The image uses the process the art-direction doc assigned, and only that one | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.2 | Black plus two inks at most (covers and the tabloid poster may use more) | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.3 | Pink marks one thing in the image. If two things are pink, one is wrong | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.4 | The joke is true on the evidence: the art implies no crime, deal or motive that the article's Scorecard rates below its top proof level | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 3A.5 | Living people are shown only doing what sourced public facts support | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 3A.6 | Historical figures, if shown, appear as caricature, real period art or a generated likeness; all three are allowed | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.7 | Letters to the AI Dead figures are skulls wearing one defining prop, never a portrait | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.8 | The image contains no generated lettering, letters or fake writing anywhere, including on signs, papers and labels | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.9 | Period art is not recolored pink or neon; peach is used only on cut-out skin | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.10 | The illustration does not imitate a period document and does not sit inside one | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.11 | Neon reads as flat ink on paper: no glow, chrome, gradient or screen sheen | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.12 | The image looks printed, pasted or carved by hand (paper ground, halftone, speckle), not rendered on a screen | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.13 | Halftone is coarse and applied at final size; no two halftone layers overlap | Production | [ ] |
| 3A.14 | QUIET-page images are NEWSPRINT only: no neon, no collage, no joke | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.15 | No readable brand logos or personal information appear in found material | Rights Checker | [ ] |
| 3A.16 | The final prompt (A or B), settings and style reference are recorded in the art log | AD | [ ] |
| 3A.17 | Effective resolution at placed size is 300 ppi or more (600 for line art and scans), never under 200 | Production | [ ] |
| 3A.18 | The print file is CMYK or Grayscale, with flat-ink art rebuilt to the house builds by number | Production | [ ] |
| 3A.19 | Total ink is 240% or less across the image | Production | [ ] |
| 3A.20 | The RGB master and the print file are both saved and named per 09 | Designer / Layout | [ ] |
| 3A.21 | Neon builds in the image are flagged for the proof | Production | [ ] |

If any image will also be used in video, it also passes the pause test: no frame could pass for real footage of a real person (hub, AI-generated video).

Run per image, then once for the whole issue. See 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits.

| 3B.7 | No shop's or magazine's name, logo, typefaces cut from their blocks, or recognizable layouts appear. Influences are credited only in the colophon | AD, Rights Checker | [ ] |
| 3B.8 | Every font used has a recorded license, or is a free stand-in with an open license | Production | [ ] |
| 3B.9 | Every quoted text, letter or document reproduced is public domain or cleared | Fact-check / Research, Rights Checker | [ ] |
| 3B.10 | Any image with a license question is marked "not cleared" and kept out of layout until cleared | Rights Checker | [ ] |
| 3B.11 | The image-rights log for the issue is complete, one row per image | Rights Checker | [ ] |

## Stage 5: Review and Legal Read (copy and legal handoff)

The legal read comes last, after layout, so it reads what will print. Use this check to hand the laid-out issue to the editor and standards reader, and to hand it back to layout.

| # | Check | Who | Yes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | A full low-resolution review PDF of the current layout was sent for the read, with its date and build in the file name | Designer / Layout | [ ] |
| 5.2 | Every claim in the issue has a source and a proof level in the fact-check log | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 5.3 | The back-of-issue sources section matches the fact-check log one to one, with page numbers | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 5.4 | Headlines, decks, cover lines, captions, pull quotes and jokes claim no more than the proof supports | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 5.5 | The legal read checklist was run by someone who did not write the piece. Every line or image that frames a living person as doing wrong is softened to court and filing language, or cut. (Heather's decision, November 24, 2026) | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 5.6 | The Must NOT list was checked line by line against the text and the art (the art binds as much as the text) | Fact-check / Research, AD | [ ] |
| 5.7 | Any sensitivity or expert reads the brief calls for are done; no joke punches down; QUIET subjects are treated as QUIET | Readers named in the brief, AD | [ ] |
| 5.8 | No banned words or phrases, including "receipts" used as a metaphor for proof | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 5.9 | The corrections page prints every known error in the same size type as the original error | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 5.10 | The colophon carries the Midjourney credit, all image credits and the influence credits | Fact-check / Research, Rights Checker | [ ] |
| 5.11 | Hosts appear by first name only, with no last names, phone numbers, email or home location anywhere in the issue | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 5.12 | Heather's notes and the read's fixes are sorted into fixes and Change Requests, by page number, in one list | AD | [ ] |
| 5.13 | After fixes, each changed page was checked again; no text changed after the read without a re-read of that page | Fact-check / Research, AD | [ ] |
| 5.14 | Heather has signed off text and layout for prepress. After this, changes need a Change Request | Heather | [ ] |

Open items to watch here (see 13 Open Questions for Heather): whether the no-em-dash rule covers print design copy, and the name of the back-of-issue sources section.

## Stage 7: Proof Copy

Only Heather orders a printed proof, because it spends money. If the Issue Brief records her waiver of the printed proof, skip to 7.16.

| # | Check | Who | Yes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | The proof was printed from the exact files that passed Stage 6 | Production | [ ] |
| 7.2 | Every page was checked in daylight or daylight-balanced light, with problems photographed and logged by page number | Production, AD | [ ] |
| 7.3 | Side Eye Pink reads as loud and warm on every page that uses it, with one pink focus per page | AD | [ ] |
| 7.4 | Cyan, orange and yellow read as bright; yellow doesn't vanish on the cream tint | AD | [ ] |
| 7.5 | Navy and rich black fields are even and solid, with no show-through that hurts the facing page | Production | [ ] |
| 7.6 | Cream, Newsprint Gray and Kraft tints are even and match from page to page | Production | [ ] |
| 7.7 | 7 pt type and reversed type are sharp and readable | Production, Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 7.8 | Halftone reads as dots, with no moiré | Production | [ ] |
| 7.9 | Nothing important is lost in the gutter; spread images line up across it | Designer / Layout | [ ] |
| 7.10 | Nothing important is trimmed off; folios and tab labels sit safely inside | Designer / Layout | [ ] |
| 7.11 | Cover: spine color sits on the spine, cover lines are safe, barcode is clear, matte finish is even | Production, AD | [ ] |
| 7.12 | Binding: pages in order, none missing or doubled | Production | [ ] |
| 7.13 | Any ink build changed because of the proof is logged in 12 Decision Log with the date and page | Production | [ ] |
| 7.14 | Every proof problem is fixed at the source, exported, preflighted again (Stage 6) and re-uploaded | Production | [ ] |
| 7.15 | The proof report (page, problem, fix) is saved; Heather decided on a second proof at this report for any changed ink build, tint or cover | Production, Heather | [ ] |
| 7.16 | If the proof was waived: only builds already seen on a printed SIDE EYE copy are used, every page was checked in the KDP Previewer at full size, the proof report records "waived" with the date of Heather's decision, and Heather approved publishing without a proof | Production, Heather | [ ] |

---

## Stage 8: Release and Archive

| # | Check | Who | Yes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8.1 | Stages 0 to 7 checklists are complete and saved with the issue | Production | [ ] |
| 8.2 | The files on KDP are the same files as in the archive package (same names, same dates) | Production | [ ] |
| 8.3 | The listing is set up as a paperback book in the numbered SIDE EYE series, with "magazine" kept out of the title fields | Heather | [ ] |
| 8.4 | Heather has given her yes to publish, with the date recorded | Heather | [ ] |
| 8.5 | Heather (or someone she named) pressed Publish. No agent or vendor published | Heather | [ ] |
| 8.6 | The archive package in 08 Package is complete: README, Layout, Links, Masters, Fonts (free-license only), Output (including a text-searchable PDF or text export), Prepress, Proof, Logs (07, section 14) | Production | [ ] |
| 8.7 | The package was opened in a clean folder and rebuilt with no missing links or fonts | Production | [ ] |
| 8.8 | Every older build and draft is moved to _Archive and marked SUPERSEDED, not mixed with final files | Production | [ ] |
| 8.9 | The Rights Log, the Art Log and the Prompt Log (with approved Midjourney job links) are final and saved with the package | Rights Checker, AD | [ ] |
| 8.10 | Any decision made during the issue is added to 12 Decision Log; any unsettled question is added to 13 Open Questions for Heather | Production | [ ] |
| 8.11 | A corrections file for this issue is started, so errors found after release go into the next issue's corrections page | Fact-check / Research | [ ] |
| 8.12 | Once the book is live, the live listing page and the look-inside preview were checked against the final files | Production, Heather |
| 8.13 | The Release Note (11 Templates) is written, and the look-back's problems are filed as Standards Change Requests (01, section 9) | Production | [ ] |

## The gates on one page

Who passes each gate follows 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files, section 1.

| Stage | Gate passes when | Who passes it |
|---|---|---|
| 0 Issue Brief | Brief complete; thesis set; extras KDP can't print redesigned; proof budget set | Heather |
| 1 Flatplan | Every page planned, even count, recipes and registers set | Heather |
| 2 Art Direction | Art-direction docs complete and checked against the Must NOT list | Heather (cover and concepts) |
| 3 Art Production | Every image passes 3A; every image and font passes 3B | AD (each image); Heather (cover art) |
| 4 Layout | Every spread passes 4A; cover passes 4B; full review PDF made | AD (ready for review) |
| 5 Review and Legal Read | Heather's read done, legal read checklist signed, fixes confirmed | Heather |
| 6 Prepress | Files pass preflight, total ink and the KDP Print Previewer | Production signs; Heather uploads |
| 7 Proof Copy | Proof checked and fixed, or the waiver recorded | Heather |
| 8 Release and Archive | Heather publishes; package complete and rebuilt; Release Note written | Heather (publish); Production (archive) |

# 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Wren Hale, Onboarding and Handoff
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review
**Who this is for:** Everyone who saves, names, delivers or receives a SIDE EYE issue file: designers, illustrators, AI agents, print vendors and the art director.
**Use it when:** You create a folder, save or rename a file, deliver work, or hand an issue to someone else.
**Related:** 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files; 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards); 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits; 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP); 08 Quality Checklists; 11 Templates (issue brief, flatplan, art-direction doc, handoff note); Design System v2 Addendum A: KDP Production Spec, 8.5 x 11; SIDE EYE Brand Standards (section Logo)

---

## Five rules for every file

1. **Save it in the issue's folder tree,** in the stage folder it belongs to. Nothing lives on a desktop or in a personal folder.
2. **Name it by the pattern in this doc.** A file someone else can't identify from its name alone is misnamed.
3. **Never overwrite a delivered file.** Save a new version number instead.
4. **Never move, rename or delete a file you didn't make.** Older issue files stay where they are until Heather says yes to a cleanup.
5. **Update the art log and the rights log** every time you add, pick, change or place an image.

---

## 1. The issue folder tree

### Where issues live today

Three layouts exist in Drive. Don't move anything in them.

| Folder | What it is | How to treat it |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Side Eye > Side Eye_Issue 1 | Vol. 1, built before any standard. Loose feature and sources docs, plus subfolders "Issue 1 to print", "Side Eye_Issue 1_Images", "SIDE EYE – Side-Eye Art (public domain)", "Packaged In Progress" and "YouTube". | Leave as is. Use the map below to find things. |
| Side Eye Production > Magazine > SE-V0N (issue title) | The stage-folder layout the team's production app set up on Nov 24, 2026, with an Issue Hub doc, numbered stage folders and an art log. | **The standard tree.** Use it for every new issue. |
| Side Eye > Side Eye Issue 2, Side Eye Issue 3 | Two empty folders made on Nov 24, 2026. | Don't use them until Heather says which home wins (open question). |

**Follow the SE-V0N stage folders in Side Eye Production > Magazine until Heather decides** whether issues live there or in the Side Eye Issue N folders at the Side Eye root. Either way, the tree inside the issue folder is the same.

### The standard tree

The issue folder is named `SE-V02 The Money Is Fake Issue`: the issue code, a space, then the issue's title. Inside it:

```
SE-V0N <Issue title>/
  00 Issue Hub – SE-V0N <Issue title>      (Google Doc: the one page that links every live file)
  01 Plan/                                  issue brief, flatplan, schedule notes
  02 Research/                              Scorecards, fact-check log, sources doc
  03 Manuscript/                            approved text, one doc per piece or one full manuscript
  04 Art/
    00 Art Log – SE-V0N <Issue title>       (Google Sheet: one row per image slot)
    00 Rights Log – SE-V0N <Issue title>    (Google Sheet: one row per image, keyed by image ID; see 06)
    01 Briefs/                              art-direction docs and per-page art briefs
    02 Prompts/                             prompt sheets (A and B per image)
    03 Generations/                         every downloaded candidate, untouched
    04 Selects/                             the candidates picked for use, untouched
    05 Archival/                            public-domain and free-license originals, untouched
    06 Rights Evidence/                     saved PDFs or screenshots of each rights statement, named by image ID
    07 Composites/                          layered working files (HYBRID, COLLAGE, edited RELIEF)
    08 Textures and Kit/                    paper, ink textures, ornaments, icons, reusable pieces
    09 Finals/                              flattened, print-ready art that layout links to
  05 Layout/
    Source/                                 the live layout file or HTML build
    Links/                                  copies of everything placed (or a pointer to 04 Art/09 Finals)
    Fonts/                                  free-license fonts only
    Page Specs/                             page-by-page specs from agents who can't run layout software
    Exports/                                review PDFs
  06 Reviews and Legal/                     marked-up review PDFs, review notes, legal and standards reads, sign-offs
  07 KDP and Print/
    Interior/                               interior PDFs sent or ready to send to KDP
    Cover/                                  cover PDFs, the KDP cover template used
    Proofs/                                 KDP previewer notes and screenshots, proof-copy photos and notes
  08 Package/                               the closing bundle: layout, links, fonts, PDFs, README
  _Archive/                                 superseded versions, kept for history
```

Folders 00 Art Log through 04 Selects inside 04 Art already exist in the production app's tree. The 00 Rights Log sheet, folders 05 to 09 inside 04 Art, the subfolders of 05 Layout and 07 KDP and Print, and 08 Package are additions from this standard. Create them when you first need them. Don't rename the existing ones.

The issue's YouTube files stay out of this tree. Video has its own folders and rules (see the Brand Standards hub, section The YouTube show).

### What goes where (quick reference)

| You have | Put it in |
| :- | :- |
| A Midjourney download you haven't picked | 04 Art/03 Generations |
| The candidate you picked, as downloaded | 04 Art/04 Selects |
| A public-domain painting, as downloaded | 04 Art/05 Archival |
| The saved rights statement for any image | 04 Art/06 Rights Evidence |
| A new image's rights row | 04 Art/00 Rights Log |
| The layered file where you cut out a period figure | 04 Art/07 Composites |
| A paper texture or ornament sheet used on many pages | 04 Art/08 Textures and Kit |
| The flattened file layout places | 04 Art/09 Finals |
| A review PDF with Heather's comments | 06 Reviews and Legal |
| The PDF uploaded to KDP | 07 KDP and Print/Interior or Cover |
| An older version of anything | _Archive (keep the same name) |

### Where Vol. 1 material sits (map, nothing moved)

| Standard folder | Vol. 1 equivalent in Side Eye_Issue 1 |
| :- | :- |
| 02 Research | the "Final ... Feature – Sources" docs, loose in the issue folder |
| 03 Manuscript | the "Final ... Feature" and sidebar docs, loose in the issue folder; the full manuscripts are at the Side Eye root |
| 04 Art/03 Generations and 04 Selects | Side Eye_Issue 1_Images (with its midjourney_session and upscale subfolders) |
| 04 Art/05 Archival and 00 Rights Log | SIDE EYE – Side-Eye Art (public domain), with its Credits sheet (the Vol. 1 stand-in for a rights log) |
| 06 Reviews and Legal | the MedRes and MedRes-Review PDFs, loose in the issue folder |
| 07 KDP and Print | Issue 1 to print |
| 08 Package | Packaged In Progress |

Vol. 1's Midjourney prompts and slot IDs are in the folder "SIDE EYE – Midjourney Prompt Workbook v2 (V8.2-hardened, Oct 17)" at the Side Eye root.

---

## 2. File naming

### General rules

- **No spaces in file names.** Use underscores between fields and hyphens inside a field. Layout links and HTML builds break on spaces.
- **Lowercase for slugs,** CAPS only for the process tag. No accents, apostrophes, slashes or emoji.
- **Dates as YYYY-MM-DD** (2026-12-15), in file names and in new folder names.
- **Versions as v01, v02, v03.** Two digits. Raise the number for every changed delivery.
- **Never put a host's last name, a phone number, an email or a location in a file name.**
- **Rename Midjourney downloads right away.** Midjourney's default names carry the account's user name and a long code. Don't keep them in any shared folder.
- **Google Docs and Sheets** use readable titles with spaces (see below). The no-spaces rule is for files that layout or code links to.

### Issue code

`SE-V02` = SIDE EYE, volume 2. Always two digits. Use it at the start of every art and layout file name, and in Drive titles inside the stage tree.

### Image ID

Every image in an issue gets one image ID. 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits defines it, and this doc uses the same format.

```
SE-V<vol>-<type><NN>
```

| Part | Rule | Example |
| :- | :- | :- |
| `SE-V<vol>` | Issue code | SE-V02 |
| `-` | One hyphen | |
| `<type>` | One letter from 06, section 2.1: I generated art, H archival image, X composite, P our photo, F found ephemera, S our scan, L hand lettering | H |
| `<NN>` | Two digits, counted per type, in order of first use in the issue | 03 |

Examples: `SE-V02-H03` (the third archival image), `SE-V02-I12` (the twelfth generated image), `SE-V02-X01` (the first composite).

Rules:

- **Give the ID when the image first gets a row** in the art log or the rights log. Never change it after that, even if the page moves.
- **Never reuse an ID,** even for an image you dropped. Mark the dropped row Skip and give the replacement a new ID.
- **Use the same ID in the art log, the rights log, the rights evidence file and the credit list.** The image ID is the key that joins them. The file name is how layout finds the file.
- **Name each rights evidence file by its image ID:** `SE-V<vol>-<type><NN>_rights_<YYYY-MM-DD>.pdf` (or .png for a screenshot), dated the day the rights statement was read. Example: `SE-V02-H03_rights_2026-12-02.pdf`. Save it in 04 Art/06 Rights Evidence.
- **Don't put the image ID in the image file name.** The file name pattern below already says the page, piece, process and version. Record both in the same art log row.

### Art files (generated, archival, composites, finals)

Pattern:

```
SE-V<vol>_<page>_<piece-slug>_<PROCESS>_<variant>_v<NN>.<ext>
```

| Field | Rule | Examples |
| :- | :- | :- |
| `SE-V<vol>` | Issue code | SE-V02 |
| `<page>` | First page where the image is placed, three digits. A spread uses both pages. Covers use `cvr` (front), `bck` (back) or `spn` (spine). Use `p000` until the flatplan gives the image a page. | p014, p014-015, cvr |
| `<piece-slug>` | Short name of the piece and the image, lowercase, hyphens, 24 characters or fewer. Add `-2`, `-3` when one piece has several images on the same page. | bower-resume, ai-dead-newton, potosi-mine-2 |
| `<PROCESS>` | One of: RELIEF, COLLAGE, WOODTYPE, HYBRID, NEWSPRINT for made art; ARCHIVAL for historical images; TEXTURE for paper and ink textures; BUILT for vector or type built by hand in layout | HYBRID, ARCHIVAL |
| `<variant>` | A or B for the prompt it came from, plus the grid number of the pick (1 to 4). Leave it out for ARCHIVAL, TEXTURE and BUILT. | A, B3 |
| `v<NN>` | Version | v01, v03 |
| `<ext>` | See the image specs in section 3 | png, tif, psd |

Examples:

- `SE-V02_p014-015_bower-resume_HYBRID_B2_v03.tif` (a spread opener, from prompt B, grid image 2, third version)
- `SE-V02_p022_potosi-mine_ARCHIVAL_v01.tif` (a historical image, cleaned and resized)
- `SE-V02_cvr_hero-emu_COLLAGE_A4_v02.psd` (the cover hero, layered)
- `SE-V02_p000_cast-cameo-boot_RELIEF_A1_v01.png` (a select not yet given a page)

Rules:

- **The page number is set when the file is first named for placement.** If the flatplan later moves the page, don't rename a file that is already linked in layout. Update the Page(s) column in the art log instead.
- **One file name, one image.** Don't reuse a name for a different picture, even after a delete.
- **Generations** keep the same pattern with the grid number, so a whole Midjourney run is easy to find: `SE-V02_p014-015_bower-resume_HYBRID_B1_v01.png` to `..._B4_v01.png`.
- **Archival originals** keep a second, untouched copy named the way Vol. 1's public-domain folder did: source ID, artist, title. Example: `cma109042 – Honoré Daumier – Art Lovers.jpg`. The working copy then takes the standard pattern.
- **Vol. 1 slot IDs** (V1-010-1, MJ-01 and so on, from the Prompt Workbook) stay as they are. When you reuse a Vol. 1 file, keep its name and note the reuse in the art log.

The current art log's column header reads "File name (p###_piece_v#)". This pattern starts the same way and adds the issue code, process and variant. **Follow this pattern until Heather decides** whether it replaces both the art log's short form and the Workbook slot IDs (open question).

### Logo files

Never rename a logo file. Use the logo pack's own names, which follow `SIDE-EYE_<layout>_<color>[_<background>][_<width>w]`. The Brand Standards hub, section Logo, says which file to use for which job.

### Layout files

| File | Pattern | Example |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Whole-issue layout | `SE-V<vol>_layout_v<NN>.<ext>` | SE-V02_layout_v07.indd |
| Section layout (when an issue is split) | `SE-V<vol>_<pages>_<section>_v<NN>.<ext>` | SE-V02_p014-061_files_v03.indd |
| HTML build folder | `SE-V<vol>_layout_v<NN>/` | SE-V02_layout_v07/ |
| Page spec (agents) | `SE-V<vol>_<page>_pagespec_v<NN>.md` or `.json` | SE-V02_p014-015_pagespec_v02.json |

### Review and print PDFs

These follow the names Vol. 1 already used: `SIDE-EYE-Vol1-MedRes.pdf`, `SIDE-EYE-Vol1-Interior-KDP.pdf`, `SIDE-EYE-Vol1-Cover-KDP.pdf`.

| File | Pattern | Example |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Review PDF (whole issue, lower resolution) | `SIDE-EYE-Vol<N>-MedRes_<YYYY-MM-DD>_v<NN>.pdf` | SIDE-EYE-Vol2-MedRes_2026-12-15_v01.pdf |
| Marked-up review PDF | add `-Review` and the reviewer's role, not their name | SIDE-EYE-Vol2-MedRes-Review-publisher_2026-12-16.pdf |
| Interior for KDP, working | `SIDE-EYE-Vol<N>-Interior-KDP_v<NN>.pdf` | SIDE-EYE-Vol2-Interior-KDP_v03.pdf |
| Cover for KDP, working | `SIDE-EYE-Vol<N>-Cover-KDP_v<NN>.pdf` | SIDE-EYE-Vol2-Cover-KDP_v02.pdf |
| The exact files uploaded to KDP | replace the version with `-final` | SIDE-EYE-Vol2-Interior-KDP-final.pdf |

Only the files actually uploaded to KDP carry `-final`. If a fix is needed after upload, the next upload becomes `-final-2`, and the earlier one moves to _Archive.

PDF technical specs (page size, bleed, fonts, color, flattening) are in 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP). This doc covers names and places only.

### Google Docs and Sheets titles

Drive already uses two title styles. Keep both.

| Kind | Title pattern | Example |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Stage-tree working docs | `<NN> <Doc name> – SE-V<vol> <Issue title>` | 00 Art Log – SE-V03 The Mating Issue |
| Standalone issue docs | `SIDE EYE Vol. <N> – <Doc name> v<N> (CURRENT)` | SIDE EYE Vol. 1 – Manuscript v3 (CURRENT) |
| Drafts | add ` – DRAFT, <YYYY-MM-DD>` | ... – DRAFT, 2026-12-03 |
| Replaced docs | rename the old one to `SUPERSEDED – <old title> (use v<N>)` and move it to _Archive | SUPERSEDED – MASTER Writing Style Guide v1 (use v2, CURRENT) |

Rules:

- **Only one doc of each kind is CURRENT at a time.** When you make a new version, mark the old one SUPERSEDED in the same session.
- **Update the Issue Hub link** to the new version at the same time.
- Older titles used dates like "Oct 6". Don't rename them. Use YYYY-MM-DD for new ones.

---

## 3. Image file specs

Print numbers come from Design System v2 Addendum A. Color conversion and ink limits are in 07.

| Stage | Format | Color | Resolution | Layers |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| Generations (03) | PNG, exactly as downloaded. Never re-save as JPEG. | RGB as delivered | Native Midjourney size (use the HD setting for full-page and spread art, per 05) | Flat |
| Selects (04) | Same file as the generation, copied | RGB | Native | Flat |
| Archival originals (05) | The highest-resolution file the holding institution offers, unchanged | As delivered | Largest available | Flat |
| Composites and edits (07) | Layered TIFF or PSD (Affinity users: .afphoto, plus a layered TIFF copy) | RGB working space until the final step | Built at final placed size, 300 ppi or more | **Layered and named:** every source on its own layer, named by its source file name |
| Textures and kit (08) | TIFF or PNG; tileable textures say "tile" in the slug | Grayscale where the texture is one ink | 300 ppi at the largest size used | Flat |
| Finals for layout (09) | Flattened TIFF with LZW compression. For the HTML build route, PNG. | Follow 07 for when and how RGB becomes CMYK. Convert once, never twice. | **300 ppi at placed size.** Full-bleed page: at least 2,588 × 3,375 px. Never below 200 ppi at any size. Line art and document scans: 600 ppi grayscale where possible. | Flat |

More rules:

- **Upscale before you edit,** and sharpen after scaling, never before (Addendum A).
- **Check resolution at placed size,** not at file size. Divide the pixel width by the placed width in inches. Record the pixel size and the yes/no 300 ppi check in the art log.
- **Peach is for period skin on cut-outs only.** Pink is never painted onto period art. See 05 and the hub's color rules.
- **Keep the untouched generation.** Edits happen on a copy in 07 Composites. The pick in 04 Selects is never changed.
- **No generated lettering survives into a final.** If a generation shows letters, a logo or an eye mark, clean it or regenerate it. Real type is added in layout.

---

## 4. Layout handoff

There are three routes to a finished layout. Pick the one your tools allow and say which one in your handoff note.

### Route 1: Packaged layout file (InDesign or Affinity Publisher)

Deliver a package, not a bare layout file.

- **InDesign:** File > Package. Include copied fonts that may be shared (free-license fonts only), copied linked graphics, an IDML copy, and a review PDF. Name the package folder `SE-V<vol>_layout_v<NN>_package`.
- **Affinity Publisher:** Save as Package, with the same contents and a PDF.
- Every link must resolve inside the package. No missing or modified links.
- Paid fonts (Druk, Tiempos, GT America Mono, the Hamilton Wood Type faces) have no recorded print license. Don't send paid font files. If the layout uses them, list them in the README and say which free stand-in each one maps to (see 04).

### Route 2: HTML build (the Vol. 1 route)

Vol. 1's interior was not built in InDesign. It was an HTML and CSS document, paginated by a script and printed to PDF in Chromium. An AI agent with code tools can use the same route.

Deliver what the Vol. 1 package delivered:

- The layout source (the HTML, the CSS with color tokens at the top, the pagination script, and the build and render scripts).
- The manuscript text files the build reads.
- Every placed image, in folders that match the paths in the CSS.
- The fonts (free-license only).
- The output PDFs.
- A README that says, step by step, how to rebuild the PDFs on a clean machine, and what still needs doing.

Never hard-code a path to a temporary folder or a personal machine. Every path must be relative to the package.

### Route 3: Page spec (agents who can't run layout software)

If you can't produce pages, don't hand back a description. Hand back a **page spec**: an exact, page-by-page build sheet that a designer or a layout script can follow without guessing.

One spec per page, in Markdown with a JSON block, or JSON alone. Pages are single pages in reading order, as KDP wants them. Odd pages are right-hand pages.

Coordinates:

- Units are inches, to three decimals.
- The origin (0, 0) is the **top-left corner of the trimmed page.**
- Bleed elements extend past the trim: to −0.125 at the top, to 11.125 at the bottom, and past the outside edge (to −0.125 on a left-hand page, to 8.625 on a right-hand page). Never bleed into the gutter.
- Give each element's grid columns too. Grid math is in 04 Grid and Typography. Columns are counted 1 to 12 from the gutter side on right-hand pages and from the outside edge on left-hand pages, so column 1 always starts at the left margin.

Every page spec includes:

| Field | What to give |
| :- | :- |
| `issue`, `page`, `side` | SE-V02, 15, right |
| `spread_with` | The facing page number |
| `piece`, `recipe` | The manuscript piece and the page recipe name from 03 |
| `register` | LOUD or QUIET |
| `tab` | Filing tab color and label, or none |
| `pink_focus` | The id of the one element that carries pink, or none for QUIET |
| `inks` | The inks on the page |
| `elements` | Every element, in stacking order from back to front |
| `checks` | Safety, gutter and type-size checks you ran, each true or false |
| `open_items` | Anything you couldn't settle |

Each element includes: `id`, `type` (image, text, rule, shape, logo), `source` (an exact file name from 04 Art/09 Finals, a logo pack file name, or a manuscript reference with the first and last words), `box` (x, y, w, h in inches), `grid` (first column and span), `style` (a named style from 04, such as body, caption, deck), `rotation` in degrees, and `notes`.

Example (a right-hand QUIET page, shortened):

```json
{
  "issue": "SE-V02",
  "page": 15,
  "side": "right",
  "spread_with": 14,
  "piece": "The Mountain",
  "recipe": "QUIET pages",
  "register": "QUIET",
  "tab": {"color": "gray", "label": "Paid For"},
  "pink_focus": "none",
  "inks": ["black", "newsprint gray tint"],
  "elements": [
    {"id": "bg", "type": "shape", "source": "Newsprint Gray tint per 07",
     "box": {"x": 0, "y": -0.125, "w": 8.625, "h": 11.25}, "grid": null,
     "style": "page-tint", "rotation": 0, "notes": "bleeds top, bottom and outside only"},
    {"id": "img1", "type": "image",
     "source": "SE-V02_p015_potosi-mine_ARCHIVAL_v02.tif",
     "box": {"x": 0.375, "y": 0.5, "w": 7.625, "h": 4.5}, "grid": {"start": 1, "span": 12},
     "style": "halftone-photo", "rotation": 0, "notes": "grayscale, coarse halftone"},
    {"id": "cap1", "type": "text",
     "source": "manuscript: The Mountain, caption 1, 'Cerro Rico...' to '...1590s.'",
     "box": {"x": 0.375, "y": 5.1, "w": 3.75, "h": 0.6}, "grid": {"start": 1, "span": 6},
     "style": "caption", "rotation": 0, "notes": "credit line follows the caption"}
  ],
  "checks": {"text_inside_safety": true, "nothing_essential_within_0.5_of_gutter": true,
             "min_type_7pt": true, "one_pink_focus": true},
  "open_items": ["img1 is SE-V02-H03; rights log status still Pending"]
}
```

The values above are for showing the format only. Use 04 for real column positions.

A page spec is finished only when every image in it is in 04 Art/09 Finals and every text box points to approved manuscript text.

---

## 5. The handoff note

Every delivery, of any size, comes with a handoff note. Use the handoff note template in 11 Templates. Save it in the folder you delivered to, named `SE-V<vol>_handoff_<YYYY-MM-DD>_<what>.md` (or as a Google Doc titled the same way with spaces).

A handoff note answers these questions, in this order:

1. **What this is.** The issue, the pieces or pages, the route (packaged file, HTML build or page spec), and the version.
2. **What's in it.** A list of every file delivered, by exact file name and folder.
3. **What changed** since the last version, in one line per change.
4. **Checks run.** Which checklists from 08 you ran and the result. Any check that failed, and why.
5. **Rights and credits.** Every new image's image ID, rights status (Cleared, Pending or Blocked, per 06) and credit line, matching the rights log.
6. **Art log and rights log.** Which rows you updated, by image ID.
7. **Open items.** Each one with the options, what each costs, and what you did in the meantime.
8. **Rule conflicts you hit.** Which two sources disagreed and which one you followed.
9. **What you need back,** from whom, and by when.
10. **What's next** if you keep the job, or who picks it up if you don't.

Rules:

- **Never write "approved"** unless the art log shows who approved it and when. AI agents never mark anything Approved.
- **No private data** in a handoff note: no personal contact details, no addresses, no account names.
- **Plain words.** Someone who has never seen the issue should understand it.

---

## 6. Delivery checklist

Run this before every delivery. Run the matching checklists in 08 too.

**Files and names**
- [ ] Every file is in the right stage folder of the issue tree.
- [ ] Every file name follows section 2. No spaces in linked files. No default Midjourney names.
- [ ] No delivered file was overwritten. Old versions are in _Archive.
- [ ] No host last names, contact details or locations in any file name or file.

**Images**
- [ ] Each image's untouched generation is in 03 Generations and its pick in 04 Selects.
- [ ] Each final is in 04 Art/09 Finals at 300 ppi at placed size (200 ppi absolute floor).
- [ ] Composites are layered with named layers.
- [ ] No generated lettering, logos or eye marks in any image.
- [ ] Color handled the way 07 says, converted once.

**Art log and rights log**
- [ ] One row per image slot, with page, status, brief, prompt or source, chosen file, file name, source and license, credit line, pixel size and the 300 ppi check.
- [ ] Every image has an image ID (`SE-V<vol>-<type><NN>`) used the same way in the art log, the rights log and the credit list.
- [ ] Every image has a rights log row. Its status is true (Cleared, Pending or Blocked). Nothing Pending or Blocked goes to print.
- [ ] Every rights statement is saved in 04 Art/06 Rights Evidence, named by image ID.
- [ ] Approved by and Approved on are blank unless Heather or the art director approved it.

**Layout**
- [ ] The route is named (packaged file, HTML build, page spec).
- [ ] All links resolve inside the package. Fonts included only if free-license.
- [ ] Review PDF exported and named by section 2.

**Handoff**
- [ ] Handoff note written and saved next to the delivery.
- [ ] Issue Hub links updated to the new versions.
- [ ] Nothing shared, moved or renamed outside your own files. No sharing settings changed.

---

## Sources this is built on

- SIDE EYE Brand Standards (the Brand Standards hub), sections For vendors, editors and AI agents, Logo, Decision log
- Design System v2 Addendum A: KDP Production Spec, 8.5 x 11
- Design System v3: Neon Zine Print

# 10 Vendor and Agent Onboarding Starter
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Wren Hale, Onboarding and Handoff
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review
**Who this is for:** New outside vendors (designers, illustrators, cover designers, prepress shops) and new AI agents working on a SIDE EYE magazine issue.
**Use it when:** It is your first day on SIDE EYE, you are setting up an AI agent for SIDE EYE work, or you need a term explained.
**Related:** 00 Start Here: How to Use the SIDE EYE Art Direction Standards; 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files; 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards); 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits; 08 Quality Checklists; 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs; 11 Templates (issue brief, flatplan, art-direction doc, handoff note); SIDE EYE Brand Standards (section For vendors, editors and AI agents; section AI files)

---

This doc builds on the Brand Standards hub, section For vendors, editors and AI agents. The hub's rules for everyone still apply. This doc adds what a magazine vendor or agent needs on top of them.

## 1. Your first hour

Do these in order. Tick each one.

| Minutes | Do this | Done when |
| :- | :- | :- |
| 0 to 10 | Read the hub's Start here and Brand core. | You can say the tagline and the five print processes. |
| 10 to 20 | Read 00 Start Here: How to Use the SIDE EYE Art Direction Standards, including the golden rules and the source-of-truth order. | You know which document wins when two disagree. |
| 20 to 25 | Skim the hub's Decision log and 12 Decision Log. | You know Heather's newest calls. |
| 25 to 35 | Read the docs in your row of the reading-order table in 00. Start with the one for your job (section 4 below). | You have opened every doc on your list. |
| 35 to 45 | Read your issue's Issue Hub doc and the issue brief. Find the flatplan, the Scorecards and the art log. | You know the issue's theme, art director and deadline. |
| 45 to 50 | AI agents: load the files in section 2. Vendors using AI tools: do the same for each tool. | The agent answers the check questions in section 2 correctly. |
| 50 to 60 | Write your first status note (section 6). List what you'll do first and any question you already have. | The art director has your note. |

If you have only ten minutes, read the golden rules in 00 and section 5 of this doc (who decides what).

## 2. Setting up an AI agent

### What to load

| Order | File | Where it goes | Why |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| 1 | SIDE-EYE-SYSTEM-PROMPT.md (from the hub, section AI files) | The instructions box of a Claude Project, custom GPT or Gemini Gem | The brand rules in short form |
| 2 | The starter block in section 3 below | Paste it under the hub prompt, in the same box | The magazine production rules in short form |
| 3 | SIDE-EYE-BRAND.md (from the hub, section AI files) | Upload as a knowledge file | The whole brand guide |
| 4 | This set, 00 to 13, as Markdown files | Upload as knowledge files | The design and production standards |
| 5 | The side-eye-art-direction skill (Claude only) | Install it as a skill | The art-direction method and prompt templates |
| 6 | The issue's brief, flatplan, Scorecards and manuscript | Attach for each job, not as standing files | The facts for this issue |

Other AIs can't load a skill. Paste the skill's rules into the instructions instead, as the hub's Skills section says.

### Known out-of-date rules to override

Installed skills lag behind Heather's decisions. Tell the agent these, in its instructions or at the start of the job. Each line comes from the hub's Skills section ("Rules inside skills that may be out of date") or its Decision log.

- **People:** older skill text says no faces or likenesses of real people. The current rule: generated likenesses of historical figures are allowed. Living people follow the sourced-public-facts-only rule. AI Dead figures are always skulls wearing a prop.
- **Pink:** "pink marks one thing" is a print rule. Print keeps one pink focus per page. In video, pink can be used freely.
- **AI video:** generated video only illustrates an idea and must pass the pause test (see the glossary). That rule is for video. Print art follows 05.
- **Prompt endings and settings:** use the templates in 05 exactly as written. If a skill's ending differs, 05 wins until Heather decides otherwise (see 13 Open Questions for Heather).

### Check questions

Ask the agent these before you give it real work. Every answer must match.

1. "How many inks can one print image use?" Black plus two, one pink focus per page, total ink under 240%.
2. "Can you put the word SIDE EYE into a Midjourney prompt so it prints on the cover?" No. No generated lettering. The logo is placed from the logo pack.
3. "Can you name a famous poster artist in a prompt to get the style?" No. Describe materials and methods only.
4. "A feature is about a massacre. Which register?" QUIET. No neon, no collage, no jokes.
5. "Can you mark the cover Approved?" No. Only Heather approves.

If the agent gets one wrong, fix its instructions and ask again.

## 3. Paste-in starter prompt

Paste this plain text into the instructions box, under the hub's short system prompt. It holds no private data. Don't add any.

```
SIDE EYE MAGAZINE: ART DIRECTION AND PRODUCTION RULES

You help make the SIDE EYE print magazine (8.5 x 11 in, Amazon KDP). The brand rules are in SIDE-EYE-BRAND.md. The design and production rules are in the SIDE EYE Art Direction Standards, documents 00 to 13. Read 00 Start Here and 10 Vendor and Agent Onboarding Starter before any work.

WHICH RULE WINS
1. Heather's newest dated decision (12 Decision Log and the brand file's Decision log).
2. The brand file.
3. The Art Direction Standards, 00 to 13.
4. Design System v3: Neon Zine Print.
5. Design System v2 Core and v2 Addendum A (KDP Production Spec).
6. Older docs and skills, for history only.
If sources still disagree, do not pick. Follow the rule the standards name, write "Following X until Heather decides," and list the question in your report.

THE LOOK
- Every image is one process: RELIEF, COLLAGE, WOOD TYPE, HYBRID or NEWSPRINT. Never invent a new look.
- Print: black plus two inks per image, one pink focus per page, total ink under 240%.
- QUIET pages (deaths, atrocities, real suffering): NEWSPRINT only, no neon, no collage, no jokes.
- No generated lettering anywhere. Never generate the logo.
- Never name an artist, studio, shop or magazine in a prompt.
- Never recolor period art. An illustration never imitates a period document or sits inside one.
- Generated likenesses of historical figures are allowed. Living people: sourced public facts only, never shown doing something we can't prove.

THE FACTS
- The feature's Scorecard binds the art. Never show anything on its Must NOT claim list, or any crime, deal or motive it can't prove.
- Every image needs a rights log row, an image ID, a cleared rights status and a credit line (document 06).

NUMBERS
- Trim 8.5 x 11 in. Bleed 0.125 in on top, bottom and outside only. Safety 0.25 in. Nothing essential within 0.5 in of the gutter.
- Body 9.5/13, QUIET body 10/15, captions 7.5/10. Never below 7 pt.
- Images 300 ppi at placed size, never below 200.
- Use the exact grid, type and print specs in documents 04 and 07. Don't round them.

FILES
- Use the folders, file names and image IDs in document 09. Never rename, move, share or delete a file you didn't make.
- If you can't run layout software, deliver a page spec (document 09, section 4, route 3), not a description.
- Midjourney is run by hand by a person. You write the prompts; you don't run the generator by automation.

NEVER
- Mark anything Approved or Published. Only Heather approves. The art director picks images.
- Publish, upload, post, send, buy, sign or spend.
- Share unpublished pages, art or text outside the SIDE EYE team.
- Put a host's last name, a phone number, an email, a home location or an account name in a file, a file name or a prompt.

HOW TO REPORT
End each work session with: Done, Next, Blocked, Questions. Write each question so it can be answered in one word, with the options and what you did in the meantime. Deliver every file with a handoff note (document 09, section 5).

When unsure, say so and ask.
```

## 4. Typical engagements

Find your job below. It tells you what you get, what you deliver and which docs to read first.

| Engagement | You receive | You deliver | Stages you work in (see 01) | Read first |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| **Cover only** | Issue brief, cover lines, the cover concept if one exists, page count for the spine | Cover art-direction doc, COLLAGE prompts (A and B), the layered cover file, the cover PDF built on KDP's cover template | 2, 3, 4, 6 | 02, 05, 06, 07 (cover section), 08, 09 |
| **Illustrations only** | Art-direction doc or page briefs, Scorecards, the flatplan | Prompts (A and B per image), the picks, edited finals in 04 Art/09 Finals, an art log row and a rights log row per image | 2, 3 | 05, 06, 03 (your page recipes), 08, 09 |
| **Archival imagery and rights** | Flatplan, Scorecards, image wish list | Archival originals, working copies, rights evidence, rights log rows, credit lines | 3 | 06, 09, 08 |
| **Full layout** | Everything above, plus approved manuscript and final art | A layout package, an HTML build or page specs; review PDFs; a handoff note | 4, 5, 6 | 01, 04, 03, 02, 07, 08, 09 |
| **Prepress only** | Layout package and review sign-off | Preflighted interior and cover PDFs, KDP previewer notes | 6 | 07, 08, 09 |

In every engagement:

- **You never upload to KDP.** Heather uploads.
- **You never order a proof copy, buy a font, buy an image license or pay for any tool.** Spending money needs Heather's yes.
- **You work only inside the issue folders you were given,** in the stage folders for your job.
- **Style references** are SIDE EYE's own approved images. They are private. Ask the art director for them. Never upload anyone else's art as a style reference.

## 5. Who decides what

| Decision | You decide alone | Art director decides | Heather decides |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| Wording of a prompt inside a 05 template | Yes | | |
| Applying a fix from 05 when a prompt misbehaves | Yes | | |
| Which candidates to shortlist | Yes | | |
| File names, folders, versions (per 09) | Yes | | |
| Running checklists from 08 and reporting results | Yes | | |
| Taking the safer option while a question is open | Yes, and say so | | |
| Picking the image for a slot (status Picked) | | Yes | |
| Revisions inside an approved concept | | Yes | |
| Ink variant for a feature (per 05) | | Yes | |
| Page recipe for a page, inside the approved flatplan | | Yes | |
| Swapping a generated image for an archival one, or the reverse | | Yes | |
| Calling layout ready for review (end of Stage 4) | | Yes | |
| Passing every gate in 01, except where 01 names the art director | | | Yes |
| The cover, every opener and every image of a real person | | | Yes |
| Anything near a Scorecard's Must NOT claim list | | | Yes |
| A new logo use, color, font, format or page recipe | | | Yes |
| Spending money, signing, licensing | | | Yes |
| Uploading, publishing, ordering a proof | | | Yes |
| Sharing unpublished work outside the team | | | Yes |
| Changing a standard in this set | | | Yes |
| Any conflict listed in 13 Open Questions for Heather | | | Yes |

If the issue brief names no art director, Heather fills that role.

Never mark anything Approved. Leave the Approved by and Approved on cells blank. Heather, or the person she names, fills them.

## 6. How to report

### Where

- **Status of each image:** the Status cell in the art log.
- **Rights status of each image:** the Status cell in the rights log.
- **Each delivery:** a handoff note saved next to the files (09, section 5).
- **Issue-level questions:** the open items list in the Issue Hub doc.
- **Standards-level questions** (a rule that affects every issue): tell the art director. They go to 13 Open Questions for Heather.

### When

- At the end of every work session.
- At every gate, before it is asked for.
- **Right away** when you are blocked, when a check fails, or when an image might show something on a Must NOT claim list.

### Status words

Use only these words, so everyone reads them the same way.

| Where | Allowed values |
| :- | :- |
| Art log, Status | To run · Running · Picked · Needs rerun · Placed · Skip |
| Rights log, Status | Pending · Cleared · Blocked |
| Your status note, each item | Done · In progress · Blocked · Waiting on answer |

The art log made on November 24, 2026 starts new rows as "Not started." Treat that as the same as To run.

### The status note

Four headings, short lines, no filler:

```
Done: what you finished, with file names
Next: what you'll do next, in order
Blocked: what stops you, and who can unblock it
Questions: each one answerable in one word, with the options,
           what each costs, and what you did meanwhile
```

## 7. What to expect at each gate

01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files is the authority on gates. This table is a summary so you know what is coming.

| Stage | What gets checked | Who passes it | What you hand in |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| 0 Issue Brief | Theme, page count, schedule, who does what | Heather | Nothing, unless asked to draft |
| 1 Flatplan | Every page's piece, recipe, register and tab | Heather | Flatplan draft, if you are the art director |
| 2 Art Direction | Concepts, processes, inks, prompts, people rules, Scorecard limits | Heather for the cover and concepts; art director for revisions inside them | The art-direction doc (05, section 14) |
| 3 Art Production | Each image against the pick checklist in 05 and the rights checks in 06 | Art director picks each image; Heather approves the cover, openers and any real person (05, section 12.4) | Finals, art log rows, rights log rows, rights evidence |
| 4 Layout | Pages built to 03, 04 and 07; links and fonts; checks in 08 | Art director | Review PDF and your package, build or page specs |
| 5 Review and Legal Read | Every page against the Scorecards, the legal read, credits | Heather | Fixes and a new review PDF |
| 6 Prepress | KDP specs and previewer | Production for preflight; Heather for upload | Print-ready PDFs |
| 7 Proof Copy | The printed proof, when the brief calls for one | Heather | Proof notes, if asked |
| 8 Release and Archive | The live book and the closing package | Heather | The 08 Package folder (09) |

Expect notes at every gate. A gate can send work back. That is normal. Fix it, raise the version number, and hand it back with a new handoff note.

## 8. Your first assignment (a test)

Every new vendor or agent does this test before real issue work. It takes one to two hours. It uses a made-up test issue, so nothing you make goes into a real issue.

### The brief

- **Issue code:** SE-V00 (test only)
- **Piece:** a sidebar on the myth that people in the Middle Ages thought the Earth was flat.
- **Job:** one RELIEF spot illustration, square (1:1), printed about 3.4 in wide on a LOUD page.
- **Ink variant:** pink + pale cyan (the house default).
- **Pink:** on one object only, the punchline.
- **Type space:** leave plain paper across the top third.

### The practice Scorecard

This is a practice Scorecard written for this test. Treat it as binding for the test.

| Claim | Proof level |
| :- | :- |
| Educated people in medieval Europe knew the Earth was round. Standard textbooks of the time taught a round Earth. | PROVEN |
| Medieval kings held a royal orb, a round ball with a cross on top, as a symbol of the world. | PROVEN |
| The idea that "medieval people thought the world was flat" became popular in the 1800s, long after the Middle Ages. | LIKELY |

**Must NOT claim:**

- Don't show any real person, living or historical.
- Don't show anyone saying or teaching that the Earth is flat.
- Don't suggest the Church taught a flat Earth.
- Don't imitate a medieval manuscript, map or document.

### What to do

1. Read 05 sections 3.1 (RELIEF), 5 (the joke rule), 8 and 9.1 (the RELIEF template).
2. Write a one-line concept: the visual joke and what pink marks.
3. Write **Prompt A** (safer joke) and **Prompt B** (bolder joke) from the 05 RELIEF template. Change only the words in brackets. Keep the style reference as the placeholder `[RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL]`.
4. Check both prompts against the Must NOT list and the golden rules in 00.
5. Name the file the image will have once it is run and picked (09).
6. Fill one art log row and one rights log row (below).
7. Write a short handoff note (09, section 5).
8. Save everything in the test folder the art director gives you. If you have no folder, send the files with your status note.

You don't run Midjourney for this test. A person runs it by hand later, if the art director wants to see results.

### A sample answer

Use this to check your format, not to copy. Your joke can be different.

**Concept:** The royal orb, the round world in a king's hand, sits on a plate next to a flat pancake. Pink marks the orb.

**Prompt A (safer):**

```
flat three-ink relief print of a jeweled royal orb topped with a small cross resting on a plain plate beside a flat round pancake, the royal orb printed in fluorescent pink, bold simple shapes, thick uneven carved black outline, fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink, coarse halftone dot shading, speckled ink texture, slight misregistration, printed on bare warm cream paper, wide empty paper margins, plain empty paper across the top third, evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper --ar 1:1 --s 150 --c 0 --sref [RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 60 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, skull, glow, gradient --raw --v 8.2
```

**Prompt B (bolder):**

```
flat three-ink relief print of a jeweled royal orb topped with a small cross sitting in a cast-iron frying pan, a gloved hand pressing down on it with a bent spatula, the orb still perfectly round, only hands and sleeves visible, the royal orb printed in fluorescent pink, bold simple shapes, thick uneven carved black outline, fluorescent pink and pale cyan ink, coarse halftone dot shading, speckled ink texture, slight misregistration, printed on bare warm cream paper, wide empty paper margins, plain empty paper across the top third, evenly lit, flat ink printed on paper --ar 1:1 --s 150 --c 10 --sref [RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL] --sw 60 --no text, letters, words, numbers, logo, watermark, skull, glow, gradient, faces --raw --v 8.2
```

Before you hand in, copy the ending from the current 05 template, not from this sample. 05 wins if they differ.

**File name once picked (09):** `SE-V00_p000_flat-earth-orb_RELIEF_A1_v01.png` (page p000 because the test has no page; A1 means Prompt A, grid image 1).

**Image ID (09 and 06):** `SE-V00-I01`

**Art log row:**

| Field | Entry |
| :- | :- |
| # | 1 |
| Image ID | SE-V00-I01 |
| Section | Test |
| Piece | Flat Earth myth sidebar |
| Page(s) | not placed |
| Art status | To run |
| Brief (what the art must show) | A round royal orb beside a flat pancake. Pink on the orb. No people, no lettering, no manuscript look. |
| Process / Midjourney prompt | RELIEF, pink + cyan. Prompt A and Prompt B as written in the handoff note. |
| Generations kept | none yet |
| Chosen file (in Selects) | none yet |
| File name | SE-V00_p000_flat-earth-orb_RELIEF_A1_v01.png (planned) |
| Source and license | Generated, Midjourney v8.2 |
| Credit line | Illustration generated with Midjourney and edited by SIDE EYE. |
| Pixel size | not yet run (needs at least 1,020 px wide for 3.4 in at 300 ppi) |
| 300 dpi at print size? | not yet run |
| Approved by | (blank) |
| Approved on | (blank) |
| Notes | Must NOT list checked: no person, no one teaching flat Earth, no Church, no period document. |

**Rights log row (06, section 5):** Image ID SE-V00-I01; Type I; Source "Midjourney v8.2"; Creator "SIDE EYE"; Status Pending (until it is generated, picked and checked); credit line as above.

### How the test is graded

The art director passes the test when every line is true.

- [ ] Both prompts use the 05 RELIEF template, with only the bracketed words changed.
- [ ] No artist, studio, shop or magazine name. No lettering asked for.
- [ ] Pink is on one object, and that object is the punchline.
- [ ] Nothing in either prompt breaks the Must NOT list.
- [ ] No period document, map or manuscript look.
- [ ] The file name, image ID and both log rows follow 09 and 06.
- [ ] Approved cells are blank.
- [ ] The handoff note answers the questions in 09, section 5.
- [ ] No em-dashes and no banned words or phrases (hub, section Voice and writing) in any text you wrote.

## 9. Privacy and confidentiality

- **Unreleased issues are confidential.** Don't share pages, art, text, themes or dates outside the SIDE EYE team until the issue is on sale.
- **Never post work in progress** anywhere public: no portfolio, no social post, no public gallery.
- **Generator galleries:** some image tools post your images to a public gallery by default. Before you generate art for an unreleased issue, ask the art director how the SIDE EYE account is set up (open question, see 13).
- **No personal data.** Never put a host's last name, a phone number, an email, a home location or an account user name on a page, in a file, in a file name, in a prompt or in a handoff note.
- **Rename downloads.** Image tools often put the account's user name in the file name. Rename every file per 09 before it goes into a shared folder.
- **Leave Drive as you found it.** Don't move, rename, share or delete files you didn't make. Don't change sharing settings. Don't make copies outside the issue folders.
- **The standards portal is unlisted.** Its address is given to you directly. Don't post it or link to it.
- **Private people in found material:** no readable names, addresses or personal details of private people (see 06).
- **When a job ends,** hand back everything in the issue folders and delete your local copies, unless the art director asks you to keep them.

## 10. Glossary

| Term | Meaning |
| :- | :- |
| **A and B prompts** | Two prompts per image. A is the safer joke, B the bolder one. See 05. |
| **AI Dead** | The column "Letters to the AI Dead." Its portraits are always a skull wearing the figure's defining prop, never a face. |
| **Archival image** | A public-domain or free-license period image. The only image type that can serve as proof. Type code H. |
| **Art log** | The issue's sheet with one row per image slot: brief, prompt, file, status, rights, credit. Lives in 04 Art. |
| **Black plus two** | Every print image uses black plus at most two inks. |
| **Bleed** | Art that runs past the trim edge by 0.125 in, on top, bottom and outside only. Never into the gutter. |
| **Box Score** | A WOOD TYPE fight-card plate on kraft paper, names stacked, with "VS." the biggest word. |
| **Cast cameo** | A small RELIEF portrait of a real person told through a prop, inside a plain round frame. |
| **Coda mark** | The small mark that ends a piece: the eye mark for most columns, a pixel icon for an AI Dead letter, a tiny tombstone for Still Wrong. |
| **COLLAGE** | Layered paper collage. Used for covers, section openers, the tabloid poster and one full-bleed opener per issue. |
| **Corner bar** | A solid black bar at the page corner carrying a column's name in reversed type. Its exact place is in 04. |
| **CURRENT / SUPERSEDED** | Words in Drive titles. CURRENT is the live version. SUPERSEDED is history; don't work from it. |
| **Evidence rail** | A narrow strip on the outer edge of a page that carries the filing tab, the running section title in mono type, and the folio as a file number. Sizes are in 04. |
| **Filing tab** | A colored tab (red, blue or gray) on the page edge. Each color has a meaning set per issue in the issue brief. |
| **Flatplan** | The page-by-page plan of the issue: what goes on each page, with its recipe, register and tab. |
| **Folio** | The page number line. |
| **Gate** | The approval point at the end of each stage in 01. Work can't move on until it passes. |
| **Gutter** | The inside edge where pages meet the spine. Keep anything essential 0.5 in away from it. |
| **Handoff note** | The note that goes with every delivery. Its contents are in 09, section 5. |
| **HYBRID** | A real period figure, cut out and placed into a RELIEF scene. Used for openers about a historical person. |
| **Image ID** | One ID per image, like SE-V02-H03, used in the art log, rights log and credit list. See 09 and 06. |
| **Ink variant** | The second ink beside pink: pale cyan (default), acid yellow (money), neon orange (fire, war, emergencies), or cyan + orange with no pink (a LOUD page next to a QUIET one). |
| **Issue code** | SE-V plus the two-digit volume, like SE-V02. |
| **KDP** | Amazon Kindle Direct Publishing, the print-on-demand service that prints and sells the magazine. |
| **LOUD** | The default register: neon inks, jokes, collage and wood type allowed. |
| **Money Pages** | The front and back matter: the two front spreads, the classifieds, the last spread and the back cover. Each reads in under 15 seconds. |
| **Must NOT claim list** | The part of a Scorecard that lists what the piece, and its art, may never say or show. |
| **NEWSPRINT** | Soft black ink on gray newsprint, coarse halftone, lots of empty space. Used for QUIET pages and Out Back. |
| **Out Back** | The back section of the issue, printed in the NEWSPRINT look. |
| **Page recipe** | The fixed layout pattern for a recurring page type. See 03. |
| **Page spec** | An exact, page-by-page build sheet with coordinates, from someone who can't run layout software. See 09. |
| **Pause test** | For AI video: if any paused frame could pass for real film of a real person, restyle it or cut it. |
| **Pink focus** | The one thing on a print page that carries pink. One per page. |
| **Proof levels** | PROVEN, LIKELY or NOT PROVEN, given to every claim in the research. They never print on the page. |
| **QUIET** | The register for serious subjects: deaths, atrocities, real suffering. NEWSPRINT only, no neon, no collage, no jokes. |
| **RELIEF** | The house illustration: a carved-block look, thick black outline, black plus two inks, halftone dots, cream paper, one visual joke. |
| **Rights log** | The issue's sheet with one row per image, recording source, license basis, credit line and rights status. See 06. |
| **Safety** | Keep text and key art 0.25 in inside the trim. |
| **Scorecard** | The research summary for each feature: claims with proof levels, the Must NOT claim list, corrections, the other side, and checks before print. It binds the art. |
| **Selects** | The candidates picked from the generations, kept untouched. |
| **Split fountain** | Two or three inks blended across one run of a print plate. Used with WOOD TYPE only. |
| **Style reference** | SIDE EYE's own approved image that guides Midjourney's look. Private. Ask the art director. |
| **Trim** | The final page size: 8.5 × 11 in. |
| **WOOD TYPE** | Big letterpress poster type, plates and woodcut cuts. Used for the Box Score, posters and contents. Letters are set in real type, never generated. |

Terms for the brand itself (the logo, colors, hosts, voice) are in the Brand Standards hub.

---

- Design System v3: Neon Zine Print
- Design System v2 Core
- Design System v2 Addendum A: KDP Production Spec, 8.5 x 11
