# 11 Templates
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Lena Marsh, Documentation Specialist, Process and Governance
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review

**Who this is for:** Art directors, designers, image makers, rights checkers, production staff, vendors and AI agents working on a SIDE EYE issue.
**Use it when:** A stage in 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files asks you to make one of these files. Copy the template, fill every field, and delete the guidance lines in *italics*.
**Related:** 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files; 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards); 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits; 08 Quality Checklists; 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs; Napoleon Art Direction (model art-direction deliverable); side-eye-art-direction skill.

---

## How to use these templates

- Copy the block into a new Google Doc or Sheet in the issue folder that doc 09 names. Name the file the way doc 09 says (stage-tree working docs read `<NN> <Doc name> – SE-V<vol> <Issue title>`). Versions are two digits: v01, v02.
- Fill every field. If a field doesn't apply, write "n/a" and why. Never leave it blank.
- Use only role names in templates (Heather, Art Director, Designer / Layout, Illustrator / Image Generator, Rights Checker, Fact-check / Research, Production). Never put a phone number, personal email, home location or a host's last name in any file or file name (Brand Standards hub, "Privacy").
- Status words, in this order: DRAFT, IN REVIEW, APPROVED, LOCKED, SUPERSEDED. Only Heather sets APPROVED or LOCKED. Agents set DRAFT and IN REVIEW only.
- Logs (art log, rights log, Change Log) are Google Sheets: one row per item, the columns below as the header row.

| Template | Made at stage | Who fills it |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Issue Brief | 0 Issue Brief | Art Director |
| 2. Flatplan | 1 Flatplan | Art Director, Designer / Layout |
| 3. Art-Direction Doc | 2 Art Direction | Art Director |
| 4. Image Request and Art Log row | 2 and 3 | Art Director, Illustrator / Image Generator |
| 5. Rights Log row | 2 and 3 | Rights Checker |
| 6. Change Request | Any stage after a gate | Anyone |
| 7. Handoff Note | End of every stage, and any time work changes hands | Whoever hands off |
| 8. Release Note | 8 Release and Archive | Production |

---

## 1. Issue Brief

```markdown
# SE-V## Issue Brief: <Issue title>
Version v01 · <date> · Prepared by: Art Director
Status: DRAFT

## The issue
- Volume: Vol. <#>
- Issue title: The <Theme> Issue
- Theme in one line: <one sentence>
- Issue thesis line (printed under the title, serif italic): "<thesis>"
- Three tabs (red · blue · gray): <Tab 1> · <Tab 2> · <Tab 3>
- Audience: Curious adults, about an 8th-grade reading level (Brand Standards hub, "Who it's for"). Note anything special for this issue: <n/a or note>

## Size and dates
- Page count target (even; plan 116 to 132): <#> pages
- First cuts if it runs long: <piece>, <piece>, <piece>
- Launch (on-sale) date: <date> (set by Heather on <date>)
- Manuscript LOCKED by: <date>
- Timeline: built from the template in 01, section 6. Gate dates live in the production tracker.

## Features and departments
| Piece | Section (Up Front / The Files / Out Back) | Tab | Register (LOUD/QUIET) | Words | Research tier | Text status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <title> | | | | | | DRAFT / LOCKED |

Issue rules check:
- [ ] At least one QUIET feature: <which>
- [ ] At least two stories centered on women: <which>
- [ ] At least two stories from outside Europe: <which>

## Must NOT claim list (binds text AND art)
*Copy every Must NOT line from each feature's Scorecard, plus issue-level flags. Keep the reason with each line.*
| # | Piece | Must NOT say, show or imply | Why (Scorecard reason) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | | | |

Living people in this issue: <names, or none>. Each follows the sourced-public-facts-only rule.
People never generated in this issue: <names and the reason, or none>. Copy this list into every art-direction doc.

## Cover
- Hero idea (one surreal idea): <one sentence>
- Hero side: <left / right> (alternates by volume; doc 02 says which)
- Cover lines (4 at most): <1> · <2> · <3> · <4>
- Masthead field color: <ink>
- Theme title down the edge: <wording>

## Things KDP can't print, and how we'll draw them instead
| Planned item | Printed replacement |
|---|---|
| <gatefold / pull-out / perforation / insert / spot ink> | <two-page spread / printed tear-line / drawn tape / CMYK build> |

## Budget
- Proof copies: <number, or "waived by Heather on <date>">. Ordered by: Heather.
- Paid image licenses: none (house rule).
- Paid fonts: <none; free stand-ins per doc 04 / licensed on <date>>
- Any other spend: <none, or item and amount for Heather's yes>

## Roles for this issue
| Role | Filled by (role or vendor name, no personal details) |
|---|---|
| Art Director | |
| Designer / Layout | |
| Illustrator / Image Generator | |
| Person who runs Midjourney | |
| Rights Checker | |
| Fact-check / Research | |
| Production | |

## Approval
Heather approves: thesis, page count, launch date, cover side, proof budget, roles.
Heather's decision: <APPROVED / changes> · Date: <date> · Recorded in the production tracker: <yes>
```

---

## 2. Flatplan

One row per page. Spreads share a spread number. Keep it as a Sheet.

```markdown
# SE-V## Flatplan v01: <Issue title>
Page count: <#> (even) · Status: DRAFT · Last change: <date>

| Spread | Pages | Section | Piece | Recipe | Register | Tab | Process | Owner | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Cover | Cover | Cover | Cover (COLLAGE) | LOUD | n/a | COLLAGE | Art Director | Not started |
| 1 | 2-3 | Up Front | <piece> | <recipe from doc 03> | LOUD | n/a | WOOD TYPE + RELIEF | Designer / Layout | Not started |
| | | | | | | | | | |
```

Field guide:
- **Spread:** 0 for the cover; then 1, 2, 3 in reading order. Page 1 sits alone (right-hand page).
- **Pages:** printed page numbers, for example 24-25.
- **Section:** Up Front, The Files, Out Back, Cover, Back Cover.
- **Recipe:** the page recipe name from doc 03 (for example Feature opener (historical person), Box Score, QUIET pages, Out Back).
- **Register:** LOUD or QUIET.
- **Tab:** one of the issue's three tabs, or n/a.
- **Process:** RELIEF, COLLAGE, WOOD TYPE, HYBRID or NEWSPRINT. One per image; list the lead process for the page.
- **Owner:** a role name.
- **Status:** Not started, In progress, Ready for review, Approved (Heather only), Blocked (say why in a note).

Change notes (one line per change after approval):

| Date | Change | Decided by | CR number (if any) |
|---|---|---|---|
| | | | |

---

## 3. Art-Direction Doc

Model: the Napoleon Art Direction doc. One per feature, plus one each for the cover, Up Front and Out Back.

~~~markdown
# SE-V## Art Direction: <Piece title>
Version v01 · <date> · Art Director: <role or vendor name>
Status: DRAFT
Pages: <#>, spreads <#-#> · Register: <LOUD / QUIET> · Tab: <tab>
Built from: <piece title> (LOCKED <date>) and its Scorecard (<date>)

## 1. The concept
- The one visual idea, tied to the piece's thesis in one sentence: <...>
- The recurring device (the stand-in, the prop, the visual rule that repeats): <...>
- The joke: <...>
- What pink marks (one pink focus per page): <...>
- Evidence stays separate: illustrations carry the argument; historical images carry the proof. No illustration imitates or sits inside a period document.
- Case header or filing line, if used: <FILED UNDER: TAB · DATES · STATUS>

## 2. Print style table
| Image | Process | Inks (black + two) | Paper | Field color | Type faces |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | RELIEF | black, pink, pale cyan | warm cream | none | n/a |

## 3. Flatplan for this piece
| Spread | Pages | Text | Page recipe | Illustration | Historical image | Headline treatment | Register | Tab |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | | Opener | | I1 | none | | LOUD | |

Rhythm notes: <where illustrations lead, where evidence leads>
Gutter notes: <anything that must stay 0.5 in clear of the gutter>

## 4. Rules for this piece
- People: <how real people appear: period art, generated likeness of a historical figure, hands and props only, or no art (sensitive spreads)>
- Living people: <none / sourced public facts only; what the art must never imply>
- Sensitive spreads: <spreads with no illustrations, no pink, no jokes; any reader needed>
- Props that must be right: <...>
- Color and print: <ink builds to proof; never recolor period art>
- Type: <headline face, any vertical word, caption style, pull lines (one per spread at most)>

## 5. Prompts
*Two per generated image: A the safer read, B the bolder joke. Copy-ready. No artist, studio, shop or magazine names. No generated lettering: blank paper is "completely blank". Settings per doc 05.*

### I1 · <name> (<spread>, <placement>)
<one line: the idea this image carries>

A.
```
<prompt A text> --ar <w:h> --style raw --v 8.2 <other settings from doc 05>
```

B.
```
<prompt B text> --ar <w:h> --style raw --v 8.2 <other settings from doc 05>
```

If a prompt misbehaves: <fix, from doc 05>

## 6. Historical images
| Code | Spread | Image (credit line) | Source page | Rights basis | Rights log status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | | <Artist, Title, date, holding institution> | <source name; link goes in the rights log> | <public domain basis / free license> | Pending |

## 7. Built in layout (never generated)
| Piece | Spread | Build |
|---|---|---|
| <wood-type plate / chart / map / timeline / cover lines / ornament> | | <how it's made; every number matches the fact-checked text> |

## 8. Credit lines
- Generated art: "Illustration generated with Midjourney and edited by SIDE EYE."
- Composites: "Figure from [source], cut out and altered by SIDE EYE; illustration generated with Midjourney and edited by SIDE EYE."
- Historical art: artist, title, date, holding institution, exactly as in the rights log.

## 9. Open items before print
- [ ] Rights: <rows not yet Cleared>
- [ ] Proof: <ink builds to check on the proof>
- [ ] Scorecard: <anything the art could imply that the Scorecard can't prove>
- [ ] Independent review (cover and living-person openers): <who, when>
- [ ] Decisions waiting on Heather: <...>

## Sources
<titles of the docs this art direction is built on>
~~~

---

## 4. Image Request and Art Log row

Every image slot in the issue gets one row in the issue's art log, a Google Sheet in 04 Art (doc 09 sets its name and place). The Art Director fills the request columns in Stage 2. The Illustrator / Image Generator fills the run columns in Stage 3. Doc 05, section 12 owns the run columns; if it changes, doc 05 wins.

### Request columns (Stage 2, Art Director)

| Column | What goes in it |
|---|---|
| Image ID | Same ID as the rights log: issue code, type code, two-digit number, for example SE-V04-I07 (doc 06 sets the pattern) |
| Slot ID and page | The art-direction doc code (I3, C2, H1) and the page or spread |
| Piece | Feature or department title |
| Placement | Full page, half page, spot, cover hero, background |
| Source type | Generated (I), archival (H), composite (X), our photo (P), found (F), scan (S), hand lettering (L) (doc 06, section 2.1) |
| Process and inks | For example RELIEF, pink + cyan |
| What it must show | One sentence |
| What pink marks | One thing |
| Must NOT | Lines from the Scorecard this image must not imply |
| Placed size | Width × height in inches |
| Minimum pixels | Placed size × 300 (full-bleed page: 2,588 × 3,375 px) |
| Aspect ratio | For example 4:5 |
| Needed by | Date |

### Run columns (Stage 3, Illustrator / Image Generator)

| Column | What goes in it |
|---|---|
| Prompt as run | The exact text pasted, with the reference token left as written in doc 05 (for example `[RELIEF STYLE REFERENCE URL]`), never the link itself |
| A or B | Which prompt won, and any fix applied |
| Generator and version | For example Midjourney v8.2 |
| Seed | If the tool shows one; otherwise "not shown" |
| Job ID | The generator's job ID for the picked image, and its grid position (1 to 4) |
| Date | The date generated |
| Run by | Role or vendor name |
| File name | The renamed file, per doc 09 |
| Pixel size and 300 dpi check | Pixels, placed size, pass or fail |
| Edits in layout | Cut-out, composite, color shift to ink builds, cleanup |
| Source and license | "Generated," or the period source and rights line per doc 06 |
| Credit line | Per doc 06 |
| Status | To run · Running · Picked · Needs rerun · Placed · Skip |
| Approved by and date | Filled only by Heather or the person she names |

Copy-ready header row:

```
Image ID | Slot ID and page | Piece | Placement | Source type | Process and inks | What it must show | What pink marks | Must NOT | Placed size | Minimum pixels | Aspect ratio | Needed by | Prompt as run | A or B | Generator and version | Seed | Job ID | Date | Run by | File name | Pixel size and 300 dpi check | Edits in layout | Source and license | Credit line | Status | Approved by and date
```

---

## 5. Rights Log row

One Sheet per issue, in 04 Art (doc 09 sets the name, for example `00 Rights Log – SE-V04 <Issue title>`). Every image that appears in the issue gets a row, generated art included. These fields follow doc 06, section 5; if doc 06 changes a field or a status word, doc 06 wins.

| Field | What goes in it |
|---|---|
| Image ID | Issue code, a dash, the type code, then a two-digit number in order of first use, for example SE-V04-H01 |
| File name | The exact file name of the placed file (doc 09) |
| Type | I, H, X, P, F, S or L |
| Source | The collection or program, or "SIDE EYE" or "Midjourney v8.2" |
| Creator | Artist, photographer or maker; "Unknown" if unknown; "SIDE EYE" for generated art |
| Title | The institution's title, or a plain description |
| Date | Date made or published, as the institution gives it |
| Holding institution | Who holds the original; n/a for generated art |
| URL | The item page at the institution, not an image file link |
| License or PD basis | The basis in words, plus what the item page says |
| Verified by | The person or agent who did the checks |
| Verified date | The date the rights statement was read and saved |
| Credit line | Exactly as it will print (doc 06, section 10) |
| Where used | Page or spread, and piece |
| Status | Pending, Cleared or Blocked |
| Changes made | Crop, grayscale, halftone, cut out, composite |
| Pixels and effective ppi | Pixel size and ppi at the placed size |
| Notes | Reason for Pending, the open question, the replacement; for generated art, the prompt's code from the art-direction doc |

Rules for this log:
- Only Cleared rows go to print. Pending can sit in a layout comp. Blocked is never placed; keep the row so nobody tries it again.
- Paid licenses are never Cleared (Brand Standards hub, "Image rights").
- The person who verifies reads the rights statement on the item page themselves and saves it as evidence, named by image ID.
- A row goes back to Pending if the crop, caption, page register or source file changes.

Copy-ready header row:

```
Image ID | File name | Type | Source | Creator | Title | Date | Holding institution | URL | License or PD basis | Verified by | Verified date | Credit line | Where used | Status | Changes made | Pixels and effective ppi | Notes
```

---

## 6. Change Request

Use for any change after a gate (01, section 7), and for changes to these standards.

```markdown
# CR-### · SE-V## (or STANDARDS) · <short title>
Raised by: <role> · Date: <date>
Type: Issue / Standards

## What should change
<one or two sentences>

## Why
<what went wrong, or what's better>

## What it touches
- Stage(s): <0-8>
- Pages / spreads: <...>
- Files: <names per doc 09>
- Page count change: <none / +# / -#>
- Locked text change: <none / yes, editorial must approve>
- New fact, number, quote or date: <none / yes, sent to Fact-check / Research>
- Cost: <none / amount>
- Days added: <#>
- For Standards: doc and section, current wording, proposed wording

## Decision
Decided by: <Art Director / Editorial with Heather / Heather>
Decision: <approved / approved with changes / declined>
Date: <date>
Recorded in: <issue Change Log / 12 Decision Log> and the production tracker
Checklists to re-run: <08 stage names>
```

---

## 7. Handoff Note

Put one at the top of every gate request, and with every delivery of any size. Save it in the folder you delivered to, named `SE-V<vol>_handoff_<YYYY-MM-DD>_<what>.md` (or a Google Doc titled the same way with spaces). The questions and their order come from doc 09, section 5; if doc 09 changes them, doc 09 wins. Nothing counts as handed off until it is in Drive.

```markdown
# Handoff · SE-V## · <what> · <YYYY-MM-DD>
From: <role> · To: <role> · Stage: <#> <stage name>

## 1. What this is
Issue, pieces or pages, route (packaged layout file / HTML build / page spec), version.

## 2. What's in it
- <exact file name> · <folder>

## 3. What changed since the last version
- <one line per change>

## 4. Checks run
08 Quality Checklists, Stage <#> <stage name>: <all pass / exceptions>
Failed checks and why: <...>

## 5. Rights and credits
| Image ID | Rights status (Cleared / Pending / Blocked) | Credit line |
|---|---|---|
| | | |

## 6. Art log and rights log
Rows updated, by image ID: <...>

## 7. Open items
| Item | Options | What each costs | What I did meanwhile |
|---|---|---|---|
| | | | |

## 8. Rule conflicts I hit
<which two sources disagreed, and which one I followed>

## 9. What I need back
<what, from whom, by when>
For a gate request: the decision Heather is asked to make, the options, and my recommendation.

## 10. What's next
<next step if I keep the job, or who picks it up>

Status updated in the production tracker: <yes>
```

Rules: never write "approved" unless the art log or the production tracker shows who approved it and when. No private data: no personal contact details, no addresses, no account names.

---

## 8. Release Note

Written by Production at Stage 8. Filed with the archive package.

```markdown

Date: <date> · Prepared by: Production

## What shipped
- Title as listed: <...>
- On-sale date: <date> · Published in KDP: <date> · Live: <date>
- Final page count: <#> · Spine width: <in>
- Final files: <interior file name> · <cover file name>
- Text-searchable copy archived: <file name>

## Standards used
- This set: version <#.#> (and any doc on a different version: <...>)
- Brand Standards hub: version <#.#>
- Design System v3 and Addendum A, as in force on <date>

## Approvals
| Gate | Approved by | Date |
|---|---|---|
| 0 Issue Brief | Heather | |
| 1 Flatplan | Heather | |
| 2 Art Direction (cover and concepts) | Heather | |
| 3 Cover art | Heather | |
| 5 Layout sign-off | Heather | |
| 6 Upload | Heather | |
| 7 Proof (or waiver) | Heather | |
| 8 Publish | Heather | |

## Proof
- Proofs ordered: <#>, or "released without a printed proof" (waived by Heather on <date>)
- What the proof changed: <...>

## Change Requests
- Total: <#> · Approved: <#> · Declined: <#>
- Ones that changed page count, cover or schedule: <CR numbers>

## Known issues at release
- <anything shipped with a known limit, and why>

## Lessons for the next issue
- <what went wrong, what it cost, the proposed fix>
- Standards Change Requests filed: <CR numbers>
- Questions added to 13 Open Questions for Heather: <...>

## Archive package checklist
- [ ] Layout source files
- [ ] Every placed image, final versions
- [ ] Font list with license status
```

# 12 Decision Log
Version 1.0 draft · November 24, 2026 · Owner: Lena Marsh, Documentation Specialist, Process and Governance
Status: DRAFT for Heather's review
**Who this is for:** Anyone designing, producing or checking a SIDE EYE issue, and anyone changing these standards.
**Use it when:** Two sources disagree, you need to know whether a rule is Heather's own call, or you are adding a decision after Heather says yes.
**Related:** 00 Start Here: How to Use the SIDE EYE Art Direction Standards; 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files; 02 Cover Standards; 03 Page and Spread Standards; 04 Grid and Typography; 05 Art and Illustration Direction (with Midjourney Prompt Standards); 06 Imagery Sourcing, Rights and Credits; 07 Print Production and Prepress (Amazon KDP); 08 Quality Checklists; 09 File Naming, Folders and Handoff Specs; 10 Vendor and Agent Onboarding Starter; 11 Templates; 13 Open Questions for Heather; SIDE EYE Brand Standards hub (Decision log)

---

## How to read this log

**The Brand Standards hub's Decision log wins on logo, color and type.** It also wins on voice, rights and every other brand rule. This log covers how an issue is designed and produced. If a line here ever disagrees with the hub, follow the hub and add the conflict to 13 Open Questions for Heather.

Rules for reading a row:

1. **Newest Heather decision wins.** When two rows disagree, the newer row that says "Yes" in the Heather column wins.
2. **"Yes" means Heather made the call herself,** in her own words or by approving the document that holds the rule. "Via hub" means the rule sits in the hub, which only Heather approves.
3. **"No" means the rule is a team standard, a spec rule or an outside fact** (for example, a KDP rule). It stands until Heather changes it, but she has not ruled on it herself.
4. **Interim rules are not decisions.** Where the set says "Follow X until Heather decides," the question is in 13 Open Questions for Heather, not here.
5. **Hub rows** copy the hub's own Decision log lines that this set relies on. Hub lines about video and scripts only (the standing intro, the host split, chapter titles, what hosts say on screen) are left out. Read them in the hub.

Docs are named by number in the "Applied in" column. The full titles are in the Related line above.

## How to add a decision

This follows 01 Issue Design Process: From Brief to Print-Ready Files, section 9.

1. **Get Heather's yes in writing.** A decision enters this log only after Heather approves it, or after it appears in the hub's Decision log. An agent's or vendor's suggestion is not a decision.
2. **Check the hub first.** If the decision is about logo, color, type, voice or rights, it belongs in the hub's Decision log. Ask the hub owner to add it there, then copy the line here only if this set relies on it.
3. **Add one row** in the right section. Use the next free ID (never reuse an ID, even for a row that is later replaced). Fill every column: date (YYYY-MM-DD), the decision as a rule someone can check, the source by title and date, whether it is Heather's own call, and which docs apply it.
4. **Mark what it replaces.** If it overrides an earlier row, write "Replaced by D-0NN on <date>" at the end of the old row's Decision cell. Leave the old row in place.
5. **Close the open question.** If it answers a question in 13 Open Questions for Heather, move that question to the "Answered" list there with the new decision ID.
6. **Fix the docs.** Each doc owner updates the docs in "Applied in," bumps their version (whole number if a rule changed, decimal for wording), and notes the decision ID in the change.
7. **Tell anyone mid-issue.** An issue past Stage 2 may finish on the old rule unless Heather says otherwise (01, section 9). Record which rule the issue used in its Release Note.

Only the process owner (the Documentation Specialist for Process and Governance) edits this log. Agents never write "Heather's own call: Yes" without her words or an approved document to point to.
